Suzune Year 1: Horikita Suzune's all Feat List Documentary (Y1) (semi-finished)
Status: In process

Warning: This document is primarily based on the original Classroom of the Elite light novel, but aspects of the manga and anime will be mentioned when the time comes.

Just a heads-up: this document is a summary of Horikita in the COTE series. Therefore, not only her greatest feats will be reviewed, but all of them good, bad, and even useless ones. The best feats will be in the final summary.

My native language isn't English, it is Spanish, so to convey my ideas I used AI translation because I'm not confident in my English writing. I apologize for that and hope you don't mind.

* * Suzune context
* * ————————————————————————————————————

(Y1) - It was a decisive year, long after people had become accustomed to the 21st century. While the world was grappling with all kinds of different problems, Japan itself was also standing at a turning point. With declining birth rates, an aging population, environmental issues, and a weakening national power, Japanese society was beginning to show signs of decay. In order to rebuild from the ground up, the government began to seriously focus its efforts on cultivating human resources, and as part of this shift in policy, a certain high school was established: Advanced Nurturing High School (ANHS).

Each school year is divided into four different classes, ranging from Class A to Class D. At the time of enrollment, each class is assigned 40 students, for a total of 160 students overall. That said, there are several other aspects of this school that make it dramatically different from ordinary high schools.

Students are provided with a form of currency known as “private points,” which they can use to make purchases throughout their time at the school. These points have an easy-to-understand one-to-one exchange rate with the Japanese yen and can be used just like real money. However, private points do not appear out of nowhere. Each month, students receive private points equal to their class’s current class points multiplied by 100. In other words, securing class points became the top priority in order to obtain the private points necessary for daily life.

There are several ways to earn class points, but the most standard method is by overcoming challenges given by the school known as “special exams.”

Essentially, during these special exams, the four classes compete against one another, with the top performers earning class points and the lowest-ranked classes losing class points. If a class were to finish with 1,000 class points, the students in that class would receive a monthly allowance equivalent to 100,000 yen. Conversely, if a class were to continuously lose these exams, their class points would eventually be reduced to zero, and as a result, they would receive a monthly allowance of zero private points.

The greatest selling point of the school was graduating as a member of Class A. Students who managed to achieve this were granted the luxury of advancing to any university or employment opportunity they desired. Even in the most extreme cases—whether it be a university with the lowest acceptance rates imaginable or a prestigious top-tier company—admission would be guaranteed with a free pass. However, this did not mean they could afford to be overly optimistic. After being accepted, if one’s raw potential was insufficient to meet the demands, it was only natural that they would eventually be discarded and eliminated.

Horikita Suzune is a student enrolled at this school. As of April 1st, she is a Class D student, now with a total of 275 class points. This means she receives approximately 27,500 yen in private points each month. For reference, the current highest-ranked class, Class A, is led by Sakayanagi and holds an overwhelming total of 1,119 class points. Following that is Class B, led by Ichinose, with 542 points. Close behind is Class C, led by Ryūen, with 540 points.

FSIQ Categories:
VCI (Verbal Comprehension Index) -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): The prolonged and structured negotiation with Nanase and later with Hōsen, where Suzune formulates, defends and adapts multiple complex proposals without support from private points.
Suzune articulates abstract frameworks such as:
   * equal cooperation
   * long-term trust
   * alliances without monetary compensation
She is capable of:
   * detecting contradictions in Hōsen's discourse
   * reformulating his proposal according to the interlocutor (Nanase ≠ Hōsen)
   * sustaining extensive discussions under emotional and physical pressure
She maintains rhetorical control even when:
   * she is insulted
   * she is deliberately provoked
   * the negotiation turns hostile

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): Interprets complex exam rules without confusion. Distinguishes between:
   * Real cooperation vs. apparent cooperation
   * Verbal alliance vs. strategic intent
Understands what is left unsaid:
   * Class A's silence
   * 2-B/2-C's lack of contact
Correctly decodes the role with:
   * “Noon”
   * “K.A.”
   * “Expelled”
   * “I2”
Suzune doesn't talk much, but when she does:
   * Her messages are precise
   * They are never redundant
She never reveals more information than necessary and uses it as a form of containment for Class 2-D
   * She avoids lengthy discussions within the class.
   * She cuts short debates that don't lead to implementation.
   * She uses closed phrases to prevent deviations.
And she is also capable of provoking, as with Ibuki. By manipulating their rivalry:
   * igniting her pride
   * forcing temporary cooperation
Because Suzune understands:
   * When someone is lying
   * When someone is omitting information
   * When someone is speaking with ulterior motives
For example, with Amasawa. It detects that:
   * she knows more about Kiyotaka
   * she is not the author of the paper
   * she deliberately tries to stop her
None of this is explicitly stated. Everything is inferred from tone, word choice, and timing.

   * UVE (Y2V5): Suzune's control over each of the motions in the Unanimous Voting Exam, for example, her persuasion to "Increase penalties." Initially, the vote was fragmented:
   * Increase difficulty: 6 votes
   * Increase penalties: 18 votes
   * Decrease rewards: 15 votes
There was no consensus. The classroom was divided by individual interests (strong vs. weak students). However, after the debate, the result was: 39 unanimous votes to increase penalties. All three options were perceived as negative. Suzune shifts the discussion from which is "less bad" to which maximizes strategic control of the class. This involves:
   * Transforming an emotional evaluation into a structural one.
   * Converting individual fear into collective calculation.
This type of reframing is a high-level verbal skill because it requires: A deep understanding of the listeners' mental models. Reframing the problem without altering the facts. There were two groups:
   * Academically strong students
   * Academically weak students
Achieving unanimity implies that her speech:
   * Did not visibly favor one group over the other.
   * Was framed in terms of class stability.
That requires:
   * Use of inclusive language
   * Elimination of implicit threats
   * Reduction of psychological friction
This is a strong indicator of advanced pragmatic competence. A lengthy or emotional speech is not described. That suggests:
   * Concise communication
   * Structured argumentation
   * Elimination of ambiguity
In unanimous voting contexts, verbal excess often generates resistance. Effectiveness here indicates: High semantic efficiency (high impact with minimal speech). Furthermore, increased penalties generate anxiety. Suzune manages to get the class to accept greater risk without rebellion. That implies:
   * Tone regulation
   * Control of the argumentative pace
   * Implicit management of collective fear

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): Suzune demonstrates a solid command of language as a strategic, not merely communicative, tool. She not only understands the rules, but also:
   * Identifies hidden implications (e.g., scoring limits, risks of inter-class cooperation)
   * Translates complex rules into clear operational models, not only for the exam itself but also for the strategies and tactics of those who will be discussing their exam ideas.
This indicates a deep semantic understanding and the ability to abstract verbally. It also demonstrates strategic communication within the group. Suzune adapts her language to the context:
   * With less capable colleagues → clear, structured, and direct language
   * In strategic discussions → technical, logical, and argumentative language
She not only communicates ideas, but also:
   * Convinces
   * Corrects errors
   * Restructures collective thinking
Her explanations follow consistent patterns:
   * Premise → development → conclusion
   * Identification of variables → evaluation → decision
An example is when analyzing rival strategies:
   * She defines the expected behavior
   * Evaluates consequences
   * Draws operational conclusions
This reflects highly organized verbal thinking (logical-formal type). She does not exhibit advanced rhetorical manipulation (she does not use emotional language or intense indirect persuasion). As always, her style is more analytical than charismatic.

UVE (Y2V5) > Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4) > Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10) > Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1)

VSI (Visual Spatial Index) -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): The logistical planning of the “kitchen operation” with Kiyotaka and Amasawa, coordinating physical positions, hidden devices, times and routes without raising suspicion. Suzune.
   * Design a complete spatial setup (earphone, backpack, phone, Kiyotaka's location, in a room she's unfamiliar with (8 tatamis = (90 cm x 180 cm)*8 = 129 600 cm² = 12.96 m²).
   * Anticipate Amasawa's lines of sight and behavior.
   * Foresee failures and create alternative cues.

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): Quickly internalizes:
   * the 100-area map
   * adjacency logic (lateral + diagonal)
   * the difference between safe routes vs. contested routes
   * Does not depend on GPS to move:
   * moves to I2 by memory
   * maintains orientation even under pressure
   * Avoids highly congested areas. Suzune prioritizes:
   * predictable routes
   * low-risk movements
   * Positions herself as:
   * “mobile support node”
   * not as a spearhead
   * Knows that, depending on the space, the pressure can be equal. Especially with people like the first-years and Nagumo acting:
   * an isolated group is vulnerable
   * proximity between enemies increases friction
   * Therefore:
   * avoids being the center of conflicts, preventing any 2-D class member from being hurt in battle
   * operates on the “edges” of the conflict, using the fewest possible units in battle. Moving them under Sakayanagi's precise guidance, coordinating 2-D groups
The confrontation with Amasawa reveals a surprisingly high VSI at the micro-dynamic level in real time. Suzune stops looking at the face and observes feet and shoulders.
   * She reads:
   * movements
   * centers of gravity
   * timing of entries
   * She also coordinates attacks with Ibuki:
   * controls angles
   * maintains distances
   * avoids alignment (not ending up in the same line of attack)

   * Handwriting Identification (Y2V4.5-Y2V8):  Suzune's analysis of handwriting is not limited to reading text, but rather treats the strokes of kanji characters as geometric compositions made up of proportions, pressure vectors, and spatial balances.
   * Suzune breaks down character strokes into geometric variables:
   * the proportional relationship between radicals
   * The management of white space (yohaku)
   * The structural balance of the stroke. She identifies that an experienced writer maintains these proportions instinctively.
   * She evaluates variables such as fluidity, stroke speed, micro-tremors (microscopic tremors), pressure at starting points (tome), turns, and finishes (hane or harai). She understands how these pressure vectors act as unique spatial fingerprints that are difficult to counterfeit.
   * Upon examining the student council notebooks written by Yagami Takuya, Suzune instantly compares the visual memory with the note previously found on the island. She identifies the symmetry and the exact calligraphic style despite the passage of time.
   * While reviewing the reservation sheet for the private pool area and the treasure hunt log, she scans the handwriting patterns of multiple students in real time. She quickly dismisses those with dissimilar strokes (like Kibayashi-kun or Mochidzuki-san) and detects the calligraphic similarity in Ishigami Kyō.
Suzune was able to retain the structure, the balance of negative space (yohaku), and the strokes of a short note in her mind, and then compare that "visual map" against hundreds of real-time signature lists without losing accuracy for months until the cultural festival arrived and she saw Yagami's handwriting.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): Suzune demonstrates a high VSI applied to abstract systems, excelling in:
   * Mental modeling
   * Scenario simulation
   * Managing multiple simultaneous relationships
Suzune constructs an “abstract map” of the exam:
   * Classes → nodes
   * Points → dynamic flow
   * Errors → limited resources
   * Turns → time cycles
It can:
   * Visualize simultaneous interactions
   * Understand how an action affects multiple variables
This is conceptual visualization. It also handles multiple simultaneous variables. During the exam, it monitors:
   * Points of each class
   * State of peers
   * Potential targets
   * Active protection
   * It integrates everything into rapid decisions
This requires spatial processing of dynamic relationships (strategic board type). Suzune:
   * "Visualizes" possible strategic routes for other classes
   * Predicts how they will move within the rule system
Example:
   * Understands how Ichinose will move defensively
   * Anticipates pressure from Ryuuen
   * Considers interference from Sakayanagi
This involves spatial simulation of future scenarios, where it makes an optimal allocation of resources. Although not physical, there is a spatial logic to:
   * Protection allocation
   * Target selection
   * Risk distribution among members

CPI (Cognitive Proficiency Index) -

   * Class 2-D leadership
   * Interclass negotiations (Class 1-B)
   * Academic tutoring
   * Personal study
   * Strategic planning
   * Crisis management (Hōsen, Amasawa, Nanase)
Suzune remembers data, offers, numbers, and relationship statuses. It responds quickly to daily changes in OAA and coordinates multiple tasks without significant performance loss.

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): Throughout the entire fight with Amasawa, someone with subsonic speed detects under physical stress:
   * changes in Amasawa's rhythm
   * progressive decrease in speed
   * adjusts timing in microseconds
While fighting Amasawa:
   * recalls ancient teachings
   * simulates mental scenarios
   * coordinates Ibuki
   * maintains awareness of:
   * expulsion
   * medical alert
   * exam status
In the midst of the fight, based on:
   * Amasawa's behavior
   * physical condition
   * emotional reaction
Suzune deduces:
   * that he wasn't fighting at full capacity
   * that his stamina was his weak point

   * Handwriting Identification (Y2V4-Y2V8): Handling of:
   * Participants (66% of the first year)
   * Identities
   * Individual handwritings
   * System conditions (event rules)
Retention of:
   * Specific features of previous handwriting (fine comparative memory)
Parallel evaluation:
   * Participants vs. non-participants (reduction from 100% → 34%)
Suzune not only retains information, but also:
   * Actively manipulates it
   * Reorganizes it in real time (progressive filtering)
   * Uses it for subsequent inferences
This indicates a high cognitive load capacity. Evidence:
   * Massive handwriting review within a limited timeframe. Simultaneous evaluation of multiple variables:
   * Form
   * Pressure
   * Fluency
   * Kanji structure
Rapid system implementation (registration + verification + control)
Suzune not only processes quickly, but also:
   * Maintains accuracy under high data volumes
   * Does not sacrifice analytical quality for speed
Its strongest point is how it transforms a social event (treasure hunt) into:
   * A data collection system
   * A population filter
   * A verification platform
to efficiently process information and facilitate complex tasks. Suzune:
   * Reduces future cognitive load
   * Optimizes overall effort
   * Automates part of the process (participation → writing → analysis)

   * Suzune's Flow State (Y2V5): Suzune's cognitive "overclocking" during the decision regarding Kushida. During that instant, she actively maintains:
   * Multiple figures (-350 p, -100 p, future rewards)
   * Exam conditions
   * Expulsion rules
   * Emotional state of the classroom
   * Ayanokouji's strategic position
   * Kushida's future value
   * Who should be expelled
She doesn't just remember data; she manipulates it simultaneously. Estimated real time:
   * ≈ 0.5–0.8 seconds
Subjective time:
   * ≈ minutes of deliberation
She processes:
   * Alternative scenarios
   * Probabilistic consequences
   * Reputational risk
   * Offensive/defensive balance
Time compression is the strongest indicator here. There is no analysis paralysis. It signifies cognitive performance under maximum pressure. The decision was not linear. It wasn't: "If A then B." It was multivariable:
   * Tangible assets (points)
   * Human assets (potential vs. toxicity)
   * Long-term strategic goal
   * Building self-leadership
That requires complex structural reasoning, not simple calculation.

Personal ranking for the best CPI feats:
Suzune's Flow State (Y2V5) > Handwriting Identification (Y2V4-Y2V8) > Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4) > Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1)

FRI (Fluid Reasoning Index) -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): To secure third place without anyone being expelled and without spending private points by inferring that Hōsen's true objective was not the points, but Class 2-D as an entity, based solely on his behavior, language, and contradictory decisions.

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): Suzune is capable of detecting implicit conceptual relationships within the UIE system, abstracting the true logic of the exam beyond its formal rules, and making coherent decisions under incomplete information, in clear and unprecedented scenarios, and with many rivals around. Its thinking adapts to new problems without relying on prior experience, infers hidden causes from observed inconsistencies (such as Amasawa's actual state) under stress, and generates viable solutions when conventional options fail.

   * Handwriting Identification (Y2V4.5-Y2V8): Suzune's approach stands out for transforming a subjective variable ("pretty handwriting") into a system of objective, technical criteria, such as stroke pressure and spatial balance. She constructs a conceptual model that links handwriting to mental state, allowing her to identify subconscious patterns even when attempts are made to disguise the writing. Finally, Suzune solves an unprecedented problem by creating a method from scratch—a massive sample collection event—and using metacognition to anticipate and neutralize potential evasive tactics.

   * UVE (Y2V5): Develop a strategy to pass each motion as quickly as possible. The exam presented an irresolvable dilemma under conventional logic due to the atypical and fixed voting system (Agree > Time Out > Disagree). Suzune broke with the initial conceptual framework and radically changed the exam premise to adapt a non-linear solution.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Suzune takes on the task of reorganizing the class after their failure at UVE, regaining the class's trust, reintegrating Kushida, fixing internal rifts, and winning the Sports Festival by allying with the class with the highest physical average to undermine Class A.

   * Suzune Cultural Festival Strategy (Y2V5-Y2V7): Conceptual reconfiguration of the festival as a system of limited attention and competitive polarization

   * Written Comprehensive Cooperative Exam (Y2V9): Maximize the potential of your academically weakest students to achieve the highest possible score.

   * Kiryuuin Trial (Y2V9): To make the culprit and mastermind behind the case reveal himself despite having no evidence, witnesses, and being in a position of advantage.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): Instead of focusing solely on the surface of the game, she identifies hidden variables such as error management and her opponents' behavioral patterns, using figures like Ryuuen and Ichinose as pieces within his scheme. Her ability shines particularly brightly in detecting second-order patterns and creating a methodology from scratch, demonstrating that his strength lies in systemic analysis rather than mere memorization.

RCI (Reading Comprehension Index)/ NVI (Non Verbal Index)/ QRI (Quantitative Reasoning Index)/ GAI (General Ability Index)/ AWMI (Arithmetic Working Memory Index)  -

   * Suzune is able to understand and analyze an exam and answer it with ease.
   * Suzune reads the rule manual for each exam quickly and finds loopholes to exploit and instructions that are not detailed.

FSEQ Categories:
EM (Emotional Management) -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): Maintain emotional composure throughout the negotiation and subsequent violent confrontation with Hōsen, even after insults, humiliations, physical threats and direct aggression.
   * She doesn't lose her verbal composure despite constant provocations.
   * She repeatedly stops Sudō when he's about to explode.
   * She continues negotiating even after being physically attacked.
She keeps thinking even when:
   * negotiations fail
   * violence escalates
   * his own body is injured

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): The entire UIE Suzune supports:
   * Two weeks alone in a survival exam.
   * Accumulating fatigue, constant pressure to avoid expulsion.
   * Lack of direct support and fewer resources compared to other classes. Commanding the 2-D ground troops against the first-years.
All of this culminated in her confrontation with Amasawa, where she faced extreme factors:
   * A real physical threat
   * Direct psychological provocation
   * Attacks on her personal worth and relationship with Kiyotaka
Her emotional response was:
   * Not to panic despite the power difference.
   * She acknowledged her inferiority without denial or defensive pride.
   * She transformed anxiety into situational analysis.
After the physical confrontation, she sustained minor injuries, was exhausted, and in shock, yet:
   * She reorganized her priorities
   * She continued toward I2
   * She maintained strategic clarity
Physically and emotionally worn down, Suzune did feel:
   * Insecurity
   * Frustration
   * A sense of inadequacy
But:
   * She didn't freeze up
   * She didn't project her feelings
   * She didn't blame others

   * UVE (Y2V5): Suzune's emotional manage during the vote on Kushida. Context:
   * Risk of losing 350 class points
   * High social tension
   * Divided class
   * Decision affecting her legitimacy as leader
   * Kushida is a personal and structural threat
This is a scenario that typically produces:
   * Impulsivity
   * Resentment
   * Emotional bias
   * Conflict avoidance
But Suzune doesn't collapse. First, she suppresses her personal bias. Kushida is not neutral for Suzune. There is:
   * A history of conflict
   * Internal toxicity
   * Reputational risk
However, Suzune doesn't make the decision based on:
   * Rancor
   * Pride
   * Desire for punishment
That is affective bias control. Then she regulates her implicit physiology. In the moment of "time dilation":
   * She doesn't panic.
   * She doesn't freeze.
   * She doesn't impulsively rush the decision.
Her state shifts from collective stress to controlled maximum concentration. This indicates:
   * Control of the sympathetic nervous system.
   * Reorientation towards cognitive analysis.
Maintaining her stable identity. Key question she asks herself: “What do I want to do, Horikita Suzune?” Instead of reacting to the environment, she reaffirms her internal axis. This is an advanced form of emotional regulation:
   * She doesn't define her actions based on external pressure.
   * She defines her actions based on personal consistency.
Ultimately, she experiences stability after making the decision. Most importantly, after deciding, she doesn't show:
   * Immediate regret
   * Public doubt
   * Visible insecurity
After Kushida's chaotic revelation of secrets turned the classroom into a "hell," Suzune didn't succumb to panic or allow the classroom to disintegrate. She maintained her composure to take control of the situation and achieve her goal.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): During the Sports Festival, Suzune's greatest feat of Emotional Management (EM) wasn't limited to maintaining composure in isolated moments, but rather sustaining constant emotional stability while simultaneously facing strategic (Leadership), political (Kushida), social (Ryuuen), and physical (The Festival) pressures. Suzune simultaneously faced:
   * Full leadership responsibility.
   * Alliance with an enemy (Ryuuen).
   * Kushida's reintegration after serious conflicts.
   * Indirect political threat from Nagumo.
   * Possible Sakayanagi–Ichinose coalition.
   * Constant physical competition (100m, hurdles, team events).
From the outset, Suzune bears the full responsibility for her class. She must not only compete physically in multiple events (100 meters, obstacle course, team events), but also coordinate a complex alliance with Ryuuen, reintegrate Kushida after previous conflicts, mend internal rifts, and recalculate the strategy after accepting the 10-point deduction due to Sakayanagi's absence. All of this occurs while facing the latent threat of a potential rival coalition and indirect pressure from the student council president due to Kiyotaka's rejection of his challenge. Despite receiving hateful stares, cold contempt from her classmates, and the fierce verbal provocations of Shinohara and Hasebe Suzune doesn't falter. She maintains a steely composure, suppressing her inner doubts and insecurities so as not to show weakness in front of the class.

One of the clearest indicators of her Emotional Management is her control of her pride in the face of Ryuuen, historically antagonistic to her class. However, Suzune does not allow past resentments to interfere with the strategic objective. She does not act from ego nor seek to impose her will emotionally in negotiations. She honestly reveals that the strategy is hers, formalizes the agreement institutionally, and eliminates friction before it can escalate into internal sabotage.

Internally, her class was experiencing significant tension. Kushida's reintegration and the cooperation with Ryuuen could generate emotional resistance. Suzune neither ignores this factor nor suppresses it authoritatively. She rebuilds cohesion before the event, reduces opposition, and transforms the emotional climate into competitive discipline. Here, she not only regulates her own emotions but also prevents collective emotions from destabilizing the plan.

Physically, she competes consistently and rigorously, winning key events and accumulating crucial points. However, fatigue does not impair her tactical judgment. Even in the volleyball match, when the score is tied 6-6 and Amasawa displays a surprising adaptation, Suzune does not panic or force impulsive decisions. She adjusts tactics, strategically uses Ibuki as a decoy, and maintains coordination until the match is over. Physiological regulation under fatigue combined with cognitive clarity.

Facing Nagumo, the pressure takes on a real hierarchical dimension. He is suspicious, makes indirect threats, and seeks to provoke a reaction. Suzune maintains a neutral stance, doesn't reveal unnecessary information, and shows no visible intimidation. She controls her potential fear and avoids an emotional confrontation that could jeopardize her academic year.

   * Class Reconstruction (Y2V6-Y2V7): Following the Unanimous Special Exam, the class atmosphere turned completely hostile toward Suzune. Kushida, Hasebe, and Mii-chan were all skipping school by feigning illness, jeopardizing the class's overall score. Instead of backing down in the face of the angry glares and general resentment, Suzune stood at the forefront of the Sports Festival meeting. She offered a public apology for breaking her promise but unwaveringly defended the strategic value of keeping Kushida. She withstood Shinohara's direct verbal attack (one of those most affected by Kushida) without showing a single crack of weakness or doubt. Her control can be categorized as follows:
   * Repression of the "cowardly self": Suzune experienced anxiety and thoughts about the hypothetical scenario ("What if we had protected everyone by sacrificing points?"). However, she categorized these thoughts as those of a "coward" and actively suppressed them to avoid self-sabotage.
   * Preservation of the leader role: If Suzune had shown guilt, fear, or hesitation in front of Shinohara, the class would have crumbled into ungovernable factions. By maintaining her emotional state identical to that of a determined leader (despite the internal chaos), she forced the class to base the discussion on logic (Kushida's abilities) and not on mere emotional outbursts.
   * Acceptance of risk as a shield: By declaring that she would assume full responsibility if her plan failed, she transformed her emotional stability into a buffer for the rest of the students, who went from fury to awe at her determination.

   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): She demonstrated impeccable emotional management when Sudou confessed,  she remained calm, did not become fazed or mocked, and processed the situation maturely to respond appropriately.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): Suzune demonstrates exceptional emotional control under multiple types of pressure:
A) Stability in the face of internal disruptions (Kouenji)
   * Kouenji repeatedly responds blankly. The class becomes emotionally tense.
   * Suzune remains completely unaffected and composed. This is critical:
   * No irritation
   * No impulsive reaction
   * No loss of authority
   * Result: Prevents emotional contagion (collective panic) and maintains group cohesion
B) Emotional regulation of the group
   * She not only controls her own emotions but also those of others:
   * She comforts Yukimura after he fails.
   * She promises to protect him → restores trust.
   * This serves two purposes:
   * It prevents a drop in performance.
   * It maintains the emotional stability of the system.
   * This means that she is not only able to control her own emotions but also to help others control theirs.
C) Control Under Extreme Strategic Pressure
   * Example: Chaotic situation in the second half (comebacks, point pressure) where Ryuuen and Ichinose create a highly volatile environment
   * Reaction: There was nothing more to be done. The only thing was not to panic. This defines its core:
   * Recognizes limits → avoids overreaction
   * Prioritizes consistency over desperation
D) Tolerance for Strategic Failure
   * Example: Strategy fails against Kouenji
   * His trap is seen through by Sakayanagi
   * Strategy partially invalidated
   * Reaction:
   * Does not emotionally collapse
   * Continues executing the overall plan
   * There is no:
   * visible frustration
   * impulsive deviation
   * Even when she felt it

   * Koenji Neutralization Strategy (Y2V10): She managed to handle the frustration of Sakayanagi noticing the trap in time and stopping her attack on Kouenji. She accepted the slight disappointment without losing focus for the remaining 20 turns of the exam.

   * Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12): With no points to lose, the exam seems safe, but the enormous number of points at stake (250 if they win) is vital to Suzune's dream of reaching Class A. This generates nervousness, worry, immense pressure as a leader, and a moderate level of stress that even compels her to seek out sugar to keep her brain functioning. And this pressure increases with Kiyotaka's conditions, where she would lose her best asset if she doesn't perform perfectly. With all these feelings and more, Suzune remained calm throughout the special exam. Even in the face of the various problems she encountered, such as:
   * During her confrontation against Kanzaki, Suzune demonstrated excellent emotional management by maintaining affective discipline and coldly rejecting her opponent's desperate plea, who went so far as to humiliate herself and bow deeply before her, begging for victory. Despite the emotional impact and the space for sympathy or anger that the situation generated, she did not allow these emotions to alter her state nor did she waver in her strategic decisions.
   * Even when she faced Ichinose, when the latter correctly guessed her first consecutive nominations and claimed to have deciphered multiple roles, Suzune felt a chill and the temptation to panic. However, she managed to control her emotions immediately, avoided acting impulsively or prematurely, and consciously calmed herself.a concentrarse en buscar resultados en las siguientes rondas.

Personal ranking for the best EM feats:
UVE (Y2V5) >  Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1) > Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6) > Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12) > Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10) > Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4) > Sudou Integration

EQE/EE (Emotional Engagement) -
   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-now): Suzune begins the story under crushing pressure. She faces the school system with 0 points, the indifference of her tutor, the disadvantage of private capital, and, above all, a deep insecurity about not being "good enough" to receive the recognition of her brother Manabu. Instead of letting isolation or despair paralyze her, Suzune uses this pressure as strategic fuel for herself. For example, during the Year-End Exam (Y2V12), facing a clear numerical and emotional disadvantage against Ichinose, Suzune uses the pressure of the stress to stay focused on decoding microexpressions and tactically interacting on the monitor. Her ability to process her own insecurity and transform that burden into discipline for performing repetitive tasks is her greatest strength. Another examples:
   * During the Class Poll (Y1V10) and the Unanimous Voting Exam (Y2V5), the class descends into collective panic. The threat of expelling a classmate destroys the climate of social cohesion. A normal person would have broken down emotionally to please the group or succumbed to the fear of being hated. Suzune, on the other hand:
   * In Y2V5, she willingly accepts the blame, shattering the class's momentary trust and bearing the hatred of his classmates by saving Kushida (recognizing her long-term strategic value) and signaling Sakura's expulsion. She understands and absorbs the emotional impact of making unpopular decisions.
   * She actively manages the subsequent emotional damage, orchestrating apologies, restructuring the group dynamics through the Cultural Festival, and using the psychological shock of Sakura's expulsion to motivate lower-achieving students (like Hondo) to study desperately to avoid being next.
EE also encompasses the ability to shape and redirect the emotions of others to serve cognitive and academic functions that benefit the overall strategy:
   * Sudō was a violent, impulsive student with zero academic performance. Suzune not only calmed him down but also used the admiration and interest Sudō felt for her to direct his motivation toward studying and self-control. She gradually transformed him into an exemplary, top-10 student.
   * After being exposed in the unanimous exam, Kushida was an emotionally unstable outcast. Suzune emotionally engaged with her, not through naive sympathy, but by aligning interests: she gave her a purpose of status by recruiting her for the Student Council, using Kushida's desire for validation to put her social skills at the service of the group.
   * When Hirata broke down psychologically after Yamauchi's expulsion, Suzune supported the stabilization process (using Kiyotaka as a catalyst) to restore the class's emotional and social foundation.
This is best seen throughout her journey. At the start of Year 1, Suzune's emotional stance was one of isolation ("I can do it alone"). Upon encountering her limitations in the Deserted Island Exam and the Sports Festival, she reconfigures her mental approach. She understands that the emotion of individual pride is useless for winning the game and channels her desire for victory into building a collective machine. This cognitive-emotional flexibility allows her to correct her own biases.

EQU/EU (Emotional Understanding) -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): To understand that Hōsen does not act solely out of greed, but out of an extreme individualistic ideology and a desire for class domination.
   * Distinguishes between apparent (points) and real (control) motivations
   * Understands that their emotional system is incompatible with cooperation
   * Anticipates that even a reasonable offer would be rejected
This understanding applies to both:
   * Hōsen
   * Nanase
   * The collective psychology of Class 1-D

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): Throughout the entire UIE, Suzune clearly detects the emotional state of 2-D and anticipates her reaction. Using Kiyotaka's words, she is able to perform an emotional analysis of Ike and Shinohara's situation. However, her best demonstration of her ability in this exam is in her battle with Amasawa. Before any physical confrontation, Suzune detects an emotional incongruity in Amasawa:
   * Relaxed language, mocking tone → inconsistent with the high-pressure context.
   * Implicit knowledge about Kiyotaka → excessive information for a casual interaction.
   * Persistence in conversation → psychological, not social, objective.
During the interaction (and later in combat), Suzune reaches a key conclusion: Amasawa was already damaged before the encounter. Her aggression stems not from superiority, but from:
   * urgency
   * time pressure
   * the need to prevent Suzune from reaching I2
This is critical because Suzune understands that Amasawa isn't acting from a place of control, but from pent-up tension. This causes Suzune to change her perception of Amasawa from:
   * “dominant enemy”
   * “opponent forced to act”
Suzune understands that:
   * The fight isn't the goal
   * The goal is to stop her, delay her, and emotionally destabilize her
Therefore, she concludes:
   * “If Amasawa is going to such lengths to stop me, then something is going to happen in I2.”
Here, Suzune reverses the emotional manipulation, using Amasawa's hostility as indirect confirmation of the threat and that someone is pulling the strings from the shadows. However, she still had to overcome her. To do so, Suzune uses Ibuki, understanding that Ibuki:
   * Doesn't act logically
   * Actes out of pride and rivalry
So she doesn't try to calm her down. Suzune intentionally provokes her, reinforcing her competitive nature by choosing precisely the emotional stimulus that guarantees cooperation. She makes her an ally because Suzune understands her limitations, recognizing that Amasawa is superior:
   * physically
   * technically
   * in experience
But Suzune understands her own emotions (fear, frustration, inferiority) without allowing them to distort her assessment of the enemy. She:
   * doesn't panic
   * doesn't fall into denial
   * doesn't lose her way
Even under extreme pressure, Suzune maintains a clear idea:
   * Kiyotaka doesn't need to be saved
   * But he can be attacked indirectly
That is mature emotional understanding:
   * She doesn't idealize him
   * She doesn't underestimate him
   * She doesn't project her insecurities onto him

   * UVE (Y2V5): Suzune's assessment of the collective state before forcing unanimity. During Motion 4 (increase difficulty/penalties/decrease rewards), the class was divided:
   * The academically strong weren't afraid of difficulty.
   * The weaker students feared penalties.
   * Others wanted to minimize direct loss.
It wasn't just a rational disagreement. It was a structural emotional conflict. Suzune manages to identify:
   * Which group was voting for personal safety.
   * Which group was voting for academic pride.
   * Which group was undecided but swayed.
And from that, she guides the debate toward an option that ends in unanimity. Suzune identifies the underlying motivation; she doesn't just analyze the vote, she analyzes the emotional reasons behind it:
   * Fear of punishment
   * Confidence in one's own abilities
   * Aversion to uncertainty
That's emotional understanding beyond the superficial. Then she profiles implicit risk. Suzune understands that:
   * Weaker students react to fear.
   * Strong students react to structural incentives.
The majority want stability. Build an argumentative framework that satisfies all three without directly confronting them. This involves collective emotional modeling. Later, during Kushida's explosion, she came to understand the psychological background behind Kushida's mask: the exhaustion and extreme stress of maintaining a perfect facade and the unconscious desire for someone to finally know her true self.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Understanding Ryuuen's psychology and structuring the alliance on terms that reduce his distrust. She understands that:
   * Ryuuen needs to know if the strategy is his or Kiyotaka's.
   * If there is internal doubt within the coalition, its performance will suffer.
   * Ryuuen's reputation creates internal friction, so she had to legitimize the cooperation.

   * Handwriting Identification (Y2V4-Y2V7): Suzune doesn't rely on visible expressions, but rather on:
   * The intention behind actions
   * Implicit emotional context
   * Consistency between behavior and situation
She reads emotions through decisions, not gestures. From the letter, Suzune infers:
   * The author's level of confidence
   * A possible relationship with Kiyotaka
   * The type of mindset (cautious, provocative, calculating)
Building a psychological profile without direct interaction. Suzune distinguishes between:
   * Direct hostility
   * A veiled warning
   * Emotional manipulation
And she avoids simplistic interpretations and emotional ambiguity. Using this understanding. She anticipates that the author will act again and that the threat is not isolated but ongoing, something that happened in Y2V5. It all started with a letter, which only had 3 coded words that had nothing to do with each other.

   * Sudou Integration (Y2V8): Before Sudou says a word, Suzune already understands his emotional state. She notices:
   * He arrives restless
   * He walks quickly
   * He seeks a secluded spot
   * He displays obvious nervousness.
Instead of pressuring him, Suzune adopts an emotionally appropriate strategy:
   * She doesn't rush him
   * She doesn't show irritation
   * She maintains calm curiosity
   * She gives him time to speak.
She understands that Sudou is in a psychologically vulnerable state and that pressuring him could shut him down. When Sudou confesses, Suzune is initially surprised. However, she immediately analyzes his emotional state. She observes:
   * Passion
   * Enthusiasm
   * Sincerity.
This is important because Suzune rules out the possibility of it being a joke. Not because Sudou says so, but because his emotions are too genuine. After analyzing the situation, Suzune takes a few seconds of silence. She understands that how she responds can deeply affect Sudou. Therefore, she formulates a response with three objectives:
   * to reject the confession
   * to avoid hurting him deeply
   * to preserve his personal growth.
Suzune doesn't just think about the present moment. She considers:
   * their future relationship
   * Sudou's self-esteem
   * her personal motivation.
And she managed to reject him gracefully despite it being a completely new situation for her.

   * Kiryuuin Trial (Y2V9): Suzune dismisses Nagumo as the culprit not only logically, but also through an analysis of his personality. Emotional elements she considers:
   * Nagumo enjoys competing with strong rivals.
   * His primary objective was his conflict with Kiyotaka.
   * He would have no interest in starting a conflict with Kiryuuin.
This implies that Suzune understands Nagumo:
   * seeks stimulating challenges
   * doesn't create unnecessary conflicts with people irrelevant to his goals.
She also recognizes that starting a conflict with Kiryuuin right before leaving his post doesn't fit with his competitive mindset. Suzune also analyzes Kiryuuin's emotional reaction. She understands that:
   * Kiryuuin shows genuine displeasure at the accusation of theft.
   * She gains nothing from falsely accusing Nagumo.
   * She has historically had no interest in competing with him.
Suzune correctly interprets Kiryuuin's emotional reaction as authentic. This eliminates the possibility that Kiryuuin is manipulating the situation. Another important element is the information about the third-year students' feelings toward Nagumo. Suzune understands that:
   * many students are dissatisfied with Nagumo's control system
   * there is pent-up resentment.
This leads her to consider that someone close to Nagumo might act against him, even if that someone had been his subordinate. This emotional element is crucial to understanding Kiriyama's motive. Kiriyama is described as:
   * extremely disciplined
   * deeply loyal
   * but increasingly frustrated with Nagumo.
Suzune understands that someone with that profile might act out of:
   * pent-up resentment
   * a desire to rein in Nagumo
   * concern for the students' future.
This explains why Kiriyama would risk his position despite having a guaranteed ticket to Class A.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): Suzune identifies:
   * Ichinose prioritizes protecting everyone.
   * He avoids expulsions even at the cost of points.
   * He has an emotional bias: extreme altruism.
What he uses to predict his actions based on her objective: He keeps the number of Reachers at 5 to force constant protection. The result is that Ichinose enters an emotional loop:
   * She always protects the same people.
   * She neglects the rest.
   * Her class accumulates structural errors.
This is not just about understanding emotions, but about exploiting a stable emotional structure like Ichinose's for one's own benefit. This also applies to Ichinose's defensive target selection. Suzune is unaware of personal factors, yet she deduces the logic behind decisions and uses:
   * behavioral consistency
   * exam context
   * choice patterns
The same applies to her reading of Sakayanagi's intentions, where she:
   * predicts her movements
   * avoids traps
   * seeks total control of the flow
Even recognizing when she's been predicted, she continues without collapsing. And finally during a three-way interaction during the break, with:
   * Sakayanagi (provocation)
   * Ryuuen (aggression)
Suzune:
   * doesn't react emotionally
   * analyzes the dynamic between them
Extracts:
   * Ryuuen's objective (specific expulsions)
   * Sakayanagi's limitations

   * Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12):  During exam preparation, Suzune demonstrates a deep understanding of the emotional dynamics of the second-year Class D. She was able to accurately profile her rivals' intentions.
   * Accurately deduce that Ichinose will occupy the position of General.
   * Correctly predict that Kanzaki will be the Middle Guard.
   * Identify the most likely candidates for the Vanguard position (Hatsukawa, Hamaguchi, or Niiura) based on their competitiveness level.
Demonstrating a remarkable ability to understand the profiles of students outside his class. And showing that he knows his closest allies, this is demonstrated when
   * Despite Hirata's defeat in the initial rounds, Suzune demonstrates empathy and a mature understanding of her classmate's psychology by coming to his comfort. She fully understands that Hirata's failure isn't due to a lack of observational skills, but rather that his very benevolent nature makes him completely incompatible with an exam that, by design, requires actively questioning people.

EQP/EP (Emotional Perception) -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): Suzune detects Nanase's guilt, doubt, and inner conflict from early stages, even when she claimed to be following Hōsen.
   * Nonverbal tension
   * Emotional inconsistencies in speech
   * Signs of repressed disagreement
Thanks to this:
   * She formulates proposals directed at Nanase
   * She avoids treating her as an enemy
   * She turns her into a key ally

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): This feat occurs before any direct confrontation and before there is any objective evidence of danger.

Suzune perceives that Amasawa's behavior is inauthentic. The relaxed smile, the mocking tone, and the carefree attitude don't match the context: the last day of the exam, a critical area, the presence of sensitive information (the paper), and a situation where any mistake could lead to expulsion. This incongruity is the first trigger. Suzune doesn't process it as "weird" or "awkward," but as a clear difference between displayed emotion and genuine emotion.

The crucial point is that Suzune doesn't need to know Amasawa beforehand. Her perception isn't based on history or reputation, but on situational micro-cues: unnecessary insistence, control of the physical space, calculated interruptions, and a calmness that doesn't waver even when Ibuki raises her voice. This artificial emotional stability is correctly interpreted as contained tension, not genuine confidence.

Suzune distinguishes between simulated emotion and authentic emotion under stress. She doesn't mistake arrogance for confidence or mockery for flippancy. She senses that Amasawa is emotionally invested in hindering her progress, which implies an urgent and concrete objective. This reading isn't verbal or logical; it's purely perceptive.

Furthermore, during the fight, this same perception remains active. Suzune notices that, despite Amasawa's physical superiority, there are moments when her breathing quickens and her aggression increases disproportionately. She doesn't interpret this as sadism or amusement, but as accumulated frustration and repressed pain. This reading allows her to understand that Amasawa's stamina is low, something that isn't immediately apparent.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): To detect Nagumo's emotional and psychological decline during the festival. She observes that:
   * Although he wins all his competitions, he appears dejected.
   * His persistence with Kiyotaka is not casual, but obsessive.
   * His threat to the second-year students stems from wounded pride.

   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): She sensed the authenticity, nervousness, and genuine passion in Sudou's voice and posture as he confessed, dismissing any idea that it was a joke and treating him with due respect.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): Suzune identifies in Ichinose:
   * Emotional tendency: empathy + harm aversion
   * Behavioral pattern: avoidance of aggressive attacks
   * Weakness: prioritizing group well-being over efficiency
This is not based on facial expressions, but on reasoning after:
   * Repeated decisions
   * Moral structure
   * Behavioral consistency
I also include her reading of Sakayanagi where Suzune perceives:
   * Her strategic coldness
   * Lack of operational empathy
   * Tendency to maximize efficiency
And his intra-class reading ability. Examples:
   * Detects insecurity in weaker peers
   * Recognizes cognitive limitations (e.g., Ike)
   * Identifies emotional risk (panic, mistakes)
This allows her to:
   * Assign roles
   * Prevent emotional breakdowns among her peers
Suzune uses:
   * Contextual perception (decisions → emotion)
   * Predictive perception (emotion → future action)
   * Comparative perception (how each leader differs)

   * Kanzaki Control (Y2V12): Suzune immediately picks up on the apathy, the weight of shame, and the abandonment of the fight in Kanzaki's gaze and physical posture as he returns to his seat after the confrontation.

   * Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12): Perception and exploitation of the emotional resources of the participants and their rivals. For example, during the first discussion of the confrontation against Kanzaki, Suzune meticulously analyzes the participants' behavior on screen. This is evident when Miyake openly declares himself a "graduate" and accuses Norihito of being an honor student.
   * Suzune realizes Kanzaki falls into the trap and assumes Miyake is telling the truth.
   * Suzune perceives the true situation through reasoning: she notices that executive roles are driven by self-interest (private interests) and perceives that Miyake is acting to attract attention.
This leads her to correctly nominate Miyake as the true honor student, instantly taking away three lives from Kanzaki. Or perhaps immediately after Miyake's elimination, Suzune notices the change in Ninomiya's body language, who is visibly bewildered.
   * Instead of assuming it's an act, Suzune accurately perceives the authenticity of that nervous reaction and correctly deduces that she is the second honor student.
By flawlessly interpreting this micro-gesture, she makes the nomination without fear of making a mistake, winning the round decisively. These defeats push Kanzaki to the breaking point. Kanzaki completely loses the will to fight, displaying a blank body language and simply passing in each round.
   * Suzune immediately senses this state of absolute surrender and, setting aside any kind of condescension, reads the lack of tension in the atmosphere to execute her final nomination on Mine without making any mistakes, sealing a perfect 7-0 victory.
This is also evident even when cornered by Ichinose's overwhelming ability to read people. Suzune realizes she needs to alter her opponent's perception to buy time. She uses a logical argument based on school equity to plant a seed of doubt (the "bait") in Ichinose's mind, suggesting that executive roles are split 50/50 between the two classes.
   * Realizing that Ichinose will have to divide his attention to cover that 1% possibility,
Suzune takes advantage of that minimal distraction to successfully identify Hatsutori as an executive role and regain a life.

EQF/EF (Emotional Facilitation) -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): Deliberately manipulate Sudō's perceived strength in relation to Amasawa to induce respect, interest, and ultimately cooperation. Suzune:
   * She understands how Sudō is perceived emotionally.
   * She reinforces that image of "reliable brute force."
   * She directs the narrative without direct confrontation.

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): Suzune accurately recognizes how Ibuki perceives her:
   * Direct rival → competitiveness, provocation, pride.
She doesn't overestimate her authority, she understands that she doesn't intimidate, but she does provoke a reaction. She detects that her mere presence intensifies Ibuki's emotional rivalry.
   * Suzune deliberately facilitates specific emotions in Ibuki: Provocation → wounded pride.
   * Strategic praise → validation.
   * Direct challenge → competitive activation.
She manipulates her emotional rivalry without escalating to chaos and gets Ibuki to:
   * Accept implicit orders.
   * Synchronize movements.
   * Relinquish tactical control.

   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): She understood that Sudou harbored romantic feelings for her and used that impact constructively to motivate him to study, self-regulate his anger, and improve his grades without dishonestly manipulating or exploiting him.

   * UVE (Y2V5): Suzune's intervention during Kushida's presentation. When the class turns hostile after Kushida's true nature is revealed, the group's natural reaction is:
   * Total rejection
   * Collective anger
   * Immediate desire for expulsion
   * Collapse of consensus
At that point, the group's rational decision had already been made emotionally. But Suzune doesn't try to debate logically as usual. First, she alters the collective emotional state. What exactly does she do?
   * She stops the demonization
   * She doesn't deny the facts
   * She doesn't justify it
   * She changes the emotional interpretation
   * She reframes Kushida psychologically
   * She turns her flaws into an extreme social effort
   * She makes the class go from hatred → discomfort → doubt.
Causing emotional dissonance
   * The class was enjoying condemning her
   * Suzune introduces mandatory empathy
   * No one can continue attacking without appearing cruel
She changes the group's emotional objective
   * From punishment → future utility
   * From morality → strategy
Why it's Emotional Facilitation
   * It's not normal empathy.
   * It's not logical persuasion.
It's intentional group emotional regulation. She understands:
   * The class doesn't decide with arguments.
   * It decides with the emotional climate.
So she modifies the climate first to make the decision viable. Result:
   * She prevents a virtually certain expulsion.
   * She makes possible an option that was psychologically impossible seconds before.
   * She forces the class to accept an alternative against its emotional impulse.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): The feat of EF lies in transforming an emotionally fractured environment into a synchronized, competitive system. Suzune starts from an emotionally fragile situation:
   * Internal resentment within her class.
   * Distrust of Ryuuen.
   * Fatigue accumulated from previous conflicts (SVE and internal tensions).
   * Rejection of Kushida.
The environment was not neutral. It was emotionally volatile. Kushida represented:
   * Internal division.
   * Distrust.
   * Potential emotional sabotage.
Instead of isolating or suppressing her, Suzune:
   * Reorganizes the class structure.
   * Reduces the previously created rifts.
   * Integrates her social influence within the system.
This is advanced EF because it doesn't eliminate the source of tension but redirects it, transforming a potentially destabilizing element into a strategic resource. During the event:
   * Some students were hesitant to cooperate with Ryuuen.
   * There was pressure for results.
   * There was physical fatigue.
However:
   * There are no emotional breakdowns.
   * There are no public conflicts.
   * There is no internal sabotage.
Even initially negative students:
   * Practice day and night.
   * Become committed.
This indicates that Suzune managed to:
   * Stabilize the emotional climate.
   * Maintain competitive morale.
   * Prevent psychological breakdown under pressure.
   * Even with all the problems they brought.

   * Sudou Integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): Initially, Sudou exhibits multiple problems:
   * aggressiveness,
   * low academic intelligence,
   * impulsivity,
   * a tendency towards violence,
   * and a poor social reputation.
Suzune specifically identifies:
   * a need for recognition,
   * fragile pride,
   * a tendency to react to provocations,
   * and an inability to reflect on his actions.
This is the foundation upon which she works. Starting in Year 2, volume 2, Suzune deliberately  provokes
   * anxiety,
   * insecurity,
   * and psychological pressure in order to force introspection.
If she simply resolved the problem, Sudou would continue to depend on others and would not change his behavior. Therefore, Suzune uses controlled negative emotion as a learning mechanism. After stabilizing the situation, Suzune changes her strategy. Instead of pressure, she uses the fact that he admires her to apply positive reinforcement.
   * She acknowledges Sudou's progress,
   * confirms his growth,
   *  and encourages him to continue improving.
This is seen when she analyzes his points earned in the club and motivates him to continue. The emotional effect generated is:
   * healthy pride
   * desire for approval
   * commitment to growth.
The key moment occurs when Suzune speaks honestly about herself to convince Sudou to return.
   * She shares her story
   * admits her weaknesses
   * shows her vulnerability
This has a powerful psychological effect on Sudou:
   * He identifies with her
   * understands her motivations
   * feels genuine respect
This kind of emotional openness creates a deep psychological connection that goes beyond simple admiration. Suzune uses this as motivation to continue training Sudou. The end result is one of the most drastic changes in the series. Sudou goes from Impulsive delinquent to an ally and disciplined student. The changes include:
   * improved behavior
   * reduced violence
   * increased academic effort
   * motivation for the future
   * He even achieves:
   * Top of the class in exams, something unthinkable at the beginning.
All of this happens because Suzune manages to redirect his emotions toward productive goals. The confession scene further reinforces this ability. Suzune:
   * recognizes Sudou's sincerity
   * avoids ridiculing him
   * responds respectfully
And rejects him without damaging her self-esteem. Emotional outcome:
   * She doesn't lose motivation
   * She doesn't distance herself from him
   * She maintains her personal growth

Personal ranking for the best EF feats:
UVE (Y2V5) > Sudou Integration (Y1V2-Y2V8) > Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6) > Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4)  > Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1)

EQR/ER (Emotional Realization) -
   * She is herself (Y1V1-now):  Suzune comes to understand her own limitations when she realizes that she spent years acting as a "cheap imitation" of her brother Manabu. By the end of her first year, she recognizes that her dependence and desire for recognition were hindering her true development as a human being. She recognizes that her initial mindset (being self-sufficient and solitary) was flawed and that mere individual performance was not enough to achieve Class A. She identifies that she required cooperation with others.  She assesses herself as a "shallow pond" compared to the "deep wells" represented by leaders like Ryūen, Sakayanagi, or Ichinose. She openly acknowledges her lack of a tactical specialty or defined fighting style, which allows her to understand the reasons for her strategic setbacks. She also weighs Kushida's toxicity against its strategic value and the impact of losing 350 points. She quickly identifies her own biases and doubts regarding leadership to arrive at an insight that resolves the contradiction. Furthermore, over time, Suzune develops an analytical ability regarding the potential, cognitive and emotional setbacks of her classmates.
   * For example, she understands that even though Sakura was expelled due to her OAA score, she wasn't without merit. She recognizes that Sakura's social blocks at school wouldn't allow her to develop at the same level as her classmates, understanding the internal complexities that affected her overall performance, and acknowledging that, in terms of potential, she wasn't at Kushida's level.
   * She detects the maturation and awareness in Ike after Maezono's expulsion, noticing how his companions recognize their own limitations and seek to overcome them in the long term.
   * She also understands that she experiences a mental block when faced with the possibility of expelling Ibuki. Suzune realizes that her former cold stance ("expel without hesitation") has collapsed because she now understands Ibuki on an emotional level and recognizes her as a friend. She detects the conflict between her strategic principles and her emotional feelings.
   * Over time, Suzune realizes that Ayanokōji doesn't fit the typical mold of an ordinary student. She tries to understand the hidden motives behind his actions and acknowledges the discomfort and vulnerability she feels as she realizes how much he relies on her validation. This was something she feared in her first year; even then, Suzune had a clear understanding of the danger of placing her complete trust in him. By opening her heart and envisioning him as her partner for the following year, Ayanokōji's sudden transfer triggers her greatest emotional and cognitive setback: abandonment at the moment of greatest trust.

   * UVE (Y2V5): Suzune accurately identified her own internal contradictions and her previous weakness in being unable to take the step of carrying out an expulsion due to attachment and idealism. She also recognized Kushida's broken emotional state and hidden need for validation.

   * Kanzaki Control (Y2V12): Suzune instantly identifies Kanzaki's mental breakdown and how his lack of faith in Ichinose and despair over the point gap destroyed him internally, using that cognitive breakdown to dictate the pace of the duel. This means she fully understood Kanzaki's internal turmoil and his lack of faith in Ichinose. Furthermore, at the end of the exam, she painfully acknowledges her own cognitive and social limitations.

Social Skills -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): Simultaneously coordinate Sudō, Kiyotaka, Kushida, Hirata and external students (1-D / 1-B) without breaking the coherence of the overall plan.

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): The UIE is, socially, the most hostile exam up to that point:
   * Non-permanent groups
   * Actual expulsions
   * Limited communication
   * Incomplete information
   * Inter-class and intra-class conflicts
   * Prolonged emotional pressure (2 weeks)
In that environment:
   * A communication error → expulsion
   * A bad message → panic
   * Poor leadership → mass dropouts
Suzune lacks natural charisma, she's not Ichinose, she's not Nagumo. And yet, her class survives intact. Her goal was to keep students with different interests, different fears, and different levels of competence functioning compatiblely, without breaking down, without constant communication, for two weeks. Before the exam begins, Suzune makes a minimal but early intervention with a clear message: "Don't form groups yet, contact me."
   * She doesn't threaten.
   * She doesn't promise rewards.
   * She doesn't coerce.
With this simple action:
   * She establishes functional authority.
   * She reduces impulsive decisions.
   * She doesn't generate resistance.
Class 2-D includes:
   * Responsible students
   * Impulsive students
   * Emotional students
   * Inefficient students
   * Individualistic students (Kouenji)
   * Dependent students (Ike, Sakura)
Suzune understands, and she:
   * Doesn't try to harmonize them.
   * Doesn't try to change them.
   * Doesn't try to resolve emotions.
Instead:
   * She places them where their flaws cause the least harm.
   * She prevents points of friction from arising.
   * She minimizes destructive interactions.
Throughout the island exam, Suzune visits each group in Class 2-D:
   * She intervenes only when there is a risk.
   * She gives only the necessary information.
   * She supports without creating dependency.

   * UVE (Y2V5): The transition from a fragmented class to unanimity under Suzune's leadership is most powerful not in a single speech, but in the entire process:
   * Class divided through multiple votes.
   * Tensions between subgroups (strong vs. weak) (good grade vs. bad grade) (heat lovers vs. cold lovers).
   * Kushida's public exposure → hostile emotional climate.
   * Real risk of internal rupture.
And yet:
   * Unanimity is achieved on critical decisions.
   * Cohesion is maintained.
   * A controversial figure is protected.
   * There is no visible permanent fracture.
This is social skill applied to a complex group system. First, there is status management; Suzune doesn't impose authority, she consolidates it progressively. When she proposes controversial decisions, the class already perceives her as:
   * Competent
   * Consistent
   * Not impulsive
This indicates prior social capital building, which is supported by her social adaptation. Her discourse changes depending on the audience:
   * With rational arguments → strategic arguments.
   * With emotionally driven students → climate regulation.
   * With undecided students → stability.
This implies situational awareness + communicative adjustment. Which is protected by its polarization prevention. In contexts of forced voting, the following are common:
   * Bloc formation.
   * Subsequent resentment.
   * Permanent factions.
However, in UVE:
   * Division leads to unanimity.
   * No visible rupture occurs.
This demonstrates social friction management. Which is reflected when a conflict is transformed into cohesion. The situation with Kushida could have:
   * Destroyed group trust.
   * Generated paranoia.
   * Weakened leadership.
Instead:
   * It becomes a reaffirmation of Suzune's leadership.
   * The class ends up more aligned.

   * Strategic communication.

   * Three-minute turns

Suzune manages to:
   * Centralize decisions without creating chaos
   * Maintain clarity in instructions (what to protect, whom to attack, when to change focus)

This indicates high-level operational communication, not just theoretical. Suzune not only communicates, but also organizes the group's behavior:

This reflects functional, not charismatic, but effective communicative leadership. While competent, she is not perfect, but good enough to:

   * UVE (Y2V5): Suzune's Decision Regarding Kushida After Understanding Her Emotional Structure: At the critical moment, the superficial, rational reaction was:

That's cold calculation, but Suzune does something different. She reconstructs Kushida's inner world. Suzune understands that:
   * Kushida's social facade isn't simple malice.

   * Current behavior
   * Future potential
   * Latent capacity for cooperation
This separation requires advanced cognitive empathy, especially in overcoming interpersonal bias. Suzune had personal reasons for rejecting her. However:
   * She doesn't decide from resentment.
   * She doesn't decide from pride.
   * She doesn't make her decision out of a desire for punishment.
That shows she managed to put herself in the shoes of someone who hurt her. Strategic but genuine empathy; it's not sentimentality. It's a cold but real understanding of Kushida's suffering and motivation. Suzune recognizes that:
   * The class is judging her from the outside,
