Resource Management -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): With significantly less financial capital than Classes 2-A and 2-C, Suzune flawlessly managed her class's most limited resource (private points and high-achieving students). She refused to engage in a bidding war that would have bankrupted Class 2-D and instead leveraged the talents of A-list students (such as Kushida, Mii-chan, Hirata, and Yukimura) to secure deals and exchanges without wasting funds.

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): Prioritizing the provision of food and supplies to the least adaptable members of Class 2-D, instead of optimizing immediate personal or group points. Its resource management is characterized by:
   * Not maximizing self-interest
   * Not centralizing resources
   * Not competing unnecessarily in high-risk tasks
Instead:
   * Using challenges to obtain food
   * Redistributing it to those who need it most
   * Reducing the likelihood of medical emergencies or collapses
This is advanced Resource Management because:
   * It prioritizes system stability, not maximum performance
   * It reduces future consumption of critical resources (time, points, rescues)
   * It keeps the class 2-D functional for 14 days
In short, despite having less financial and physical capital than other classes, Suzune masterfully managed her most unpredictable and important assets: she transformed Kōenji (an idle and selfish resource) into the class's main offensive engine. Simultaneously, she managed rations and points by gathering supplies during the exam to support the lower-performing groups and prevent their elimination.

   * Handwriting Identification (Y2V4-Y2V8): She had the ability to manage and utilize available resources efficiently. She leveraged the budget, the Student Council's logistics, and Ibuki's strength/instinct as assets in her investigation.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Suzune doesn't just see "students." She identifies differentiated resources:
   * Human Capital
   * Elite athletes (Sudou, Onodera, Koenji).
   * Functional average students.
   * Socially influential figures (Kushida).
   * External strategic allies (Ryuuen, Katsuragi).
   * External critical variable (Kiyotaka).
   * Numerical Capital
   * Student difference between classes (38 vs. 40).
   * Proportional distribution in group competitions.
   * Point Capital
   * Differential value between individual and group competitions.
   * Participation reward.
   * Impact of a 10-point penalty.
   * Energy Capital
   * Cumulative fatigue.
   * Risk of overusing strong athletes.
   * Political Capital
   * Formal contract with Ryuuen.
   * Regulatory restrictions.
Limit of 3 group events per person. This decision is key. If only using:
   * Sudou
   * Onodera
It would have maximized immediate performance but generated:
   * Exhaustion.
   * Structural dependence.
   * Long-term vulnerability.
By imposing a limit:
   * It prevents overexploitation.
   * It broadens participation.
   * It increases the sustainability of performance.
This is further enhanced by the agreement with Ryuuen, which included:
   * Equalizing the number of participants.
   * Equalizing the distribution of points.
   * Allowing for flexible victory depending on the scenario.
   * Formalizing the registration rules contractually.
This avoids:
   * Internal conflicts.
   * Accusations of betrayal.
   * Imbalance in competitive load.
It is a structural management strategy that reduces future friction. Suzune agrees to:
   * Sacrificing Kiyotaka's participation.
   * Losing 10 points.
   * Recalculating the overall strategy.
In return:
   * It neutralizes the Sakayanagi variable.
   * It reduces strategic uncertainty.
   * It ensures a structural advantage.
This is indirect resource management. The plan includes:
   * Participation control of the students of defect class.
   * Energy control (Fatigue)
   * Registration control.
   * Contract control.
   * Numerical control.
   * Political risk control.
   * External variable control.
   * Morale control.
In short, Suzune manages her class's human capital to the fullest. She knows they have 38 students (2 fewer than rival classes, starting with an implicit 20-point disadvantage). She assigns Sudou and Onodera to events where they are guaranteed to win, makes sure to include Kouenji by leveraging his financial motivation, and negotiates with Ryuuen the distribution of participation quotas in group events to avoid overloading her star athletes.

   * Suzune Cultural Festival Strategy (Y2V5-Y2V7): Suzune manages the initial capital and additional funds in such a way as to disguise the true financial power of her class, protecting her plans from possible enemy retaliation or counterattacks.
   * Suzune secures a personal bonus of 10,000 points for her work on the student council, matching the maximum possible amount achieved by Sudou and Onodera. Adding the contributions of nine other classmates, her class reaches a total of 39,400 additional points (equivalent to the initial budget of six students), surpassing Sakayanagi's Class A (18,800), Ryuuen's Class C (17,000), and Ichinose's Class D (26,600). Suzune avoids complacency and keeps these figures in strategic reserve so as not to alert rivals to her true budgetary capabilities.
   * She coordinates the use of the initial budget of 195,000 points (from all 39 students in the class) and designs a system where students invest their own private points to conduct preliminary tests (such as at the takoyaki stand). This initial private investment acts as an operational filter and a low-risk decoy, concealing the ultimate destination of the class's official funds until the final proposal is approved.
   * She analyzes the rental costs in the special building (where the first floor costs 50,000 points and the third floor ranges from 10,000 to 13,000 points). After assessing the risk of being visually blocked by other exhibitions, it deliberately chooses to assume the high cost of the first floor to ensure a steady flow of clients, using this substantial expenditure to project to external observers that the class has exhausted most of its budget.
To distort the rest of the school's perception of her class's financial health and the risks they take, Suzune runs a fake accounting infrastructure in collusion with Ryuuen.
   * It allows and supports Ryuuen's public declaration of a 5 million private point bet between Class 2-B and Class 2-C on the festival's outcome. In reality, the existence of these promised points is a facade devised by Kiyotaka and executed by Suzune, Ryuuen, and Katsuragi to capture the school's attention without actually risking the money.
   * When it's leaked publicly that her class was planning a Maid Café, Suzune appears to outside observers and rivals to be trapped in a "sunk cost bias," suggesting that the class is forced to continue only because it has already placed orders for non-refundable materials. This simulated financial vulnerability convinces competitors that Class 2-B is operating out of desperation.
   * The fake high-stakes financial bet raises the event's profile, turning the apparent resource gap into a massive, free, school-wide publicity campaign that attracts the attention of adults and evaluators.
Suzune manages the class staff by deliberately hiding the use of her best assets or reassigning destabilizing elements to protect the overall performance of the project.
   * Despite internal criticism regarding Kushida's absence from group rehearsals, Suzune conceals the fact that Kushida is training intensively at night in Suzune's room. By keeping this refinement in customer service and accounting (where she achieved zero errors) hidden, Suzune protects and deploys a "secret weapon" capable of charming adult visitors and maximizing sales on the day of the event.
   * Knowing Hasebe Haruki's resentment and desire for revenge (backed by Miyake Akito), Suzune assigns them secondary and peripheral roles far from the Maid Café's core operations. She justifies this treatment under the formal pretext of not forcing Hasebe to work as a maid, thus neutralizing any attempt at internal sabotage without openly revealing her suspicions.
   * Faced with Matsushita's sudden illness (fury, cough, and fever) and Miyake and Hasebe's initial absence on the day of the event, Suzune's human resource planning allows the class to operate without interruption or loss of income. Later, when Hasebe returns, Suzune immediately integrates her as a maid to alleviate the operational burden, transforming a problematic asset into a functional resource.
   * She assesses the unavailable resource that Koenji represents, confirming his refusal to participate beforehand and completely excluding him from the budget and role assignments to avoid wasting effort on persuading him.
Suzune does not just manage the class money; she actively manages the monetary flow of visitors (the 38,400,000 points distributed among 485 guests) through a diversified network of attractions.
   * Divide the class offering into four interconnected components to maximize resource capture:
   * Maid Café (Core): Selling high-margin beverages (tea at 800 points) and offering photo sessions (Cheki), a low-cost, fast-paced, and high-value service.
   * Outdoor Stalls #1 and #2: Flour-based foods (takoyaki/okonomiyaki) and Western-style pasta/breads to generate volume.
   * Use the male-run outdoor food stalls as advertising "steps." Offer half-price drink coupons at the Maid Café to those who purchase from the outdoor stalls, channeling customer traffic and visitor spending directly to the highest-margin area of ​​the special building.
   * Establish a system where Maid Café customers can order food from the outdoor stalls; a designated student transports the order and delivers the prepared food, achieving efficient cross-consumption that raises the average spend per visitor without increasing fixed space costs.
   * Children's Quiz: Funded at the last minute with the remaining budget (surplus budget) to diversify the demographic reach towards underage visitors.
   * Finally, she requires work teams to conduct thorough cost analyses before approving budgets. In the initial Maid Café proposal, she reviews quotes from three different costume suppliers (renting only a sample from each at 4,000 points to compare quality and price) and evaluates the difference between inexpensive and expensive tableware to keep the total operating cost below 50% of the available budget.

   * Sudou Integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): Thanks to Kiyotaka, Suzune identifies that Sudou is not just a problem, but also a potential asset. Characteristics of Sudou's resource:
   * Potential Value
   * Excellent physical ability
   * Usefulness in sporting events
   * Positive impact on class points
Risks:
   * Violence
   * Impulsivity
   * Low academic performance
   * Possible expulsion
If Sudou were expelled, the class would lose:
   * A key athlete
   * Competitive ability in physical exams
   * A member who can generate future points
Suzune understands this. That's why she decides to invest time and effort in saving him, even when doing so doesn't yield immediate benefits. This decision demonstrates long-term resource management. Suzune applies three methods to develop Sudou.
   * 1. Behavioral Correction
   * She doesn't rescue him immediately. Instead:
   * She forces him to reflect
   * She exposes him to consequences
   * She makes him confront his behavior
   * This prevents the resource from deteriorating again.
   * 2. Motivational Reorientation
   * Sudou lacks intrinsic academic motivation. Suzune shifts her motivation towards:
   * personal pride
   * recognition
   * continuous improvement
   * This transforms Sudou into a self-sustaining resource.
   * Over time, Sudou improves:
   * behavior
   * academic effort
   * emotional stability
The end result: Sudou goes from being the worst student to being at the top of the class.

   * Written Comprehensive Cooperative Exam (Y2V9): Suzune managed academic resources and each student's "point value" according to the OAA exam's scoring table. She identified that students with D and E scores awarded more points per correct answer (4 and 5 points, respectively) than those at the A level (1 point). She strategically managed her staff to maximize this scoring system without wasting the 10-minute time limit per student.

   * Sudou integration (Y1V2-Y2V8): Suzune identified Sudou not as a "disposable criminal", but as the most powerful physical asset in the class. He methodically managed his potential, investing time in personalized tutoring to refine his abilities and transform him into a key player in class point accumulation.

   * President of the student council (Y2V2-Y2V9): She efficiently managed the council's human capital. She assessed the OAA requirements (academic grade B or higher) and decided not to remove Hirata from his club in order to avoid losing the passive class points he generated, seeking external resources instead.

   * Kiryuuin Trial (Y2V9): At the school's macro-political level, Suzune used the trial verdict to dismantle Nagumo's financial resources. By stipulating that the millions of private points collected from the third year could only be used for the collective benefit of that generation and not for Nagumo's personal whims, she removed his most powerful economic weapon in the school.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): The critical resources of the exam system are:
   * Students (human capital)
   * Protections per turn (max. 5)
   * Accumulated points (for difficulty)
   * Individual errors (limit = 3 → elimination)
   * Time (3 minutes per decision)
Regarding human capital, Suzune understands that not all students have the same functional value. Therefore, she identifies students with:
   * High-value (consistent, versatile) Liabilities (risk of elimination)
She distributes exposure:
   * She prevents certain students from accumulating errors quickly
   * She doesn't always "overprotect" the same students
This prevents collapse due to risk concentration. This is where protection management comes in. Protections function as:
   * "Denial of harm + conversion to a positive point"
Suzune doesn't protect reactively, but predictively. She analyzes:
   * Enemy targeting patterns
   * Probability of each student's selection
   * Maximizing efficiency.
This is resource management with a double return (defensive + offensive). This leads us to manage errors as a negative resource. A couple of errors don't constitute a cumulative elimination point on the exam. Suzune:
   * Distributes errors among multiple students
Avoids:
   * Early eliminations (−1 point + loss of asset)
   * Maintains long-term system stability
Controls systemic risk, not just immediate performance. And her time management focuses on:
   * Centralizing decisions (efficient leadership)
   * Reducing group noise
   * Prioritizing high-impact decisions
Suzune's approach is:
   * Preventive (avoids losses)
   * Distributed (doesn't concentrate risk)
   * Predictive (anticipates optimal use)
This means she surgically managed the 5 protection slots per turn and the use of the class points earned. She knew how to determine who was worth protecting (academically weak students) and who to temporarily sacrifice in order to optimize every point.

   * Five Reachers strategy (Y2V10): She managed with mathematical precision the exam's scarcest resource: the 5 Protect slots. She completely neutralized Class C's ability to use that resource offensively or defensively against other students.

   * Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12): He distributed his classmates, structuring a highly competent group of students capable of maintaining composure and reading the room (such as Sudō, Miyake, and Matsushita). By precisely managing this human resource, he completely dominated the initial dynamics of the debate against Kanzaki.

   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-Now): Suzune transformed Class D from zero class points and extreme material deprivation. She managed human capital by refining difficult pieces (like Sudou), maximized private points through Student Council rules, and included the use of Ayanokoji as her "ultimate resource" and strategic lifeline.

Regarding the system:

She thinks socially on multiple levels:
   * Individual

It's when Suzune asks herself, "What do I, Horikita Suzune, want to do?" That's social metacognition. First, she engages in self-interpretation within the social system. Suzune understands that:
   * Her decision doesn't just affect points.
   * It defines what kind of leader she will be.
   * It sets a precedent for future crises.
   * It reconfigures how the class perceives her.
That's structural social thinking. She isn't just evaluating outcomes. She's evaluating her role in the group's architecture. She also possesses an understanding of the systemic effect of the expulsion. Expelling Kushida implied:
   * Consolidating authority through fear.
   * Reducing internal complexity.
Sending a zero-tolerance message. Saving her implied:
   * Betting on reintegration.
   * Building trust based on growth.
   * Accepting reputational risk.
Suzune understands both models of social structure and deliberately chooses one. That's social cognition. She doesn't see the class as isolated individuals. She sees it as:
   * A system with collective memory.
   * A network of trust.
   * An entity that evolves based on critical decisions.
Thinking of the group as an emergent, evolving system. The decision integrates:
   * Her personal identity.
   * Kushida's psychological profile.
   * Collective stability.
   * Competition against other classes like Sakayanagi, Ryuuen, or Ichinose.
In short, Suzune understood the deep dynamics of the class: she understood how the superficial trust that the students placed in Kushida worked and how to use that same logic to argue that Kushida's effort to fit in was an extraordinary talent.

Suzune accurately assesses her class's standing relative to the others. She understands that Class 2-D is no longer seen as a flawed group, but as a genuine threat in the standings, and she uses this renewed sense of power to negotiate on equal footing with Katsuragi and Ryuuen.

   * Suzune Cultural Festival Strategy (Y2V5-Y2V7): She understood Kushida's social and emotional dynamics. Instead of ostracizing or punishing her for her exposed secrets, Suzune protected her and welcomed her into her room to rehearse. She understood how to channel Kushida's exceptional social skills for serving adult male clients.

Before acting, she considers:

This is second- and third-order social thinking. The most important thing is that Suzune manages to integrate:
   * Individuals

   * Student council
Within a single, coherent framework. This is what makes her viable as president; she not only understands people, but also social structures and how to manage them.

This allows her to anticipate defensive actions, leading to strategic inter-class reading. Suzune understands that. The exam isn't a one-on-one competition; it's a circular system (A→B→C→D→A). Therefore, she considers:

She manipulates:

She uses these incentives to model others' decisions and understand what's happening behind the scenes so she doesn't fall behind. Suzune doesn't just think:

Leadership -
   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-Now): She took over the leadership of the "worst group in ANHS history" (38–40 students) characterized by a lack of goals, disorganization, low discipline, and constant gender conflicts.
   * She created a sustainable system of tutoring and mandatory study groups for lower-achieving students at the OAA.
   * She raised the minimum passing grade for the class from a historical 14 to an academic floor of 53 (Hondou), placing the class in direct competition with Class A.
   * She established strict protocols to prevent information leaks on critical exams.
She understood that a leader's primary skill is leveraging available resources, regardless of their nature:
   * She identified Kiyotaka from the start as the class's most valuable strategic asset, securing his cooperation in exchange for protecting his "peaceful life."
   * With Sudou, she analyzed the energy of a violent delinquent, transforming him into a top-10 academic student and sports leader.
   * With Kōenji, she created contracts to push his performance and used his Protection Points as a safety net without expending collective resources.
   * With Kushida, she prevented her expulsion by recognizing her social value, reintegrated her into the group dynamic, and appointed her vice president of the Student Council to channel her abilities.
   * With Yukimura, she motivated him and pushed his abilities to their limits.
   * With Matsushita, even without knowing about her hidden abilities, she was able to sense her intelligence and use it in the FMA.
   * With Karuizawa, she became her friend, empathizing with her and making her her loyal ally.
   * With Hirata, she accepted his support and used it to control the social dynamics of the class alongside Karuizawa.
   * With the others, she earned their respect to the point that, despite all their selfish desires, they chose to follow her and trust her.
She transitioned from a solitary, individualistic approach to becoming the "moral enforcer" and inspector of the class.
   * She bore the political cost of making unpopular decisions for the collective good (such as calling for Yamauchi's expulsion for treason or supporting Sakura's departure).
   * She assisted in mending internal divisions after the Unanimous Voting Exam, organizing group meetings and relying on social figures like Hirata, Sudou, and Karuizawa to rebuild trust.
   * She rose to the Student Council Presidency, extending her leadership beyond her classroom to exert political influence over the entire institution, faculty, and other grade levels.
She controlled 38–40 students (Class 1-D / 3-A) + the ANHS Student Council. Starting from a point of 0 Class Points, the worst academic and physical averages of her entire year, and zero social cohesion, she managed to reach 1,233 Class Points, promotion to Class 2-A, and a general increase in OAA statistics. Not to mention that she gradually detached herself from the "Ayanokōji factor," achieving tactical victories where he functioned as a mere executor under her direct orders.

   * Her reputation is destroyed.

Saving her was only the first step. The real challenge was: How to prevent the class from fracturing permanently? In adolescent groups, after a public betrayal:
   * Social exclusion arises.
   * Micro-factions form.
   * Chronic mistrust takes root.
Suzune's decision not only avoids expulsion. It seeks to prevent Kushida from becoming permanently isolated. That's long-term relationship management. Expelling her would have simplified the immediate problem. But it would have left:
   * Internal fear.
   * A precedent for expulsion.
   * Fragility in future crises.
   * By keeping her, Suzune protects the narrative of: “The class grows and corrects itself, it doesn't destroy itself.”

   * she avoids humiliating him
   * she maintains respect
The result is that Sudou doesn't break off the relationship with her and even maintains his personal motivation. This demonstrates a very high ability to preserve relationships even after emotionally dangerous situations.

   * Efficient decision → expulsion.
   * More complex decision → attempt to integrate her.
At that point, Suzune stops and asks the key question: What do I want to do? That is genuine introspection under pressure. First, she acknowledges internal conflict. Suzune identifies that within her coexist:
   * Personal resentment.

   * Easy.
   * Justifiable.
   * Socially acceptable.
But she also recognizes that part of that decision could be tainted by pride or frustration. That is awareness of internal bias. She doesn't react; she redefines herself. She decides what kind of leader she wants to be:
   * One who eliminates threats.
   * Or one who transforms problems into growth.
This act of conscious self-definition is a high level of self-awareness that leads her to a separation between past and future identity. Suzune doesn't act like the previous version of herself. She recognizes that:
   * She has changed.
   * She wants to lead differently.
   * She no longer operates in isolation.
   * This demonstrates a dynamic understanding of herself.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Recognize that you need strategic support from Kiyotaka in certain critical variables (Sakayanagi case) and accept your own limitations without excessive pride.

   * President of the student council (Y2V2-Y2V9): Suzune considered it pointless. She preferred to stay in her room studying rather than performing student council duties, because that was more beneficial for her.

Student council work was not the only thing that seemed somewhat useless. Whether it was club activities or socializing with friends, it was essentially a series of inefficiencies. Some might transition from club activities into professional careers, or from friendships into future employment opportunities—but for many, they would become nothing more than memories of the past. On the other hand, dedicating oneself to academics was more likely to lead to a prosperous future. It was the most solid and secure option students could choose.

However, Suzune learned that there were many lessons to be gained even from things that did not appear immediately useful. She also compared herself to her brother, who had been able to carry out student council responsibilities flawlessly while still achieving impeccable academic results.

That realization made her recognize just how remarkable her brother was—and how far she still had to go to surpass him, despite the additional responsibilities she had taken on.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): From the outset, he openly acknowledges that:
   * She is not the perfect choice.

Therefore:

This demonstrates:

Suzune perfectly understands that in the exam:
   * Being a leader = personal immunity + total responsibility
   * She must decide on expulsions without being able to "sacrifice" herself
This creates a strong psychological paradox, but:
   * She doesn't evade the role
   * She doesn't romanticize it
   * She assumes it functionally
Suzune not only "knows herself," but she also:
   * Accurately evaluates herself under pressure
   * Acknowledges past mistakes without distortion
   * Understands her impact as a leader
   * Detects collective emotional states

   * She is herself (Y1V1-now): Suzune demonstrates an extraordinarily profound self-awareness of her own abilities, limitations, and emotional drivers. Unlike other leaders, she fully understands that she is neither a genius nor does she possess the innate cunning of her direct competitors. She honestly acknowledges her tendency to seek validation from figures she considers superior (historically her brother Manabu and, in their second year, Kiyotaka). What is extremely valuable about her self-awareness is that, instead of allowing her insecurities or the stark self-recognition of her weaknesses to limit or paralyze her, she uses this deep self-knowledge and her dissatisfaction after defeats as psychological fuel to strengthen herself, get back up, and continue fighting with determination toward the goal of reaching Class A.

Personal ranking for the best Self Awareness feats:
UVE (Y2V5) > She is herself (Y1V1-now) > President of the student council (Y2V2-Y2V9) > Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10) > Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4) > Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6) > Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1)

Social Integration -
   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-Now): She overcame her initial isolation and communication difficulties to integrate and forge alliances with key figures within her class (Hirata, Karuizawa, Sudou, Yukimura) and leaders of other classes (Ichinose).

Personal ranking for the best Social Integration feats:

Influence Building -
   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-Now): She built her political capital from scratch: first gaining the trust of her classroom, then projecting herself into school administration by entering and assuming the Student Council Presidency, matching in impact experienced leaders like Ichinose.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Through this exam, Suzune dramatically elevates her leadership status not only within her classroom but also among the other year leaders. She projects an image of a leader willing to make ruthless decisions for the greater good, causing Katsuragi and Ryuuen to stop viewing her class as a "flawed" group and begin treating her as a respectable rival and partner.

   * President of the student council (Y2V2-Y2V9): Unlike charismatic leaders, Suzune:
   * Doesn't start with influence
   * Doesn't impose authority
   * Doesn't depend on popularity
Her process is:
   * Consistent results
   * Error reduction
   * Making difficult decisions
   * Progressive external validation
This generates cumulative and stable influence that Suzune optimizes with key resources:
   * Uses academic achievements as a basis for legitimacy
   * Leverages critical moments (exams, class decisions)
   * Leverages others' mistakes to strengthen her position
Doesn't need:
   * Constant manipulation
   * Aggressive emotional control
   * Social dominance
This indicates High cost-effectiveness of influence. The progression from the beginning is:
   * Level 1: Influence over individuals (Sudou)
   * Level 2: Influence over groups (Defective Class)
   * Level 3: Structural influence (council candidacy)
This is key because she not only gains influence, but expands it to larger systems. This gives her a solid foundation, making her influence difficult to break because:
   * It's based on results, not perception.
   * It doesn't depend on a single relationship.
   * It's not emotionally volatile.
Even under pressure:
   * It maintains credibility.
   * It prevents leadership collapses.

Social Engineering -
   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-Now): At the beginning of the series, Class D functioned like a failed society: anarchic, driven by short-term impulses, and lacking in capital (0 class points). Suzune realized that to regulate the group's future, simply winning isolated exams wasn't enough; she had to modify the daily habits of the individuals.
   * She designed a system of mandatory tutoring and study protocols focused on the weakest members.
   * She created structural incentives and an environment where poor individual performance directly affected collective survival.
This intervention raised the class's academic average from a historically low 14 to a sustainable average above 50 points, institutionalizing teamwork in a group that was originally individualistic. Furthermore, social engineering requires shaping the behavior of citizens/members without destroying them or generating continuous insurrections. For example:
   * Instead of isolating or allowing the expulsion of the most volatile and socially destructive element (Sudou), Suzune, after his growth, regulated his behavior through social conditioning and academic support. She channeled his aggression into athletic and academic discipline, transforming a social risk into a top-10 academic/physical asset who served as a pillar for the class.
   * With Kōenji, she structured negotiations and bets to force his cooperation on key points without attempting to change his indomitable personality, functionally integrating him into the system without breaking its cohesion.
   * With Kushida, after the Unanimous Voting Exam, he made the decision to save a highly destructive sociopathic element to the social fabric, absorb the cost of the group's resentment and reconfigure her role by appointing her vice president of the Student Council, neutralizing internal sabotage and taking advantage of her networks of contacts.
To guide the future of a society, a leader must manage the collective traumas that threaten to fracture the community.
   * During critical expulsions (such as Yamauchi's for treason or Sakura's sacrifice), Suzune assumed the role of "executor of unpopular decisions." This protected the group's morale by channeling the tension onto herself as a firm authority figure, preventing the class from being destroyed by recrimination wars.
   * Following the emotional fractures of the unanimous exam, she coordinated with social authority figures (Hirata, Karuizawa) to restore internal peace, ensuring that the loss of one member did not lead to the group's operational collapse in subsequent exams.
Social engineering reaches its highest level when regulation transcends the student group of origin and connects with the macro system.
   * Suzune deciphered the school's implicit rules (the value of OAA Points, the influence of the Student Council, inter-class dynamics) and readapted the culture of Class D to align with the requirements of Class A.
   * Her rise to Student Council President transformed her from regulating the behavior of 40 students to influencing the policies, regulations, and conduct of the entire ANHS student body.

   * Suzune Cultural Festival Strategy (Y2V5-Y2V7): She designed an internal socioeconomic system for the class: students who contributed viable ideas and private capital for testing would receive a proportional share of the 390,000 future monthly private points generated by the Class Points earned. This transformed the class's apathy and individualism into healthy, incentive-driven internal competition.

   * President of the student council (Y2V2-Y2V9): She regulated the future political structure of the school by actively recruiting representatives from different years, ensuring a stable student government that directly benefits her classroom towards Class A.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): She shaped the atmosphere of her own class before and during the exam. She manipulated the class leadership election by openly inviting others to put themselves forward, knowing that no one would take responsibility, thus legitimizing her authority democratically and unwaveringly.

Personal ranking for the best Social Engineering feats:
D class upgrade (Y1V1-Now)

Self Management -

Suzune can maintain appropriate behavior in public spaces even under extreme provocation. Not only in familiar places, but also in a strange location like an uninhabited island, she successfully makes the conscious choice to operate as a solitary support agent, maintaining behavioral consistency for two full weeks without social or strategic collapse. She also maintains functional leadership and public composure while the class emotionally fractures, as when Kushida rebels.

At the sports festival, while maintaining an active strategic role without letting fatigue affect her decisions, or at the cultural festival, where she worked as a maid in a costume she finds somewhat humiliating, she manages to perform and act flawlessly. This was further demonstrated when she became student council president and her command of all students, teachers, and the school itself whenever something falls under her supervision.

The next example of her emotional management occurred during the Survival and Elimination Special Exam, where Suzune demonstrates constant emotional regulation, control of the social environment, information management, interpersonal adaptability, and precise behavioral execution. And even her ability to maintain her leadership status without losing control in direct interactions and provocations with hostile and complex figures like Sakayanagi and Ryuuen during the break

And finally, the final exam, where, forced to compete in her weakest area against Ichinose's absolute dominance, Suzune demonstrated a remarkable ability to manage her actions tactically. She orchestrated a deception by sowing doubt in Ichinose's mind about an equitable distribution of executive roles between the two classes. This forced her opponent to shift her attention to her own classmates, allowing Suzune to buy vital time in the public space of the exam and land a decisive blow by identifying Hatsutori as an executive. She also demonstrated adaptability by successfully navigating Ayanokōji's harsh contractual conditions in just ten seconds to prioritize the class's victory, keeping the agreement strictly secret to prevent leaks.

Personal ranking for the best Self Management feats:

Self Regulation -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): Avoid emotional escalations even when physically assaulted. Even when it would be "justified," she does not act because:
   * Pride
   * Anger
   * Fear
   * Impulse

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): Sustained emotional control in the face of direct provocation, physical defeat, strategic uncertainty, and the threat of expulsion, without compromising her judgment. Suzune endures:
   * Ibuki's provocations
   * Amasawa's psychological manipulation
   * Real risk of expulsion
   * Extreme exhaustion
Suzune:
   * Does not react impulsively
   * Does not spiral emotionally
   * Does not make decisions to "prove something"
Even in combat:
   * Evaluates
   * Adjusts
   * Changes her objective
Key regulation factors:
   * Acknowledges fear and frustration without letting them control her
   * Uses dialogue to buy time
   * Redefines the objective (survive > win and later win > survive)

   * UVE (Y2V5): Suzune's Emotional Stability When Kushida Confronts Her Publicly During the Presentation:
   * Kushida attacks.
   * The class watches.
   * There is extreme social pressure.
   * Her leadership could crumble in seconds.
At that moment, any impulsive reaction would have:
   * Validated the personal conflict.
   * Further polarized the class.
   * Damaged her legitimacy.
But Suzune:
   * Doesn't lose her composure.
   * Doesn't respond with anger.
   * Doesn't try to humiliate her.
   * Doesn't play the victim.
   * Maintains consistent emotional regulation.
A public attack triggers:
   * A threat to status.
   * A defensive response.
   * A possible aggressive response.
Suzune inhibits that primary reaction. That's executive emotional regulation that separates emotion from action. She may feel frustration or tension, but she doesn't translate emotion into impulsive behavior. She maintains:
   * A stable posture.
   * A controlled tone.
   * A structured speech.
   * That's action-impulse control.
   * It's not a brief moment.
   * The tension persists throughout deliberation and voting. Shemaintains stability throughout the entire process.
Its stability:
   * Reduces emotional escalation.
   * Prevents the class from descending into chaos.
   * Enables the final decision to be actionable.
   * Personal regulation stabilizes the environment.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Suzune faces several challenges simultaneously:
   * Physical fatigue from actively competing.
   * Internal resistance to the alliance with Ryuuen.
   * Unresolved tensions with Kushida.
   * Institutional pressure from Nagumo.
   * Uncertainty regarding Sakayanagi.
   * Direct provocations from Amasawa.
   * The full responsibility of leadership.
Any emotional breakdown could:
   * Break the coalition.
   * Create internal division.
   * Destabilize her leadership.
   * Reduce group performance.
One of the strongest indicators of self-regulation is the ability to:
   * Cooperate with a former enemy.
   * Accept equitable contractual terms.
   * Not react defensively to strategic doubts.
Suzune actively competes (100m, hurdles, etc.) and simultaneously:
   * Coordinates strategy.
   * Monitors performance.
   * Evaluates cumulative score.
   * Mentally adjusts planning.
Physical fatigue typically decreases emotional regulation. In her case, there is no evidence of behavioral impairment. This demonstrates:
   * High psychological resilience.
   * Separation between physical condition and strategic clarity.
   * Sustained behavioral control.
Even after gaining an advantage:
   * Does not underestimate Sakayanagi.
   * Does not rule out the possibility of surprise.
   * Does not fall into premature triumphalism.
Maintains probabilistic thinking. Self-regulation here connects with:
   * Cognitive discipline.
   * Strategic prudence.
   * Avoidance of overconfidence.

   * Written Comprehensive Cooperative Exam (Y2V9): She simultaneously managed the overwhelming workload of the Student Council Presidency and the preparation for the special exam. Despite the risk of losing 100 class points to Sakayanagi, she maintained her composure, reassuring her classmates and preventing them from panicking.

   * President of the student council (Y2V2-Y2V9): She regulated her natural pride and ego by showing herself willing to bow or kneel before Kushida if that guaranteed long-term benefit for the class and its adherence to the council.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): Once again, Suzune demonstrates remarkable Self Regulation in the face of a new challenge. This exam is designed to generate:
   * Colle

   * Moral conflict (sacrificing teammates)
Even so, Suzune:
   * Maintains a completely rational stance
   * Doesn't make impulsive decisions
   * Shows no emotional bias when assigning protection or targets
This is critical because defense relies on quick and accurate decisions, not emotional ones. The leader has total control:
   * Decides on protection
   * Decides on strategy
   * Accepts final consequences
Suzune demonstrates:
   * Control of the pace of decisions
   * Ability to ignore peer pressure
   * Prioritizes strategic efficiency over popularity
This indicates executive discipline, not just emotional self-control, in an exam that demands:
   * Simultaneous processing (attack + defense + prediction)
   * Probability assessment
   * Constant adjustments
Suzune:
   * Filters irrelevant information
   * Prioritizes critical variables (accumulated errors, targets)
   * Maintains logical consistency in decisions
The system is designed to exploit fear:

   * Elimination → loss of points
   * Last place → expulsion
Even so, Suzune:
   * Does not fall into overprotection bias
   * Does not panic defensively
   * Maintains strategy even with real risk
In the end, Suzune demonstrates:
   * Emotional control → stable
   * Behavioral control → disciplined
   * Cognitive control → optimized

Despite her classmates' distress and the pressure of the moment, she neither broke character nor revealed the deception.

During the confrontation with Ichinose, when the latter began to flawlessly guess the roles, Suzune experienced
   * Chills

Despite the immense pressure and psychological terror, he regulated his behavior perfectly:
   * She showed no outward weakness.
   * She mastered her emotions through self-control.
   * She slapped her cheeks to force herself to maintain composure and not be consumed by panic.
Thanks to this strict self-regulation, she continued to fight resolutely until the end of the exam, wearing down her opponent's life force for the benefit of her class. Previously, she had also demonstrated emotional discipline toward Kanzaki by coldly and firmly rejecting his desperate pleas to surrender, preventing sympathy or guilt from interfering with her performance.

   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-now):  She suppressed her initial inclination towards isolation and her natural pride to adopt a political, mature, and receptive demeanor towards the class, appropriately managing rejections or delicate situations such as Sudou's confession without breaking the team.

Internal conclusion:

   * Does not divert it to someone else.

But:

This indicates:

   * Moderate self-assertion

Suzune:

But also:

Suzune:

She doesn't just participate → she capitalizes on the situation for her personal development

Although this could have caused others to doubt her abilities and see her as an unreliable leader for relinquishing the most important position, Suzune demonstrated great consideration for herself and her true priorities:
   * she set aside her personal pride and accepted the terms in a mere ten seconds.

Heroically adapting her strategy in the face of the overwhelming situation.

Charisma -
Suzune presence commands respect rather than closeness. Her influence is not born of charisma, but of logical consistency and the authority she builds through results. Even if she doesn't stand out for her natural charm, her honesty and determination command respect, and her ability to take the blame publicly enhances her image.

Aura -
Simply by standing at the podium, Suzune brings about immediate silence. Her presence creates tension in every student and a clear sense of inevitability, as if the outcome has already been decided. Her presence generates psychological pressure on allies and rivals. Projecting a firm, serious, and difficult-to-intimidate presence, even in the face of dominant figures like Hōsen and Ryuuen.

Cunning -
   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4):  Transform Ibuki from a risk into an asset, without Ibuki realizing she's being manipulated.
Technique used:
   * Appeal to Ibuki's pride
   * Redefine the conflict
Establish a false dichotomy: fight together or lose your competitive edge. Ibuki believes she's acting of her own free will.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): The best example of cunning in the feat is when Suzune identifies:
   * Ichinose plays it safe (avoiding mistakes)
She exploits this:
   * She causes a buildup of "Reachers"
   * She makes herself vulnerable to Ryuuen
Result: She uses Ryuuen as a tool without directly interacting with her. This failed due to the existence of a traitor in Class A.

Suzune entered the exam burdened with immense accumulated fatigue, severe stress, and a massive accumulation of disadvantages and limitations that made the exam extremely unfair. Despite the minimal margin for error—having only 7 lives compared to her opponents' 22—and the pressure of losing Kiyotaka's future cooperation if she failed, she maintained unwavering emotional discipline. During the duel, her direct rival, Kanzaki, desperate and unable to harm her, humbled himself before her, bowing deeply and repeatedly begging her to concede victory for the sake of their class. Instead of succumbing to pity or panic, Suzune coolly controlled her emotions, rejected the plea as disrespectful to the exam, and ruthlessly executed her strategy. When Kanzaki psychologically gave up and began to pass by apathetically, she didn't get overconfident or careless; she maintained absolute focus and closed the match perfectly, winning 7-0 without receiving a single scratch.

Sacrificial acceptance. Willing to:

Strategic persistence. She continues executing even when plans partially fail.
She shows no hesitation after mistakes → there is no loss of momentum.

   * Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12): Suzune displayed unwavering persistence and strategic counterattacks against Ichinose in the worst possible scenario, fighting without surrendering until the very end. Suzune faced Ichinose under conditions of utter adversity.

   * Suzune quelled the panic.

By diverting Ichinose's attention to his own comrades, he managed to bite through Ichinose's defenses, successfully identifying Hatsutori as the executive role, and fighting tooth and nail until the very last second.

Perseverance -
   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-now): At the beginning of the story, Class D faced the most adverse conditions possible within the ANHS competitive system. They had an absolute total of 0 class points, the worst academic and physical averages, complete social disorganization, and widespread internal divisions.

Suzune's initial response to this hostile scenario was severe isolation and an individualistic approach; however, upon realizing that Class A's objective required group cohesion, she tenaciously restructured her own mindset and assumed the responsibility of leading the group.

Suzune's perseverance in this endeavor is evident in her overcoming multiple structural, psychological, and strategic barriers over time. In the academic and internal management spheres, her persistence was reflected in the implementation of a mandatory tutoring system for the lowest-performing members. Despite facing explicit resistance, a lack of motivation, and constant initial failures from these students, Suzune maintained rigor and discipline without abandoning the project, progressively raising the class's minimum grade from a worrying 14 to a sustainable academic floor above 50 points. Likewise, in managing the human factor, her ability to persevere in the face of Sudou's volatile and impulsive behavior allowed her to transform a destructive element into a key member of the class's top 10 academically and athletically—a process of social rehabilitation that required years of patience, restraint, and constant strategic guidance.

On the tactical front, and in the face of external adversity, Horikita's perseverance was tested after multiple severe setbacks inflicted by figures such as Ryuuen in his early days, Ichinose, Sakayanagi, Hosen, Amasawa, Nanase, or even his own classmates and traitors, as well as the complexities of the school's special exams. Far from collapsing emotionally or abandoning her vision after being outmaneuvered or outmatched in strategic combat, Suzune used each defeat as an analytical tool to correct her cognitive biases, assimilate her weaknesses, and gradually develop genuine operational independence.

This resilience translated into sustained tactical evolution that culminated in the formulation of autonomous counterattacks and victories in her second year.

The most critical point of her psychological and leadership perseverance occurred during the Unanimous Voting Exam. Faced with unforeseen time pressure and the need to make high-stakes decisions, Suzune made the unpopular decision to carry out the expulsion necessary to close the point gap, simultaneously bearing the burden of saving Kushida. Despite the immediate social trauma, the widespread distrust within the group, and the emotional rupture of the class, Suzune persevered, refusing to succumb to the pressure of collective guilt.

Instead of resigning or becoming fragmented, she worked methodically to repair the social fabric, relying on key figures like Hirata and Karuizawa. She successfully restructured trust and reintegrated Kushida as Vice President of the Student Council, thus keeping the course firmly onward toward Class A.

Her focus on the ultimate goal for over two years, climbing from 0 class points to 1,233 points and reaching Class 2-A, while also shouldering the institutional burden of the Student Council Presidency, demonstrates a superior ability to cope with the impact of failure and an unwavering determination to regulate and guide her environment until the objective is achieved.

Perception -
   * Eyes perception/Observation:
   * Detecting changes in attitude, body tension, and risk of escalation during negotiations with Hōsen by reading the environment and its threats
   * Advanced visual reading of the environment and human micro-movements during UIE, memorizes routes and map areas without constant tablet support, detects visual anomalies in Amasawa (posture, fatigue, breathing) and observes foot and shoulder movements to anticipate attacks
   * Handwritten Japanese letters (kanji) have characteristics that change from person to person. where the order of the strokes (hitsu-jun) and pressure are essential. Even if one tries to write poorly or differently, subconscious elements give a writer away, things like: Fluency and speed,  Kanji Structure, Stroke pressure (fude-atsu), Stroke order:

Each of those pieces of data were compared by Suzune to the 105 first-year students she investigated in the treasure hunt game from her position as a prize claimer. so it's something impressive. Especially because of the short time she had to confirm it, after all, Suzune had to attend quickly so that there would be a line of people. And with all that, he was able to confirm that none of them were the culprit. Something true because Yagami worked as part of the council and did not participate. In the novel it is only mentioned that Suzune was looking for a "beautiful letter", but there are many "beautiful letters", so she must have considered many unique details to identify that "beautiful letter" she was looking for. It stands to reason. It is impossible for only one person in 160 students to have good handwriting. And when she found a similar one (Ishigami) she followed him until he confirmed that he was not the culprit. Which is coherent because it was related to WR and Atsuomi and it would be logical that it had something of the style they teach there

So, even if it is not mentioned explicitly, by different means it is implied that Suzune took many factors into account to find the person with "beautiful handwriting" she was looking for.
   * She observed minute details like the electricity meter in Kushida's room turning rapidly to confirm that he was inside using power.
   * She observed the physical infrastructure of the exam venue (the arrangement of the two doors in the classrooms and the waiting corridor) to find the exact moment when students could visually cross paths without breaking the rules of the test.
   * Suzune analyzes the emotional reactions, contrasting Nagumo's composure with Kiriyama's visible panic when the witnesses are mentioned.
   * Suzune quantifies the difficulty of the exam through cumulative observation, identifying success rates that drop drastically from 50% at Level 1 to just 20% at Level 2, finally reaching 10% at Level 3. This analysis allowed her to transform scattered results into an accurate probability model to fine-tune her attack and defense strategy.
   * Suzune simultaneously monitors the interactions of 14 participants in real time through screens, retaining patterns of glances, hesitations in speaking, and group dynamics to extract executive roles.
   * Hear perception:
   * Detecting Hosen's verbal nuances
   * Using tone, pauses, and speech rhythm to read social intentions, Suzune understands changes in tone, accelerated or mocking rhythm, and awkward silences.
   * To solve the case, Suzune pieces together several key elements: first, she notices an unusual scheduling error in the usually precise Kiriyama, which arouses her suspicions. Then, recalling a past conversation between Nagumo and Kiryuuin, she rules out any real rivalry between them, thus eliminating one of her main hypotheses. During the trial.

Knowledge/Academics -
   * Kiyotaka Bet (Y2V1): Suzune and Kiyotaka made a promise to compete against each other to see who could achieve the higher score in a written exam subject. On the day of the exam, the chosen subject was mathematics. Suzune scored 87 points, an impressive result considering that even an A-ranked student like Yukimura barely managed to reach 84 points. The exam was so demanding that the 80-point threshold itself posed a major barrier for most students, regardless of the subject.

After all, approximately 10% of the exam covered concepts beyond the first-year curriculum, not to mention the inherent difficulty of the problems themselves. No one in the class managed to score above 90 in any subject, (except for Kiyotaka) Suzune had given everything she had and was satisfied with her result, especially since she didn’t even understand what the final questions were about—topics such as measure theory and Lebesgue integrals.

   * Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12): As student council president, Suzune demonstrates exceptional retention and understanding of general institutional information. She maintains a perfect grasp of the current situation and structural dynamics of the three school years, including a precise understanding of point distribution and the leadership of first-year classes at the end of the term (such as the isolation of Class 1-A and the impact of Yagami's penalty on Class 1-B). Furthermore, she is able to fluently compare and interpret this data with her own historical performance from a year prior.

Methods -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): Forming alliances, to gain allies based on a long-term relationship of mutual trust, instead of a monetary one that benefits all involved.

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): Controlled decentralization with absolute priority to non-expulsion where its central objective is to ensure that no group in Class 2-D falls into the expulsion zone, even if that sacrifices personal prominence or first places.

   * Handwriting Identification (Y2V4-Y2V8): She applied a structured method of narrowing down suspects from 159 first-year students until she filtered out 66% who participated in the event, then those from the pool, discarding them one by one (such as Kibayashi, Mochidzuki, etc.) until she cornered the remaining 34%.

   * UVE (Y2V5): Create a situation for the public to express themselves and build consensus based on their opinions

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Design a structured procedure to maximize victory through internal class repair (including Kushida's reintegration), contractual formalization with Ryuuen, balanced participation in group events (maximum three per student), balanced distribution of points, and adjustments in individual competitions to avoid friction. Furthermore, she used the new rules imposed by Nagumo (which prioritized individual merit and the event pre-registration system) to design an incentive structure with which she could buy Ibuki's favor and, at the same time, secure individual victories for her class.

   * Suzune Cultural Festival Strategy (Y2V5-Y2V7): A structured system for evaluating and filtering internal proposals. Suzune rejects ideas based solely on enthusiasm and requires a mandatory procedure with detailed costs, projections, margin analysis, and supplier comparisons. She centralizes administration, divides the work into four units, and conducts preliminary tests to correct any flaws.

   * Sudou Integration (Y1V2-Y2V8):  Suzune transforms a troubled student into one of the strongest weapons in the class.

   * Written Comprehensive Cooperative Exam (Y2V9): The creation of the systematic procedure of the "relay system": High OAA student enters ⇒ Locates easy questions ⇒ Exits and gives the hand signal ⇒ Low OAA student enters ⇒ Solves high direct value questions.

   * President of the student council (Y2V2-Y2V9): Suzune builds a highly profitable influence based on consistent results and the strategic reduction of errors. Her leadership is not imposed through authority or popularity, but rather evolves through progressive external validation; she uses academic achievements and the management of critical moments to gain legitimacy without resorting to constant manipulation or social dominance. This structure allows her to expand her impact from the individual to the systemic level, creating a stable and relatively stable power base that withstands pressure and prevents leadership collapse because it is grounded in operational effectiveness rather than emotional appeal.

   * Kiryuuin Trial (Y2V9): Suzune followed a clear structure throughout the trial. First, she reconstructed the events through a compilation of testimonies and logical analysis. With this, she corroborated a hypothesis that she then confirmed through pressure.

   * Koenji Neutralization Strategy (Y2V10): Suzune employs behavioral engineering on Kouenji, using a controlled pressure technique by deliberately leaving him vulnerable to the enemy. By bringing him to the brink of elimination, she forces him to unleash his true abilities to survive, simultaneously shifting Sakayanagi's strategic perception, who ceases his attacks upon recognizing him as an ineffective target.

   * Five Reachers strategy (Y2V10): Suzune executes a strategic strangulation by limiting her targets to exactly five "reachers," blocking the expansion of the attack front to saturate Ichinose's defense. This procedure reduces the opponent's defensive flexibility, forcing her into a cycle of spending protections on the same individuals and trapping her in a moral dilemma where her value system becomes her greatest tactical weakness.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10):

   * Control and containment against Kanzaki (Y2V12): Suzune devised a meticulous procedure to strategically neutralize Kanzaki using an initial lineup composed of an "elite group" of students chosen for their composure, cunning, and expressionless faces. Her specific objective was to dominate the flow of the first debate and force Kanzaki into a purely defensive stance. By executing this plan, she managed to psychologically pressure her opponent, emotionally discipline her pleas for surrender, and close the match with a perfect 7-0 victory without conceding a single point of damage.

   * Distraction tactic against Ichinose (Y2V12): Finding himself at a disadvantage against Ichinose, Suzune employed a method of psychological deception by planting the idea that executive roles should be distributed equally between both classes for the sake of exam balance. This strategic move forced Ichinose to shift his focus and narrow his field of vision toward his own classmates, giving Horikita the necessary time to counterattack and successfully identify Hatsutori as the executive role holder.

Deduction -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): Suzune was able to accurately deduce all the nuances of the exam and how each of the other leaders, both second-year and first-year students, were going to behave.

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): The interpretation of the article: “Noon – K.A. – Expelled – I2”; has a single explanation that generates a coherent proposition: “If I do not act, Ayanokōji Kiyotaka will be expelled today, at noon, in I2.”  However, her most important feat in the exam was when she deductively reasoned that if Class 2-D attempted to form mixed groups with other classes without guarantees, the difference in split points (when dividing prizes between classes) would reduce the actual impact of the Class Points earned, concluding that victory should be monopolized internally or maximized by a solo player.

   * Handwriting Identification (Y2V4-Y2V8): She deduced that the culprit must be a first-year student (no one from 2nd or 3rd year knew that dynamic/handwriting), that he must have high physical capacity (to infiltrate her store), that he belonged to the 34% who did not compete in the treasure event and Love letter strategy.

   * Suzune Cultural Festival Strategy (Y2V5-Y2V7): When Sakayanagi tried to sow distrust by implying that Ryuuen would betray her, Suzune deduced the intentions behind Sakayanagi's speech and maintained the facade that her alliance with Ryuuen had been a one-time event, ensuring that Sakayanagi did not discover the ongoing ploy.

   * Written Comprehensive Cooperative Exam (Y2V9): Suzune analyzed the rules on absences (which randomly selected unanswered questions) and logically concluded that trying to make an opponent absent intentionally did not grant any real tactical advantage, leading her to discard external sabotage strategies and focus solely on internal optimization of her class.

   * President of the student council (Y2V2-Y2V9): She accurately deduced that the 20 million reward for expelling Kiyotaka did not come from the first-years, but from an upper-year student with enough status to give them confidence, Nagumo Miyabi.

   * Kiryuuin Trial (Y2V9): Suzune deduces that Kiriyama is the true mastermind.

   * Koenji Neutralization Strategy (Y2V10): To deduce that Sakayanagi somehow knew about her pact with Koenji and Process where the conclusion is logically derived from the available premises.
   * P1: Kouenji won't let his expulsion depend on others.
   * P2: If I don't protect him and he makes two mistakes, a third mistake puts him at real risk if the class loses.
   * C1: Kouenji will logically be forced to perform well on his own.

   * Five Reachers strategy (Y2V10): A process where the conclusion is logically derived from the available premises.
   * P1: Ichinose will protect any student with 2 failing grades.
   * P2: There are 5 protection slots, and exactly 5 students with 2 failing grades are created.
   * C1: 100% of Ichinose's protection capacity will be frozen and useless for the rest of the class.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): To deduce that Ichinose and Ryuuen formed an alliance. Furthermore, with Kiyotaka's help, she correctly deduced that Sakayanagi was aware of the private agreement with Kouenji. By evaluating the anomalous patterns in the Class A nomination turns (pointing at Kouenji three times in a row and then completely ignoring him after he answered correctly), she accurately concluded that there had been an internal leak of information.

   * Kanzaki Control (Y2V12):  She deduces that if Miyake was acting aggressively to expose a participant, he was not doing so for the good of the class (given the selfish nature of his group), but to secure private points as an Honor Student, leading her to the unequivocal conclusion to nominate him.

   * Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12): She deduces that the school would not reveal a purely academic or physical exam in the Special Building without notice, correctly inferring that the exam would require dialogue, observation, and negotiation skills.

Improvisation -
   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4):  The instant formation of a group with Ibuki DURING the fight against Amasawa Ichika. Suzune faces simultaneously:
   * Amasawa Ichika → overwhelming physical threat
   * Two weeks of accumulated fatigue
   * Last day of the exam → no margin for error
Real risk of:
   * Triggering a medical alert
   * Withdrawing from the exam
   * Being expelled
   * Unforeseen appearance of Ibuki → chaotic variable
   * None of this was considered in her pre-visualization of the battle
The critical problem is, if Suzune fights alone:
   * She can lose → expulsion
   * She can win but be injured → expulsion
   * She can trigger an alert → automatic withdrawal
If Ibuki fights alone:
   * Similar outcome
   * Any normal outcome = failure to save Kiyotaka and the exam
   * Suzune creates a solution that didn't exist before the fight:
   * Forming a group with Ibuki in the middle of the confrontation
   * This instantly changes the framework of the problem and alters the system
Before:
   * Withdrawal = immediate expulsion
   * Physical damage = end of the Exam
   * Combat = all or nothing
Afterwards:
   * If one falls → the other keeps the group together
   * The risk of expulsion is neutralized
   * Combat ceases to be binary
   * You can fight to wear down the opponent, not to win quickly
Why is this NOT a pre-arranged strategy?
   * Suzune didn't plan to fight
   * She didn't plan to team up with Ibuki
   * She didn't plan to face Amasawa
   * She didn't plan to sacrifice points or routes
The idea only arises when she realizes: "If we continue like this, we both lose." That's genuine improvisation that has an immediate effect. Thanks to this decision:
   * She avoids expulsion
   * She maintains her ranking
   * She buys time
   * She forces Amasawa to expend stamina
   * She turns a certain defeat into a recoverable situation
   * Even if Suzune doesn't win the match, she doesn't lose the exam.

   * UVE (Y2V5): Instant restructuring of strategy in the face of a collapsed consensus. During the critical phase of the exam:
   * The initial implicit plan was to maintain stability and avoid an irreversible fracture.
   * The voting dynamic stalls.
   * The real possibility of losing 350 points arises.
   * The group's emotional pressure escalates.
At that moment, the key decision was not part of a pre-established script. There was no "definitive Plan A" that addressed that specific social scenario. Improvisation occurs when:
   * The initial position is abandoned.
   * Priorities are reorganized in real time.
   * An alternative solution is formulated that balances:
   * Class points.
   * Internal cohesion.
   * Future leadership.
   * Reputation management.
   * This reconfiguration was not entirely premeditated.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Real-time adjustments during the volleyball match against Amasawa. When she notices Amasawa improving, Suzune:
   * Recalibrates expectations.
   * Modifies the tactical approach.
   * Maintains emotional control.
   * Adjusts pace and game reading.

Furthermore, she concludes that the author of the note must have been a gifted first-year student and that the note was not written by Amasawa, since she destroyed it to avoid leaving any handwriting evidence. And in the end, based on observations and incomplete information, she concluded that the most probable and logical hypothesis was that Yagami was the author and the conspirator behind the message.

   * UVE (Y2V5): The stubborn vote in favor of expulsion does not stem from an emotional impulse, a mistake, or empty malevolence, but from a cold strategic calculation where the cost of destroying group cohesion is less than the personal benefit the voter seeks to obtain. Based on anonymous voting patterns in motion 5, defensive behavior, and small inconsistencies in Kushida's conduct, Suzune deduced the presence of the persistent pro-voter and their destructive motivation before public evidence was exposed.

Similarly, she infers the messy state of Ibuki's room by visually comparing it to the impeccable order of Kushida's. Furthermore, during the volleyball match against Amasawa and Nanase, Suzune observed Amasawa's anomalous learning rate in less than 10 minutes. She quickly abduced that it wasn't just physical fitness, but an overwhelming cognitive adaptability, adjusting the team's tactics to win by time limit by exploiting Amasawa's service errors.

Furthermore, when analyzing the demographic data of the 485 guests (age distribution, adult budget of 10,000 pts vs. under 5,000 pts), she concluded that adults in their 30s and 40s represented the largest source of income, justifying the high viability of the Maid Café to capture that market segment.

Therefore, she assumed that the correct hypothesis for winning was to prioritize accuracy in point value over speed.

   * Kiryuuin Trial (Y2V9): Since Nagumo denied the order, and Yamanaka/Tachibana claimed to have received it from Nagumo, there was a direct contradiction. The only person with that level of trust and spokesperson power on the council was Kiriyama.
