Non Verbal Deception -
   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Expressive and Body Control During Negotiations with Ryuuen at the Sports Festival. Suzune was not in a dominant position. Her class was marked by internal fractures, accumulated tension from UVE, and structural vulnerability. Facing her was Ryuuen, an opponent who bases his tactical approach on psychological intimidation, microexpressions, and direct pressure. In that scenario, any nonverbal sign of insecurity would have altered the balance of power even before the verbal negotiation began. Suzune maintains a sustained neutral expression. There are no visible micro-reactions to provocations. This serves three strategic functions:
   * It prevents Ryuuen from identifying pressure points.
   * It projects cognitive stability.
   * It reduces opportunities for psychological escalation.
The neutrality here is not passivity; it is a deliberate concealment of internal processing. She remains upright, steady, without physically retreating in the face of invasions of personal space. In terms of power dynamics:
   * Don't lean back → avoids signaling defensiveness.
   * Don't lean excessively forward → avoids signaling neediness.
   * Maintain a stable vertical axis → communicates hierarchical equality.
Ryuuen expected dominance and received symmetry with an absence of nervous movements:
   * No hand gestures.
   * No unnecessary shifting.
   * No abrupt changes in breathing.
Suzune's body conveys internal coherence, even if she is internally calculating risks. This creates a dangerous perception for the opponent that she is already in control. With this, Suzune wins:
   * Ryuuen fails to identify exploitable emotional weaknesses.
   * The negotiation shifts to the rational plane.
   * The terrain where Ryuuen usually dominates—intimidation—is eliminated.
Furthermore, during negotiations with Ibuki regarding the three matches of the festival, Suzune feigned reluctance and apathy so that Ibuki would be the one to propose and insist on competing three times. In reality, three matches was precisely the odd number that Suzune had planned from the beginning to avoid ties.

   * Written Comprehensive Cooperative Exam (Y2V9): She designed a manual sign language that operated under the radar of the exam. High OAA students (like Yukimura) would scan the 100 questions and, through hand gestures in the hallway, indicate exactly which question number the next low OAA student should answer.

   * Koenji Neutralization Strategy (Y2V10): Not protecting Kouenji when he is at risk of elimination. Situation:
   * Kouenji makes two mistakes → risk of elimination
   * The class expects Suzune to protect him
Action:
   * Suzune does NOT protect him
   * Maintains a firm stance, without explanation
   * Shows no doubt or tension
1. Deception of her class
   * Perception generated:
   * “She made a mistake”
   * “She’s being irrational”
   * Reality:
   * She is performing a psychological calculation on Kouenji
2. Deception of Kouenji
   * Implicit signal:
   * “I’m not going to save you”
   * Partial result:
   * Kouenji acts seriously and responds well (He realizes it)
3. Deception of Sakayanagi
   * External signal:
   * Kouenji = vulnerable target
   * Objective:
   * To encourage attacks against him
   * Partial result:
   * Sakayanagi detects the deception (high-level resistance)
The most affected were the members of his class, who maintained composure in the face of the class’s suspicion
   * She does not explain her decision
   * She does not attempt Justifying themselves
   * Maintains emotional control
This reinforces the deception because:
   * It prevents revealing the strategy
   * It keeps the false narrative active

Mass Deception -

By showing average first-year students that others were receiving tempting offers, she inflated their expectations, forcing Sakayanagi and Ryūen to spend more private points than planned, even on ordinary students, thus eroding their rivals' finances.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): She managed to repeatedly deceive the 38 students. She diverted the attention of the entire class after the revelation of the perfect grade in math, transforming the collective suspicion of possible "fraud" into an accepted narrative of collective strategy.

She tricked all participating students into falling into her trap to eliminate any possibilities.

The entire school believed that:
   * There was a real rift.

Personal ranking for the best Acting Skills feats:

Bluffing -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): Suzune approached three first-year students—Kaga, Mikami, and Shiratori. All three were from Class 1-D and possessed Academic Ability ratings of B− or higher. Before speaking with them, Suzune first checked their profiles on the OAA application, just to be safe.

When Suzune asked whether any of them would be willing to partner up, they hesitated—not only because no one was eager to pair with someone rated E+ in Academic Ability, but also because they wanted compensation for doing so. More specifically, they demanded 500,000 private points.

Suzune, who lacked the capital for Class 2-D to pay 500,000 points per person, instead attempted to negotiate. However, this approach failed. Before Suzune arrived, other second-year students had already advised them not to sell their academic ability cheaply.

Suzune then questioned whether those students had truly been approached, deliberately invoking their status as Class 1-D and its poor reputation. This was Suzune’s deception. In reality, a high Academic Ability rating would be valuable in this exam regardless of one’s class. She had said this specifically to confirm who had contacted them and what had been offered.

The deception worked. They revealed that they had been approached by Hashimoto from Class 2-A and Ryūen from Class 2-C, both offering large sums of private points. Moreover, it was not just them—all academically strong students had been contacted.

   * Kiyotaka Bet (Y1V11.5-Y2V2): Suzune acts as if she genuinely believes she can defeat Kiyotaka academically. But the narrative indicates that:
   * she suspects he is far more capable.
   * she knows he is holding back.
Therefore, her confidence in claiming she can beat him functions partly as a psychological bluff. The bluff serves to:
   * intensify the provocation
   * make the challenge believable.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): The strategic bluff during the negotiation with Ryuuen Kakeru, where Suzune projects tactical independence and a genuine willingness to break the agreement, even though cooperation was highly advantageous for her class. At the Sports Festival, the balance of power clearly did not favor Suzune's class. Ryuuen operated from an aggressive position, accustomed to dominating negotiations through psychological pressure. Suzune faced three disadvantages:
   * Ryuuen's history of manipulation.
   * Katsuragi's intelligence.
   * Recent internal instability following the UVE incident.
The objective need for inter-class cooperation. In other words, breaking the agreement had real costs. Suzune doesn't exaggerate nonexistent power or invent hidden resources; she merely feigns strategic indifference. She projects that:
   * She can win without Ryuuen's class.
   * She is not structurally dependent on Ryuuen.
   * She is willing to take risks.
The implicit message is:
   * "We cooperate because it's rational, not because we need to."
When in reality, cooperation significantly increased her chances of success. An effective bluff works when the opponent believes that:
   * The cost of challenging you could be high.
   * Your risk tolerance is genuine.
   * You are not emotionally pressured.
Suzune achieves this through:
   * Stable body language.
   * Controlled verbal pacing.
   * A complete absence of urgency.
Strategic silence plays a key role. It doesn't fill in the gaps; it lets Ryuuen and Katsuragi assess adverse scenarios. The bluff isn't based on exaggerated words. It's based on behavioral credibility. The bluff carried real danger:
   * If Ryuuen decided to test his resolve, Suzune would have had to compete without the advantage of the agreement.
That means the bluff wasn't empty, but rather required mental preparation to sustain the consequence. It wasn't an outright lie. It's a controlled exaggeration of self-assurance. In the end, Ryuuen chooses to accept working terms. That indicates that:
   * He perceived the risk as credible.
   * He didn't detect desperation.
   * He assessed that breaking the deal didn't give him a clear advantage. The bluff achieved its objective without being exposed.

   * Kiryuuin Trial (Y2V9):  Suzune had no physical evidence against Kiriyama (she adhered to her philosophy that there is no truth without evidence, but she needed to break through the defendant's defenses). She declared that, using her authority as president, she would immediately summon Yamanaka and Tachibana to the courtroom as witnesses. Knowing that bringing in actual witnesses would require a longer bureaucratic process, she used this empty threat as direct bait to make Kiriyama panic and confess.

   * Koenji Neutralization Strategy (Y2V10): She made a public bluff by not putting Kouenji on the protected list. She pretended she was willing to drop him or that she had complete control of the situation without using up a slot, forcing Kouenji to react under the pressure of the 1% uncertainty.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): By standing on the podium during the second half crisis (with 4 eliminated in the class and the unstoppable advance of Ichinose and Ryuuen), She feigned absolute composure and control in front of Ichinose as their lives were plummeting, hiding her racing heartbeat in an attempt to destabilize Honami's confidence.

Strategic Deception -
   * Kiyotaka Bet (Y1V11.5-Y2V2): Suzune presents the bet as if it were:
   * a personal challenge
   * a test to measure who is academically superior
   * an act motivated by pride and competition
But the real objective is different:
   * To force Kiyotaka to reveal his true level.
   * To create public evidence of his talent.
   * To construct a narrative for the class.
   * To free him from his constraints.
This means that the challenge is not what it appears to be. It is a strategic instrument where even if Suzune loses the bet, the plan works because:
   * Kiyotaka will have to prove his ability.
   * The class will see that his extraordinary performance was no fluke.
   * This creates a "guaranteed win" structure.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Hide Kushida's true personality from the other classes and ensure that no one divulges it in order to maximize Kushida's abilities. Furthermore, during negotiations with Ibuki, Suzune feigned reluctance and a negative attitude when Ibuki proposed three individual duels during the festival. Suzune knew that if she proposed three, Ibuki would demand five; therefore, she manipulated the conversation so that Ibuki herself would propose the exact number of duels Horikita had wanted from the start.

   * Suzune Cultural Festival Strategy (Y2V5-Y2V7): She orchestrated a "fake rivalry" (Maid Café vs. Kimono Café) with Kiyotaka and Ryuuen, using a fictitious 5 million private point contract. This smokescreen deceived rivals like Sakayanagi, attracted massive customer attention (early marketing), and discouraged others from competing in the same market for years to come.

   * Kiryuuin Trial (Y2V9): During the discussion, Suzune acts as if she is:
   * investigating the case from scratch
   * gradually uncovering information
   * evaluating all possibilities.
However, Suzune had already deduced that Kiriyama was the likely culprit before the trial. This means that the questioning wasn't really an investigation. It was a deliberate reconstruction to guide the group's thinking. Suzune asks questions that appear neutral, but in reality, they steer the group's logic toward a single conclusion. In other words, the participants believe they are reasoning freely, but in reality, Suzune controls the group's logical path. During the trial:
   * she doesn't directly accuse Kiriyama
   * she avoids confronting him early
   * she maintains the appearance of impartiality.
This serves two purposes:
   * it prevents Kiriyama from becoming defensive too soon
   * it allows him to participate in the reasoning that incriminates him.
Suzune doesn't lie outright. But she conceals her true conclusion until the opportune moment. The clearest instance of strategic deception occurs when Suzune announces she will call Yamanaka and Tachibana as witnesses. Context:
   * Everyone knows Kiriyama is the likely culprit. However, she has no direct evidence.
   * Nor can she prove that the testimonies obtained by Kiyotaka are entirely reliable.
At that point, Suzune announces:
   * that she will call the witnesses
   * that they will confirm what happened.
The logic behind the deception works as follows:
   1. Kiriyama believes the witnesses will be called.
   2. If that happens, they might reveal dangerous information.
   3. Kiriyama panics.
   4. He decides to confess before the witnesses appear.
Suzune creates a fictitious information threat. The threat doesn't need to be carried out; it only needs to be believable.

   * Koenji Neutralization Strategy (Y2V10): It's not just deception—it's structural deception within a dynamic strategy. Breakdown:
   * Suzune creates a real strategy (eliminating Kouenji early)
But:
   * She doesn't reveal it.
   * She acts contradictorily (not protecting him).
She creates multiple layers of deception:
   * To her class:
   * She appears incompetent/irresponsible.
   * She hides the true objective.
   * To Kouenji:
   * She forces him to act without directly telling him.
   * To Sakayanagi:
   * She presents Kouenji as a vulnerable target.
   * She tries to influence the enemy's decisions.

   * Five Reachers strategy (Y2V10): She deceived the entire board about the true purpose of her attacks. She allowed Ichinose to block her five "Reachers" by feigning an offensive failure, when in reality she was achieving her true objective.

Negotiation -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): Suzune negotiates from a structurally inferior position:
   * Class 2-D
   * Lowest number of points
   * Real risk of disqualification
   * Opponent (Hōsen) with:
   * Coercive control of their class
   * Informational advantage (covert politics)
   * Willingness to use violence
   * Lack of immediate institutional support
Suzune isn't trying to win points or dominate Class 1-D. Her goals are:
   * Avoid immediate expulsions
   * Create a sustainable, long-term relationship for future exams
   * Avoid dependence on private points
Suzune proposes a deferred symmetrical cooperation alliance, based on:
   * Point-free pairings
   * Cross-academic support
   * Structural equality
   * Guaranteed future benefit
   * Trust as an asset
Suzune understands and identifies the present problem; she does not negotiate with Hōsen as an individual, but with the system he is creating.
   * The problem isn't this exam.
   * The problem is the individualistic culture imposed by Hōsen. That culture:
   * It will fragment class 1-D.
   * It will increase future expulsions.
   * It will reduce the ability to work together in group exams.
During the negotiation, Suzune:
   * Refuses to pay private points
   * Rejects coercive contracts
   * Rejects leadership imposed by Nanase if it divides the class
   * Maintains the option to accept sanctions
   * Keeps the door open to renegotiation
   * Does not burn bridges
Suzune does not give in, even under physical threat; Suzune correctly identifies that:
   * Hōsen doesn't negotiate in good faith.
   * His demand for points isn't about money, it's about control.
   * His violence is a tool, not an emotion.
   * If he agrees once, the cost will escalate.
Although the conflict ends with external intervention (Kiyotaka), Suzune had already won the negotiation before that. Why?
   * She did not surrender points.
   * She did not sign invalid agreements.
   * She did not legitimize Hōsen's system.
   * She did not fracture class 1-D.
   * She preserved moral and strategic capital.
After the conflict:
   * A relationship is formed between classes.
   * Expulsions are avoided.
   * Points are retained.
   * Future cooperation is established.
   * Class 2-D survives.
In summary, she established a negotiation framework with Nanase and Class 1-D based on mutual benefit and future protection rather than immediate cash transfers, managing to save 15 struggling students through mutual concessions.

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): Suzune restructured Kōenji's original proposal. When he offered simply to "do well" in exchange for freedom, she raised the bar, demanding absolute first place or, failing that, the obligation to cooperate in all future exams. She secured a favorable deal without relinquishing the protection of Kōenji's Protection Points should he fail. This had a second layer: Suzune promised to protect him if he won, but if Kōenji won, he would gain a protection point. This protection point was something Suzune wasn't obligated to protect and could use to avoid expulsions, as seen in her Y2V10 strategy.

   * UVE (Y2V5): She successfully negotiated with Koenji on motion 5 to neutralize his continued "yes" vote. She offered him a monthly payment of 10,000 private points until graduation in exchange for his "no" vote, thus removing a critical obstacle in the exam.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Suzune was negotiating from an objectively weaker position:
   * A class recently fractured by UVE.
   * Internal tensions still simmering.
   * An urgent need for competitive stability.
   * Ryuuen with a history of aggressive tactics and direct manipulation.
In negotiation theory, this is classified as perceived power asymmetry. The strongest point of her feat wasn't the proposal itself, but the framework she imposed. She didn't present herself as the needy party; she framed the agreement as:
   * “Mutually efficient strategic cooperation.”
This eliminates the narrative of:
   * “I need you.”
and replaces it with:
   * “This is also in your best interest.”
In advanced negotiation, whoever controls the framework controls the perception of value. Ryuuen operates under intimidation and dominance. Suzune neutralizes this through:
   * Stable body posture.
   * Emotionally uncharged responses.
   * Constant verbal rhythm.
   * Lack of reaction to provocations.
In negotiation, this is called resistance to intimidation anchoring. By not reacting, she denies Ryuuen the psychological advantage. This shifts the interaction from social dominance to structural logic. Suzune projects that she has a viable alternative if the agreement fails. Although her class wasn't in an optimal position, she implicitly communicates:
   * "We can compete without you."
Suzune makes her alternative seem sufficient to avoid dependence. This reduces Ryuuen's leverage and balances the concessions:
   * She doesn't grant a structural advantage.
   * She doesn't reveal critical information.
   * She doesn't concede control.
   * She doesn't allow unilateral terms.
   * Cooperation is limited and functional.
With this, she achieves:
   * Stability during the event.
   * Reduced risk of sabotage.
   * Improved competitive performance.
   * Reinforced internal leadership image.
Furthermore, before that, she displays great negotiation skills by convincing Ibuki to be her instrument to break Kushida's isolation in exchange for giving her 3 direct confrontations at the festival.

   * President of the student council (Y2V2-Y2V9): She negotiated with Kushida by offering her a power exchange (joining the council to become publicly "untouchable" and acting as an influential vice president), striking a deal that benefited Suzune by monopolizing control of the council for her class.

   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-Now): She successfully negotiated a pact with Ayanokoji for the protection of her "peaceful life" in exchange for strategic cooperation until she reached Class A.

Personal ranking for the best Negotiation feats:

Concealment -
   * Kiyotaka Bet (Y1V11.5-Y2V2): Suzune conceals her true strategic intentions. Outwardly, she displays:
   * academic pride
   * curiosity
   * a desire to prove her abilities.
Her true aim:
   * to strategically free Kiyotaka
   * to strengthen the class
   * to create a public precedent.
This discrepancy between her stated motivation and her actual objective constitutes concealment.

   * Handwriting Identification (Y2V4-Y2V8): She kept her search for Yagami a complete secret, controlling her and Ibuki's information and movements, hiding it from Kiyotaka, Nagumo, the school, and the mastermind herself.

   * Suzune Cultural Festival Strategy (Y2V5-Y2V7): She concealed the true intentions of the integrated business model (the outdoor food stalls sold half-price tickets that served as advertising bait for the indoor Maid Café) until the structure was assembled and immovable.

   * President of the student council (Y2V2-Y2V9): She hid from Nagumo that her real reason for joining the council was to fulfill a lost bet with Kiyotaka and act as his spy, covering up this truth behind the narrative of "wanting to follow in her brother Manabu's footsteps".

   * Kiryuuin Trial (Y2V9): Suzune concealed the fact that she had already solved the case before taking the presidential chair. She deliberately hid her true conclusions while posing "innocent" questions to Kiriyama, Kushida, and Nagumo, preventing the culprit from realizing he was being cornered until it was too late.

   * Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12):  To prevent Kiyotaka's harsh conditions from affecting class morale or being discovered by other classrooms, Suzune took multiple immediate precautions to keep the terms a complete secret. She planned the final lineup to maximize Kiyotaka's status as a surprise factor against rival classes. However, even when the traitor revealed the secret, rival classes were still uncertain about its veracity due to Suzune's concealment.

Personal ranking for the best Concealment feats:

Information Control -
   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): She surgically managed the information about Ayanokoji's true talent over the course of two years. She deliberately concealed his abilities at first and then controlled the public narrative when Kiyotaka scored a 100 in math, preventing panic from destabilizing the classroom.

   * Kiyotaka Bet (Y1V11.5-Y2V2): This is one of the most important aspects of the feat. Suzune controls how information is interpreted within the class. When Kiyotaka scores extremely high in math in Year 2, Volume 2, Suzune guides the conversation toward a specific explanation:
   * Kiyotaka has great hidden potential.
   * He is a hidden weapon that must be protected.
   * It gives meaning to his future performance.
This prevents suspicions from arising, such as:
   * fraud
   * external help
   * unexplained anomalies.
She creates a coherent narrative:
   * “Ayanokouji-kun always had talent, but he hid it.”
This is group perception control.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Hiding the rifts caused by Sakura's expulsion from UVE. The class faced:
   * Internal mistrust.
   * Risk of sabotage.
   * Emotional fragmentation.
   * Potential exposure of Kushida to other classes.
In that scenario, information is not neutral; it multiplies chaos if it circulates unchecked. But in the end, Kushida remained a hidden asset, and the rifts in the class caused by the exam were concealed. Therefore, along with Hirata and Matsushita, she coordinates the control of information and the containment of internal rumors to prevent the scandal from spreading to other classes before the event.

   * Suzune Cultural Festival Strategy (Y2V5-Y2V7): Suzune manages the flow of information not only by protecting it, but by orchestrating its strategic disclosure to control the overall narrative of the school and manipulate the actions of enemy classes.
   * She accepts and executes the strategy of simulating a public "betrayal" and breach of confidence by Ryuuen regarding the opening of the Maid Café. Rather than it being an oversight, Suzune allows the information about the Maid Café to leak at the exact moment the location and concept are finalized, transforming the leak into a massive school-wide marketing campaign.
   * She uses the fabricated "Maid Café vs. Kimono Café" conflict and the supposed 5 million private point wager to dominate visitors' attention and block competitors from other years or classes. By simulating a real rivalry, she manipulates the perceptions of outside observers and her own classmates.
   * She strictly controls the information that the deal with Ryuuen and Katsuragi is still active, keeping it a complete secret even from her closest allies and from Sakayanagi to prevent them from tracking the true dynamics of the agreement.

   * Written Comprehensive Cooperative Exam (Y2V9): She kept the exam rotation order and the hand-sign method strictly secret within the classroom, handing the order list to Sae Chabashira the night before to prevent Sakayanagi Arisu from anticipating the internal question-filtering method

   * Kiryuuin Trial (Y2V9): Before the trial, Suzune already possessed key information obtained indirectly through Kiyotaka. This information included:
   * the structure of Nagumo's contract with the third-year students
   * the chain of intermediaries in the operation (Nagumo → Tachibana → Yamanaka)
   * the third-year students' emotional state toward Nagumo
However, Suzune quickly discerns which information is truly useful. She herself dismisses two elements:
   * Tachibana's existence as an intermediary
   * the fact that Yamanaka and Tachibana blame Nagumo
Because these pieces do not resolve the central contradiction of the case. Instead, she identifies what is truly valuable:
   * the system of control Nagumo exerts over the third-year students
   * the accumulated resentment against Nagumo
This allows her to deduce the possible political motive of an inside conspirator. After filtering out the relevant from the irrelevant, she doesn't immediately reveal her main hypothesis. Although she already suspects Kiriyama before the trial, she decides not to announce it. Reason:
   * She doesn't possess direct evidence.
   * Revealing the suspicion prematurely would allow Kiriyama to prepare or deny everything.
Therefore, Suzune conducts the trial as a process of logical reconstruction. During the meeting:
   * She asks neutral questions.
   * She allows others to present their theories.
   * She keeps her conclusion secret.
This approach creates information asymmetry where:
   * Suzune -> suspects the culprit.
   * Kiriyama -> believes Suzune doesn't suspect anything.
   * Other members -> are completely unaware of the culprit.
This asymmetry is key to eliciting a confession. Here, she controls when and how information is introduced into the trial. The information is revealed gradually:
   * Kiryuuin's testimony
   * Nagumo's denial
   * Kiriyama's opinion
   * Kushida's analysis
   * Information obtained by Kiyotaka
   * Nanase's intervention
Each step progressively narrows the range of possibilities. This has two effects:
   * It guides the group's reasoning
   * It prevents the culprit from easily changing their narrative.
The flow of information is carefully regulated to logically confine Kiriyama. After resolving the case, Suzune decides to order that the incident not be revealed to the outside world. This has several future implications:
   * Institutional protection
   * It prevents the student council from losing credibility.
   * Protection of individuals
   * It prevents severe disciplinary consequences for:
   * Yamanaka
   * Tachibana
   * Kiriyama.
   * Political control
   * It prevents other students from exploiting the incident.
By keeping the case within the council, Suzune completely centralizes the information. In short, she meticulously doled out the information Kiyotaka gave her about the third-year contracts and the intermediaries (Tachibana/Yamanaka). Instead of revealing all the information at once, she unveiled it in stages to guide everyone in the room toward the conclusion that suited her.

   * Five Reachers strategy (Y2V10): She controlled the distribution of data on the game board. She concealed her true intention (which was not to eliminate people from Ichinose immediately, but to exhaust their resources and weaken the rest of the class) by regulating the exact number of attacks per student.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): Suzune gathers and organizes all the information about her class's weaknesses and throughout the exam recognizes the weaknesses of members of other classes. Furthermore, Suzune implemented strict operational security measures (prohibiting the unrestricted use of cell phones in public areas and avoiding strategic discussions in hallways) to prevent leaks. She also managed the data on weaknesses confidentially provided by Kushida as a vital strategic resource during critical shifts.

   * Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12): Being at a critical disadvantage and realizing that Ichinose possessed a perfect understanding of her classmates' habits, Suzune took control of the exam narrative. Using the ANHS's logic of fairness, Suzune sowed doubt in Ichinose by asserting that "executive roles" should be distributed equally (1:1) between both classes and that, therefore, there must be hidden executives among the students in Suzune's Class B.

   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-Now): She established strict protocols to prevent information leaks in her class and managed the secrecy surrounding Ayanokoji's true level so as not to destabilize his life or generate panic in the class.
Persuasion -
   * Kiyotaka Bet (Y1V11.5-Y2V2): Suzune gets Kiyotaka to accept a bet that:
   * is not strategically advantageous for him
   * forces him to reveal skills he's trying to hide.
The persuasion method she uses is psychological provocation. She challenges him indirectly:
   * questions his ambition to reach Class A
   * questions his achievements
   * reduces his victories
This puts pressure on his identity. The implication is:
   * "If you're really as capable as you say, prove it."
This type of provocation activates pride and competitiveness. Kiyotaka's acceptance is a direct consequence of that pressure.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): She convinced a skeptical class that Kiyotaka should be treated as a "strategic enigma" and a "shield" for the classroom, getting them to accept the presence of a student of unknown abilities among them.

   * UVE (Y2V5): She managed to persuade the class on multiple motions (e.g., on motion 4, convincing them to increase penalties) and, at the end of the exam, managed to redirect the class narrative to accept Kushida's permanence despite the widespread hatred.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Convincing the class to accept Kushida’s strategic reintegration after the internal rift caused by UVE was a daunting task. Suzune wasn't persuading in a neutral environment. The situation included:
   * Accumulated mistrust.
   * Emotional fragmentation.
   * Risk of internal sabotage.
   * External pressure from the special exam.
The class didn't just doubt Kushida; they doubted the group's structural stability. Persuading in this context meant simultaneously modifying emotions, perceptions, and rational calculation. The problem wasn't logical; it was emotional. Expelling Kushida seemed the most intuitive solution; reintegration seemed an unnecessary risk. Suzune had to overcome:
   * Resentment.
   * Fear of future betrayal.
   * Desire for punishment.
   * Collective defensive instinct.
   * The resistance was widespread and visceral.
Suzune applied a structural persuasion based on three axes:
   * A) Reframing the Problem
   * She didn't present the decision as moral forgiveness. She presented it as strategic optimization. Change the question from:
   * “Does she deserve to stay?”
   * To
   * “Is she functional for winning?”
   * B) Reduction of Perceived Risk
   * She didn't ask for absolute trust. She implemented implicit conditions of control. The underlying message was:
   * “We're not blindly trusting; we're managing the risk.”
   * This reduces cognitive resistance.
   * C) Projected Rational Authority
   * Suzune spoke with firmness and internal consistency. Conviction reduces the perception of uncertainty. In group dynamics, the leader's confidence directly influences collective acceptance.
This persuasion was complex because:
   * It required reversing implicit consensus.
   * It involved Suzune's personal reputation.
   * It had a high cost if it failed.
If Kushida didn't act as Suzune wished, the responsibility would fall on Suzune, and her career as a leader would be over, so the political risk was high. In the end, Suzune obtained:
   * A stabilized class.
   * Recovered social capital.
   * A neutralized internal threat.
   * Strengthened leadership image.
   * Cohesive structure for the Sports Festival.

   * Kushida integration (Y1V6-Y2V10): To stop Kushida's attempts to expel her, Suzune proposes a bet on the math exam. Persuasion requires making the offer irresistible to the other party. Suzune analyzes Kushida's psychology: she knows Kushida is obsessed with expelling her and is confident in her own academic abilities. To influence Kushida to accept a formal agreement that benefits the class, Suzune grants her key advantages:
   * It allows her to choose the exam subject (Mathematics).
   * It risks her own continued enrollment at the school (if Suzune loses, she withdraws; if Kushida loses, she only needs to stop sabotaging).
   * It introduces Manabu as a neutral witness to ensure Kushida doesn't doubt the validity of the deal.
Suzune influenced Kushida's decision to enter the arena that Suzune already controlled (thanks to her preparations with Professor Sae and her handling of the questions), effectively tying the traitor's hands within the framework of the bet. In Maco, where Kushida was caught and forced to stop attacking her, Suzune spent months trying to get closer to Kushida while keeping her in check to prevent her from harming the class. For example, during the "Class Poll," Suzune cornered Yamauchi to nominate him for expulsion and dismantled Kushida's influence on the vote.
   * Suzune reveals the detailed records of academic performance, attendance, and conduct that she has been compiling for each student. By quantitatively demonstrating that Yamauchi is the weakest link and, moreover, a traitor allied with Sakayanagi, she shatters any emotional defenses his friends might offer.
   * Suzune corners Kushida in front of everyone. She knows Kushida can't break her "good girl" facade in public; therefore, Suzune uses that very image to force Kushida to admit Yamauchi's deception, compelling the rest of the class to turn against the traitor. She influences the massive vote, ensuring Kiyotaka's (their most valuable asset) survival and removing the class's burden.
Or saving her when she needed it, like when the class was against her and wanted to expel her when her true nature was exposed by Kiyotaka
   * She acknowledges that Kushida committed selfish acts, but reminds his classmates that his academic, social, and athletic contributions far exceed those of most.
   * She reminds the class that they benefited for a year and a half from Kushida's "kindness" and willingly entrusted her with their secrets.
   * She convinces the class that the best "revenge" or reparation is not to get rid of her (which would cause them to lose class points in the long run), but to force her to use her high abilities to lead them to Class A.
With the class accepting her to a certain extent, the final step was missing. Suzune and Kushida were connected at their peak after months of Suzune insisting on being her friend. Kushida was at her lowest point, and Suzune no longer had another chance to fail. In Kushida's room, Suzune employs a complex persuasion based on pride, reality, and brutal empathy:
   * She shows Kushida that shutting herself in her room and waiting for the school to punish the class is a childish attitude that only destroys her, while Suzune continues to advance.
   * Suzune does something Kushida never expected: she genuinely praises the superhuman effort Kushida made for years to maintain her facade, listen to others, and be popular. She shows her that she understands the unbearable stress that entailed. By validating Kushida's effort, Suzune breaks through the barrier of defensive hatred.
   * She logically explains that her social mask still works outside the classroom (where no one would believe a third party that Kikyo Kushida is evil without proof) and that inside the classroom she doesn't need to feign love, only perform academically so that no one can criticize her.
Suzune influences Kushida by appealing to her desire for superiority: she offers to use Class A as the ultimate platform for Kushida to achieve her own personal gain and success. This demonstrates the paradigm shift she underwent over time. Initially, she attempted to influence others through a cold and inflexible logic that generated resistance (as in the early study groups), but she learns to decipher the hidden motives of her interlocutors and adapts her arguments to the "currency" that each person values. By getting Kushida to remain at the school and fight for Class A, Suzune demonstrates a high level of persuasion because she not only didn't change Kushida's malicious nature, but she successfully influenced her decisions and desires to channel that malice and talent for the benefit of the collective that Suzune desired.

   * D class upgrade (Y1V1-Now): She managed to create a class that could compete against the others

Triangulation -
   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): The strategic triangulation that Suzune executes when negotiating with Ryuuen Kakeru, implicitly incorporating Ayanokoji Kiyotaka as a third psychological vertex within the equation. Before the intervention:
   * Vertex A: Suzune
   * Vertex B: Ryuuen and Katsuragi
Dynamics: Unequal negotiation (Ryuuen with a dominant reputation and intimidating profile, and Katsuragi serious and analytical). After the implicit intervention:
   * Vertex A: Suzune
   * Vertex B: Ryuuen and Katsuragi
   * Vertex C: Kiyotaka
The relationship ceases to be bilateral. It becomes a three-way power dynamic. Ryuuen had already experienced Ayanokoji's intellectual superiority. Suzune didn't need to mention it directly. It was enough for her to:
   * Act with absolute confidence.
   * Show no dependence.
   * Negotiate as if she had invisible backing.
This triggers an automatic inference in Ryuuen: "She's not alone." In a direct negotiation, Ryuuen could intimidate or force concessions. But with a potentially superior third factor:
   * The cost of overstepping increases.
   * The margin for aggression is reduced. The risk assessment changes.
Suzune doesn't directly control Ryuuen. She controls his perception of the environment and the negotiation. With this, she achieves:
   * Stable negotiation.
   * No structural loss.
   * No hostile escalation.
   * Preservation of her leadership image.
   * Suzune maintains authority without appearing subordinate.
All this without saying:
   * "He has my back."
It's about letting the opponent reach their own conclusion. This avoids:
   * Explicit vulnerability.
   * Appearance of dependence.
   * Reputational damage.

Unpredictability -
   * Kiyotaka Bet (Y1V11.5-Y2V2): From Kiyotaka's perspective, the action is unexpected. He underestimates Suzune and expects her to:
   * follow more conventional strategies
   * and not confront him directly.
Her entire strategy breaks that tension. This explains why he later says:
   * He thanks Suzune for making things easier for him.

   * UVE (Y2V5): Her decision to save Kushida over Sakura. Before her intervention, the class operated within a predictable framework:
   * Kushida was to be expelled for treason.
   * The expulsion was to be aligned with moral punishment.
   * They would try to avoid sacrificing someone emotionally valuable.
Suzune breaks this pattern on two levels:
   * She refuses to expel the traitor.
   * She proposes expelling the least disruptive student.
   * This two-pronged move was not foreseeable under the prevailing emotional climate.
It is not chaotic unpredictability. It is structured unpredictability:
   * Her decision does not stem from emotion.
   * It stems from a strategic assessment of resources.
   * She calculates Kushida's future value.
   * She calculates Sakura's structural cost according to the OAA.
The class expected moral consistency and received strategic consistency. Being unpredictable at that moment grants her:
   * Psychological advantage.
   * Leadership repositioning.
Redefining the focus of the debate. By breaking the implicit consensus, it forces everyone to reanalyze the situation within its framework.

   * Suzune Cultural Festival Strategy (Y2V5-Y2V7): No leader from other years or external competitors expected Horikita Suzune — a character characterized by her rigidity and moral integrity — to be willing to orchestrate a fake betrayal and openly deceive her own classmates to ensure victory.

   * Written Comprehensive Cooperative Exam (Y2V9): Sakayanagi and Class A anticipated a confrontation where Class B would try to compensate for their lower academic ability by speeding up to gain time bonuses. Suzune's conscious choice to sacrifice time bonuses to ensure the accuracy of her D/E students through hand signals threw Class A's traditional prediction off track.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): Suzune decides NOT to protect Kouenji when he is about to be eliminated (after 2 mistakes), breaking logical expectations of the system. Context:
   * Kouenji had 2 strikes → 1 mistake = immediate elimination
Suzune:
   * Had a prior protection agreement
   * Had available slots
   * Knew the risk was maximum
→ Under standard conditions, protecting him was the only expected rational move, but Suzune:
   * Violates an implicit promise
   * Ignores the optimal protocol
   * Takes an extreme risk (direct elimination)
   * Acts according to a logic not externally observable
Result:
   * The class becomes suspicious
   * Kouenji himself questions the decision
   * Even the reader perceives the move as irrational at first glance
The decision had multiple simultaneous objectives:
   * Forcing Kouenji to act
   * Eliminating his passive behavior
   * Testing his independence
   * Manipulating external perception
   * Creating the image of a “vulnerable target”
   * Conditioning enemy targeting
This is high-level unpredictability because:
   * It is not randomness → it is intentional Counterintuitive
It breaks:
   * safety logic
   * social expectations
   * previous patterns
Conclusion:
   * Suzune takes an action that no rational observer would choose, but which internally maximizes multiple hidden variables.

Moving The Goalposts -
   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): Suzune began the exam aiming to score as many points as possible and win. But in the second phase, Ichinose and Ryuuen formed an alliance. Realizing this, Suzune shifted her focus from winning to ensuring Class A placed last to avoid any demerit points. This doomed Sakayanagi, who was powerless to stop it.

Scapegoating -
   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): It's not just about blaming someone, but also:
   * Redirecting collective perception
   * Manipulating social responsibility
   * Controlling the group narrative under pressure
Kouenji is positioned as:
   * An unpredictable element
   * An internal risk
   * A potential problem for the team
This makes him an ideal candidate for implicit blame. Suzune decides not to protect Kouenji when he's in critical danger. This:
   * Creates social tension
   * Makes her look like someone who breaks promises
   * Distracts attention from the real plan
And most importantly:
   * Puts him at risk of expulsion.
Kouenji has a point of protection: if they lose the exam, Suzune would have to expel someone, which makes everyone in the class nervous. But if Koenji is among those eliminated, Suzune can use him as a scapegoat to avoid expelling one of her classmates. Not only that, but the fact that Koneji is among those eliminated reduces the mental stress of the class in general and frees up one of its protective spaces.

Sophistry -
   * Koenji Neutralization Strategy (Y2V10): She reinterprets the agreement with Kouenji. She promised him "freedom" and "no expulsion," but used a contractual sophism: if Kouenji was expelled for being stubborn and the class came in last, she would use Kouenji's Protection Point to save him. True to the promise of no expulsion, but destroying the advantage of Kouenji's point.

   * Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12): During their direct confrontation in the exam, Suzune was under immense pressure and at a critical disadvantage. To slow Ichinose's perfect pace of nominations, Suzune cleverly appealed to the school's sense of "fairness", falsely arguing that executive roles should be distributed equally (1:1) between both classes. With this plausible but inaccurate argument in its immediate context, she challenged Ichinose to find the remaining roles among the students in Suzune's class.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): Making the entire school believe and diverting their thoughts (except for a few special ones who found out through other means that she couldn't control) that you are the sole leader of Class D.

Summary

Psychology Categories:
Psychological Insight -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): Suzune correctly understands Hōsen's true motivations, beyond his superficial discourse on private matters. Suzune doesn't stop at "Hōsen is violent," but understands why he acts this way, what he gains psychologically, and how this affects the structure of Class 1-D. She identifies that:
   * He's not just after points.
   * His real goal is control and subjugation.
   * He uses individualism as an ideology to dominate the class.

   * Kiyotaka's perfect smokescreen (Y1V3-Y2V12.5): She interpreted Yukimura's insecurity and academic envy after Ayanokoji's perfect score, addressing the issue with respect for her academic pride in order to neutralize her hostility without invalidating her feelings.

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): She understood Kōenji's psychology in her negotiation, she knew that he did not respond to a sense of duty, nor to empathy, nor to social pressure, but only to long-term personal gain and the preservation of his own comfort.

   * UVE (Y2V5): The correct assessment of three psychological profiles simultaneously:
   * Kushida – her pathological need for acceptance and her dual nature.
   * The class as a whole – their volatile emotional state and desire for immediate punishment.
Suzune understands something crucial:
   * Kushida doesn't act solely out of malice.
   * She acts out of a need for validation and social control.
   * Her betrayal stems from her fear of losing the image she has cultivated.
Instead of reducing her to an “enemy,” she analyzes her as a strategic resource:
   * High social skills.
   * Extensive network of contacts.
   * Cross-cutting influence.
Most only see the betrayal. Suzune sees the functional value behind the behavior. This requires separating emotion from psychological analysis. The class was:
   * Enraged.
   * Morally outraged.
   * Desiring immediate punishment.
If Suzune had confronted this emotion head-on without preparation, she would have failed. Therefore:
   * She first introduces the logical framework.
   * She lowers the emotional temperature.
   * She gradually shifts the focus of the discussion.
This demonstrates an understanding of the group's psychological dynamics. What makes this feat particularly remarkable is that it:
   * Analyzes three levels simultaneously: individual, interpersonal, and group.
   * Makes a decision that balances all three.
   * Minimizes structural fracture.
   * Consolidates leadership.
   * The ultimate success confirms that its interpretation was correct.
If its psychological analysis had been incorrect:
   * The class would have split.
   * The vote would have collapsed.
   * Its authority would have been challenged.
Furthermore, she managed to penetrate Kushida's defensive barrier by understanding that Kushida's aggression and hatred did not stem from pure evil, but from an inability to deal with her own weaknesses and fear of rejection.

   * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6): Suzune accurately deciphers Kushida's destructive and childish mindset. She understands that Kushida prefers to isolate herself to sabotage the class and bring down her. Suzune uses this to deliberately attack her pride (pointing out that if she stays locked away, Suzune will advance and graduate while she remains relegated to the status of a child), causing her to react and join the plan. Similarly, she perfectly understands Ibuki's competitive and impulsive psychology.

   * Kiryuuin Trial (Y2V9): Suzune understood the psychology of dissatisfaction and latent resentment among the third-year students trapped under Nagumo's dictatorial regime. This allowed her to understand why Kiriyama would risk so much: not to graduate in Class A (he already had his ticket), but to stop Nagumo's irrational squandering of points and the humiliation of his grade.

   * Koenji Neutralization Strategy (Y2V10): She understood Kouenji's psychology better than anyone: although he acts apathetically, his instinct for self-preservation and his pride prevent him from leaving his fate in the hands of Suzune's decisions if there is the slightest doubt.

   * Five Reachers strategy (Y2V10): She understood Ichinose's deep motivation: the fear of regret and extreme empathy for his classmates. She knew that this psychological trait could be used as a "chain" to condition each of his choices in the exam.

   * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10): She was able to decipher Ichinose's pacifist and overprotective motivations. She understood that Ichinose would prioritize preventing her classmates' expulsion at all costs, which allowed her to anticipate how she would use her protective slots and trap her in her own moral dilemma.

   * Kanzaki Control (Y2V12): She accurately deciphers Kanzaki's defensive stance and understands that, having assumed the role of "resigned strategist", Kanzaki was no longer playing to win, but looking for ways to negotiate or waiting for defeat.

   * Kushida (Y1V1-Now): She understood Kushida's isolation psychology after being exposed in the Unanimous Vote exam. Suzune understood that Kushida was not acting with adult coldness, but rather like a wounded girl whose destroyed pride led her to self-seclusion and sabotage.

Psychological Analysis -
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1): Analyze individual and group behaviors in parallel:
   * Class 1-D → culture of fear + control
   * Class 2-A → symbolic power + brand
   * Class 2-C → aggressive financial competition
   * Class 2-B → trust as social capital
Apply this analysis to:
   * Predict decisions
   * Adjust your strategy
   * Anticipate social breakdowns

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): Continuous evaluation of collective behavior during the UIE. Suzune excels here because she doesn't analyze isolated individuals; instead, she observes patterns such as:
   * early overextension of certain groups,
   * irresponsible point accumulation,
   * predictable collapses due to fatigue or penalties.
She concludes:
   * who will fall to the bottom,
   * which groups need support,
   * when to intervene and when not to.

   * Handwriting Identification (Y2V4-Y2V7): Suzune demonstrates a consistent ability to:
   * Interpret anomalous behavior
   * Detect hidden intentions
   * Analyze indirect action patterns
In context, her analysis is based on:
   * 1. Indirect behavioral inference
   * Inconsistencies
   * Timing of actions
   * Social context
This implies that she uses contextual logic (who benefits, who has access, who is motivated)
   * 2. Reading intention
   * Suzune doesn't stop at:
   * "someone sent a letter"
   * Instead, she infers:
   * There is a directed intention
   * There is a specific objective (Kiyotaka)
   * The author possesses a certain level of prior knowledge. This involves analyzing:
   * Motivation
   * Risk assumed
   * Aggressor's level of confidence
   * 3. Threat discrimination
   * Suzune assesses that it's not just a threat that ends there, but rather:
   * Potential real danger
   * Strategic relevance
   * Impact on the environment
This is important because she doesn't just stop at detecting it, but correctly prioritizes its level of danger.

   * Kiryuuin Trial (Y2V9): Psychological analysis is the cornerstone of Suzune's reasoning in this endeavor, allowing her to eliminate suspects and construct her hypothesis. Initially, she assesses Nagumo and dismisses him, understanding that while competitive and domineering, he is not irrational; a clumsy attack on Kiryuuin would only bring him unnecessary conflict and reputational damage without any clear benefits. She complements this by observing Kiryuuin, whose genuine dislike and lack of political motivation suggest he is not manipulating the situation.

On the other hand, Suzune interprets the psychology of Yamanaka and Tachibana, deducing that they are not deliberately lying, but rather were manipulated into believing they were following Nagumo's orders, as a false accusation against him would be too risky. This leads her to focus on Kiriyama, in whom she detects suspicious behavior such as anxiety to close the case and contradictions with his profile of extreme loyalty. Finally, by analyzing the collective psychology of the third year, Suzune identifies an environment of discontent and economic pressure that makes it plausible that Kiriyama will act indirectly to correct Nagumo's leadership mistakes.

   * Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12): In the first discussion table against Kanzaki, Suzune meticulously analyzes the microexpressions, glances, and everyday gestures of the 14 participants displayed on the monitor. When Miyake Akito falsely claims to be a "graduate" and accuses Norihito of being an honor student, Kanzaki takes the bait and misinterprets Norihito's behavior. Psychological Warfare -
   * UVE (Y2V5): The structured confrontation with Kushida during the exam's peak tension forces her to relinquish the psychological initiative and accept an unfavorable position. In the UVE, the main obstacle isn't the exam rules. It's Kushida's disruptive influence. Suzune understands that:
   * As long as Kushida maintains narrative control, the class will remain divided, and the vote won't converge.
Suzune executes three key moves:
   * She doesn't impulsively accuse.
   * She presents facts at the moment the class is already aware of the conflict.
   * This eliminates Kushida's room for denial.
Instead of an emotional attack, she redefines the focus:
   * The issue isn't "betrayal."
   * The issue is "optimizing resources to win."
   * This shift reduces the emotional force of the initial attack against Kushida.
Kushida goes from being:
   * An active influencer
To
   * A variable evaluated under cold criteria.
This change in position is psychologically devastating.
   * She loses her role as a manipulator.
   * It becomes a debated resource.
Psychological warfare isn't just aimed at Kushida. It's also aimed at the class:
   * It reduces outrage.
   * It introduces inevitability.
   * It presents its solution as the only viable way out.
   * The key is the feeling of inevitability.
When a group perceives that a decision is the only structurally viable one, resistance decreases. Suzune doesn't use:
   * Lies.
   * False propaganda.
   * Direct threats.
   * Extreme emotional intimidation.
Suzune uses:
   * Structural pressure.
   * Narrative repositioning.
   * Control of the decision-making framework.
   * It's framing warfare, not mind-wrecking.
This move could have provoked:
   * Rebellion.
   * Loss of leadership.
   * Class fragmentation.
The fact that it didn't happen confirms that:
   * Her reading of the psychological terrain was accurate.
   * She applied the pressure at the exact moment.

   * Kanzaki Control (Y2V12): By responding with absolute coldness to Kanzaki's plea and revealing her insecurities about her own class, Suzune destroys what little fighting spirit she had left, causing Kanzaki to spend her turns consecutively without attempting to attack.

   * Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12): During her direct confrontation with Ichinose, Suzune was at a significant disadvantage in terms of skill, mental ability, and lives (7 to 10). Noticing that Ichinose was deducing the roles perfectly and methodically, Suzune employed a psychological manipulation tactic to alter her opponent's behavior and focus. She suggested to Ichinose that, for the sake of school fairness, executive roles should be distributed evenly between the two classes ( 1:1 ratio). Although Ichinose doubted her claims, Suzune managed to sow uncertainty (the "bait") by exploiting the slightest possibility (even a 1%) that it was true. This forced Ichinose to shift her attention to her own teammates to avoid oversights, narrowing her field of vision. Thanks to this self-induced psychological distraction, Suzune bought herself the necessary time to act aggressively and correctly identify Hatsutori as an executive role, thus gaining a life and temporarily balancing the game's pressure.

The OAA is not simply a number. It is:
   * An official evaluation tool.

Suzune understands something key:

She uses structural knowledge of the ANHS educational system:

   * How to avoid internal social fractures.
   * How to protect future legitimacy.
That's the use of prior structural knowledge. She understands that:
   * If no one is expelled → the class fails.
   * If expulsions occur based on emotion → the class fractures.
   * If expulsions are arbitrary → cohesion is lost.
Her solution uses a variable that:
   * Cannot be easily disputed.
   * Minimizes resentment.
   * Maintains strategic coherence.
This analysis isn't instinct. It's the application of accumulated learning about group dynamics and functional hierarchies. One of the greatest indicators of crystallized intelligence is the ability to apply knowledge even when it contradicts personal emotions. Suzune:
   * Save Kushida (strategic decision).
   * Expel Sakura (functional decision).
Both decisions coexist because she understands that the survival of the class requires structure, not sentimentality.    * Exclude leaders.

This transforms her decision into a generalizable logical model. Formulating a universal criterion is clear evidence of high crystallized intelligence.

Example:

This system:
   * transmits information
   * does not communicate the answer
   * does not violate rules.
It is a creative solution to a problem of constraints, which is a classic manifestation of fluid intelligence. Suzune completely redesigns the dynamics of the exam.
   * Expected normal flow: Student enters -> Looks for easy questions -> Wastes time reading -> Answers few questions
   * Flow designed by Suzune: Strong student enters -> Analyzes many questions -> Indicates numbers of easy questions -> Weak student enters -> Solves directly
This transforms the exam into a cooperative system of exploration and execution. This type of structural reconfiguration of a problem is a strong manifestation of fluid intelligence. By making the correct nomination on Miyake, Suzune not only protected her own lives but also caused Kanzaki to lose three lives. This logical move shattered Kanzaki's mental stability, and he subsequently resigned and began passing repeatedly. Suzune capitalized on this new situation with cold and ruthless efficiency, closing out the game with a perfect 7-0 victory.

When Kiyotaka offered her the position of General under the harsh condition that she withdraw all cooperation with the class until graduation if she won, Suzune faced a dilemma that directly impacted her pride and self-confidence. The position of General is usually reserved for the most capable student in the class, and relinquishing it would expose her shortcomings to everyone.
