Strategy Categories:
   * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1):
1-) STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS: It doesn't achieve everything ideal, but it meets all its defined objectives.
   * No students from Class 2-D were expelled. She ensured that vulnerable students (ranks D and E in Academic Ability) were paired with capable partners before the deadline.
   * Class 2-D achieved 3rd place with an average of 640 points, surpassing Class 2-B (621 points). This allowed them to gain class points (+8 Class Points, going from 275 to 283).
   * They did not spend the class capital competing against Class 2-A or 2-C to buy elite students.
   * Class 2-D avoids serious penalties.
   * Create an equal relationship is established with 1-D.
   * The strategy nearly collapsed physically during the ambush behind the dormitories due to an external information asymmetry:
   * Suzune was unaware of President Nagumo's relaxation of the rules regarding violence and the existence of the secret 20 million bounty for Kiyotaka's expulsion.
   * Although resolving the physical conflict required Kiyotaka's decisive action, Suzune's conceptual strategy to force Class 1-D's agreement worked in practice. The final contract negotiated with Nanase saved the remaining students.

2-) STRATEGY EFFICIENCY:
   * A class victory awarded 50 class points (approximately 2.14 million Private Points over 11 months for the class). If they spent 500,000 points per student to attract 4 to 5 first-year honors, the class would immediately go into financial deficit.
   * By refusing to participate in the auction orchestrated by Sakayanagi and Ryūen, Class 2-D retained 100% of their liquidity for future exams where money is essential.
   * She delegated the task of panic control and the search for social/sports connections. Kushida was used to connect with Yagami and secure partners in Class 1-B.
   * She transformed a weakness (Sudo as an E student) into an asset through a multifaceted maneuver.
   * Using a concealed wireless earpiece, a phone transmitting audio, and real-time guidance from her room, Suzune enabled Kiyotaka to cook a complex dish without any margin for error, ensuring Amasawa's success for Sudo.
   * Suzune organized study sessions for those ranked C- and below to raise their baseline exam level (from 150 to 200+ points), decreasing the risk margin for partners in her class.
   * In the final ambush, Suzune's plan to send Kiyotaka to look for a teacher in the adjacent dormitories was logistically unbeatable.
   * At an estimated distance of 26.4 m to 39.6 m between campus areas, a round trip at top speed would take Kiyotaka about 10 seconds. Adding the search for the teacher during rush hour (10:00 p.m.), the total isolation time for Sudo and Suzune was approximately 1 minute and 10 seconds. Suzune established minimal physical containment for an extremely short rescue response time.

3-) STRATEGY EXECUTION:
   1. Phase 1: Define objectives
   1. Since the announcement of the exam, Suzune established three clear and non-negotiable goals for Class 2-D:
   1. Ensure that all pairs in the class surpassed the 500-point combined mark.
   2. Refuse to participate in the economic warfare and student auctions planned by the other classes, as Class 2-D's finances were far inferior.
   3. Secure a decent position in the class rankings without risking valuable resources.
   2. Phase 2: Control the class
   1. To prevent panic from causing her classmates to make impulsive decisions (due to the risk of expulsion), Suzune took the following actions:
   1. She reassured the class that with proper preparation they could pass the exam and ordered that no one with a rank below B on the OAA app should form pairs on their own.
   2. She stipulated that every student (strong or weak) had to consult her or Hirata before acting, maintaining absolute control over the class's movements.
   3. She reserved the highest-achieving students for future negotiations and arranged individual tutoring for classmates performing below C-.
   3. Phase 3: Analyzing the situation
   1. After analyzing the market and predicting that the Sakayanagi (2-A) and Ryuuen (2-C) classes would monopolize the best students by buying them with private points, Suzune changed her approach:
   1. She decided not to compete in the Class 2-B confidence event, noticing that it was mostly attended by first-year students with low grades (C, D, or E).
   2. Suzune notices that no students from Class 1-D attended Ichinose's mass meeting, deducing that someone had complete control over that group. She discovers that Hōsen rules the class with an iron fist, forbidding her classmates from finding partners independently and forcing them to demand a minimum of 500,000 private points to inflate their value.
   3. Aware that Class 2-D has limited financial resources to compete in a bidding war against Classes 2-A and 2-C, Suzune seeks an alliance based on long-term trust and mutual benefit. Her goal is not to win the exam, but to prevent her struggling classmates from being expelled.
   4. She sought to negotiate a direct agreement with Class 1-D to pair struggling and academically weak students from both classes without monetary transactions, creating a long-term support network and a level playing field for future exams.
   4. Phase 4: Creation of countermeasures
   1. Faced with Hōsen's hostile nature and extortion and the strategies of other classes, Suzune also prepared alternatives:
   1. She planned to inflate the value of average students (ranks between C+ and B-) to force rival Ryuuen and Sakayanagi to spend more private points than anticipated, financially draining them and buying time.
   2. She used Kushida and Hirata as intermediaries to secure individual pairings with students from Class 1-B in case the main alliance failed.
   3. She prioritized Sudou and placed him at the top of his priority list because his aggressive appearance and history frightened the first-year students, despite his improved grades.
   5. Phase 5: Save Sudou
   1. To save Sudou, she conducted extensive research among the first-year students. While searching, Amasawa approached her, looking for a partner with a high enough skill level to compete for first place.
   1. Suzune rejected the offer for herself and redirected it towards Sudou.
   2. In response, Amasawa imposed the condition that he would team up with him if he proved to be the strongest by defeating other students in a fight.
   1. Knowing that fights broke the rules and that it was unlikely anyone would openly accept Sudou's challenge, Suzune (in cahoots with Kiyotaka and taking advantage of Sudou's natural reaction) accepted the proposal, adding a stipulation that if no one else showed up to fight, Amasawa would have to keep Sudou. This certainty led Amasawa to deduce for herself that Sudou was the strongest second-year student, falling into Suzune's trap of projecting that image without resorting to physical violence.
   3. Realizing she had been cornered and there would be no fight, Amasawa changed the rules, she demanded that a boy prepare a complex dish of her choosing. Since Sudou didn't know how to cook, Suzune seized the opportunity to offer Kiyotaka as the substitute cook.
   1. To ensure success without any margin for error, Suzune coordinated the cooking session remotely. She gave Kiyotaka a hidden wireless earpiece connected to Sudou's phone, allowing her to transmit precise instructions and recipes in real time from her own room while he cooked in his. To ensure that Amasawa wouldn't suspect anything, she trained Kiyotaka in cooking so that he could play a good role.
   2. Thanks to this meticulous teamwork between Kiyotaka and Suzune, they passed Amasawa's culinary test flawlessly, forcing the first-year student to keep her word and formally confirm her pairing with Sudou for the exam.
   6. Phase 6: Gaining Nanase's trust
   1. Suzune had already saved those who needed saving without spending a single point; all that remained was to reach third place.
   1. She first begins by gaining Nanase's trust through meetings where she clearly states her intentions without wavering.
   2. Nanase meets with Suzune and offers her a covert strategy: since many in Class 1-D reject Hōsen's methods, she plans to overthrow him and become the leader. She proposes that Class 2-D secretly ally with her dissident faction.
   * Suzune rejects this offer in the short term. She argues that a civil war would divide Class 1-D and drain resources. Her priority is bringing Hōsen into the agreement to save both classes equally.
   * Nanase's proposal was a test. Seeing that Suzune didn't sacrifice the future of 1-D for a quick profit, Nanase agreed to force a face-to-face meeting with Hōsen.
   7. Phase 7: The final negotiation
   1. Thanks to gaining Nanase's trust, she organized a final meeting on Sunday night, Hōsen proved intractable, demanding millions of points and resorting to intimidation.
   1. Suzune stood firm and threatened to withdraw and accept the exam penalty before finally giving in to his blackmail.
   2. Suzune is confident that the school rules will deter Hōsen from using actual physical violence, as he risks expulsion. However, she suffers from an information asymmetry, she is unaware that the new Student Council President, Nagumo, has secretly implemented a much more tolerant policy regarding internal conflicts. Hōsen takes advantage of this and physically attacks Sudou and Suzune behind the dormitories. The negotiation was always a facade. Hōsen's true objective was to lure them to a secluded location to stage a knife attack, self-harm, and frame Kiyotaka to get himself expelled, collecting a secret 20 million point reward set aside for first-year students.
   1. Suzune decides to use Sudou and herself as bait so that Kiyotaka will escape and find a teacher to put an end to it.
   3. Although Suzune's strategy of logical persuasion was thwarted by the knife trap, the situation was resolved and forced Class 1-D to cooperate due to Kiyotaka's intervention
   1. The following Monday, Suzune and Nanase formally meet and finalize all the details of the cooperative agreement without exchanging any private points. Thanks to this, all the pairings are successfully formalized, allowing Class 2-D to save their at-risk students and secure third place in the special exam.

4-) STRATEGY RESILIENCE:
   * Despite the attacks, Hōsen's extortion, and the lack of information regarding the reward for Kiyotaka, Suzune's strategic pillar never collapsed. Her plan was structured around three priorities:
   * Avoid expulsions in Class 2-D.
   * Even when the situation in the dormitories seemed like a complete disaster in terms of negotiations, prior work ensured that vulnerable students were already safe thanks to previously established alliances and mentorships. The strategy held because survival didn't depend solely on the success of the final meeting with Hōsen; that negotiation was merely the extra push needed to secure third place.
   * Reach the top 3.
   * Don't get caught up in the money war.
   * Faced with the economic monopoly held by Classes 2-A and 2-C, who inflated bids to 500,000 private points per student, Suzune's strategy demonstrated remarkable resilience. Instead of succumbing to extortion or depleting her class's resources in the long run—which she analytically calculated would generate a massive deficit—she resisted the temptation to enter an auction they couldn't win, keeping her plan intact through intangible assets.
   * When violently cornered by Hōsen in the rear area of ​​the first-year dormitories, the resilience of her strategy shifted to the immediate tactical field. Relying on known institutional rules and the physical structure of the campus, she plotted a logical escape route by sending Kiyotaka to find the professors in the adjacent building. Based on the area's dimensions and the speed of the attack, it was a viable window for her and Sudō to hold out. Although the knife attack altered the outcome, the defensive logic she formulated under extreme pressure demonstrated the soundness of her decision-making in a moment of crisis.

5-) STRATEGY SHIELDING:
   * Knowing the selfish, impulsive, and panic-prone nature of Class 2-D, Suzune fortified the strategy from day one by imposing a containment protocol:
   * She forbade hasty alliances for students with grades below B.
   * She centralized decision-making through herself and Hirata.
   * She reserved high-achieving students for strategic negotiations.
This eliminated the internal friction in which mediocre students would fight to monopolize the few outstanding first-year students.
   * Suzune didn't leave her plan to rely on a single channel:
   * She leveraged Kushida's social network and her connection to Yagami to channel students from Class 1-B without exposing herself to the internal sabotage Kushida usually attempts.
   * When the direct route to Hōsen turned hostile, Suzune began to devise a secondary plan to form a secret alliance with the dissident group in Class 1-D led by Nanase.
   * Lacking capital, she used Kiyotaka's culinary skills as leverage to neutralize Amasawa's demands and ensure Suduo's protection.
   * Instead of ignoring Sakayanagi and Ryūen's financial maneuvering, Suzune acted to harm their coffers. She devised a scheme to artificially raise the expectations of average students (ranks C+ to B-), giving them a false sense of worth. This forced Classes 2-A and 2-C to spend private points not only on the elite but also on ordinary students, eroding the rival budget and buying valuable time to negotiate with Class 1-D.
   * Suzune accurately detected market anomalies by identifying the complete absence of Class 1-D at the Ichinose meeting and deduced that Hōsen exerted dictatorial control over his group. Furthermore, she deciphered that Hōsen's provocations in the halls revealed a motivation of domination and subjugation toward Class 2-D, not merely economic ambition.
   * The only flaw in her cover story didn't stem from a logical deficiency, but from an insurmountable information asymmetry. Suzune had no way of knowing about the secret 20 million bounty for Kiyotaka's expulsion, nor Nagumo's relaxed policy on physical violence. Hōsen and Nanase operated within a hidden set of rules to which she had no access. Even so, Suzune suspected Hōsen's aggressive intentions, but she relied on the school's official legal framework as a firm deterrent.

6-) STRATEGY FORESIGHT:
   * From Chabashira's initial explanation, Suzune analyzed the exam rules and immediately deduced how the students would react: students with high academic ability would seek out others of equal level to maximize rewards, while those with low ability would fall into despair or try to join the strongest.
   * Knowing that Class 2-D (flawed, impulsive, and selfish by nature) would collapse trying to compete for top students, she centralized decision-making. She forbade impulsive pairings for anyone with a grade lower than a B and established a mandatory approval filter through either herself or Hirata.
   * Suzune immediately identified that Sakayanagi (Class 2-A) and Ryūen (Class 2-C) would take the initiative on Day 1 by buying talent. She assessed her class's capital and realized that entering a bidding war meant financial ruin (spending 500,000 points per student would leave the class in deficit even if they won the class points).
   * She anticipated that Ichinose's (Class 2-B) confidence-building strategy would attract a massive number of underachieving students seeking salvation. Instead of competing on that ground (where Class 2-D lacked the necessary prestige or charisma), she used the mass gathering not to find partners, but to investigate and map who had NOT attended.
   * Noticing that 100% of Class 1-D was absent from Ichinose's meeting, he accurately deduced that someone had taken complete control of that classroom and imposed a policy of no external cooperation.
   * She anticipated that between two low-achieving students, any first-year would prefer Ike to Sudō. Therefore, she positioned Sudō as Priority #1 to secure him a strong partner through direct negotiation before he was completely ruled out.
   * Deducing from Hōsen's provocative behavior in the halls ("they'll have to beg us"), Suzune identified that Hōsen's goal was not merely to accumulate private points, but to dominate and subjugate Class 2-D. This allowed her to hypothesize that there was room for non-monetary negotiation based on status or alliances.

7-) STRATEGY ADAPTABILITY:
   * If a full alliance with Class 1-D did not guarantee winning the exam, he shifted his focus to securing only the minimum quota of at-risk students (ranks E and D).
   * To neutralize Sakayanagi and Ryuuen's monetary advantage while she negotiated, Suzune devised a tactic to artificially inflate the perceived value for average first-year students (ranks C+ to B-).
   * By making them believe they could aspire to high offers, he generated discontent and caused Class 2-A and 2-C to have to spend more points than planned to recruit ordinary students, gaining valuable time for their side conversations.
   * When Ichika Amasawa appeared out of nowhere challenging them, Suzune adapted her real-time planning to channel that resource towards Sudō:
   * She diverted Amasawa's attention from herself to Sudō.
   * She accepted the absurd challenge of a fight, proposing a clause that if no one else agreed to fight Sudō, Amasawa would have to face him. When Amasawa realized the trap, Suzune reacted by feigning frustration to avoid exposing the deception.
   * When the challenge shifted to a culinary test, and knowing Sudō couldn't cook, Suzune immediately pivoted, using Kiyotaka as her enforcer. Through a hidden earpiece, she guided him from her room while consulting the recipe in real time.
   * As backup against Hōsen's obstacles, she used Hirata and Sudou to contact the soccer and basketball club and Kushida to negotiate with Yagami (Class 1-B), ensuring that average/low-achieving students like Karuizawa, Sakura, and Hasebe would find partners safely without financial cost.
   * When Nanase secretly proposed creating a faction to overthrow Hōsen and save a small group of students, Suzune analyzed the risk. Although it was an easy way out, she temporarily rejected it because fragmenting Class 1-D would cause a civil war that would damage long-term alliances. She used this refusal to demonstrate her strategic vision and gain Nanase's complete trust.
   * When the negotiations at the karaoke bar collapsed, Suzune refused to give in to Hōsen's financial blackmail. She preferred to accept the minor penalties for the last-minute couples rather than jeopardize the class's financial future.
   * Faced with Hōsen's unexpected attack at the rear of the dormitories, he quickly assessed the layout of the area. She formulated a containment strategy where he had to hold out alongside Sudō for the exact amount of time it took Kiyotaka to run and find the teachers.

8-) STRATEGIC ALIGNMENT:
   1. Suzune's first phase was purely defensive. She immobilized the desperate impulses of Class 2-D by forbidding low-achieving students from acting individually or rashly.
   1. She channeled decisions through a centralized network (herself + Hirata). This ensured that no student would give away their chance at a partner or panic upon seeing the open market on the OAA app.
   2. She identified Ken Sudō as the class's top priority, not only because of his historically low academic standing, but also because of his behavioral deterrent effect. Suzune predicted that no freshman would choose Sudō over Ike with equal grades.
   1. Instead of spending points, Suzune invested human and skill resources to capture Amasawa Ichika. She aligned Sudō's need with Amasawa's eccentricity to neutralize the risk posed by the most vulnerable student.
   3. Suzune realized that Class 2-D did not have the monetary liquidity of Sakayanagi (2-A) nor the credit aggressiveness of Ryūen (2-C). Channel Diversification:
   1. Via Class 1-B: She used Kushida and Yagami as a bridge of trust to absorb couples without excessive cost.
   2. Via Class 1-D: She identified the anomaly of Class 1-D's complete absence from the Ichinose meeting. She deduced that they were centralized by an authoritarian figure (Hōsen) and directed her diplomacy toward a class-to-class agreement (mutual aid among the vulnerable).
   4. To drain the financial resources of Class 2-A and 2-C while negotiating with Class 1-D, Suzune used average-achieving students. By intentionally exposing them to tempting offers, she forced the rival classes to pay inflated prices for mid-ranking students, making Sakayanagi and Ryūen's monopoly more expensive and slowing it down.
Suzune built a bridge where each step enabled the next. Even with the collapse of the negotiation front with Hōsen, the previous defensive measures had already ensured the survival of Class 2-D before Sunday night.

9-) TACTICAL COMPLEXITY:
   1. The Cooking Tactic
   1. Convincing Amasawa that Sudō was an unbeatable physical force through a defensive bluff (the deterrent sporting fight).
   2. Transferring the pressure to Kiyotaka without losing authority or showing weakness.
   3. Creating a one-way, real-time communication channel (Kiyotaka in the kitchen with a headset + Suzune guiding with recipes on screen in her room + Sudō's phone in his backpack capturing the atmosphere). This demonstrated tactical micromanagement skills at a distance under pressure.
   2. Market Profiling and Financial Diagnosis
   1. She mathematically demonstrated that paying 500,000 points per student (500,000 x N) would generate a net deficit for Class 2-D, even winning 1st place. So she decided not to get involved in that war
   3. The Logistics Security Network
   1. The distance from the point of confrontation to the teachers' building was approximately 26.42 m to 39.63 m (My own calculations, not hers).
   2. Suzune ordered Kiyotaka (the fastest student in the school) to go for backup. The estimated time to go, find a teacher, and return was approximately 1 minute and 10 seconds (My own calculations, not hers).
   3. Holding a standoff with Sudō for that amount of time was a tactically sound and feasible containment plan within the parameters of standard school physics and logic.

Personal ranking for the best strategy categories in this feat:
Resilience > Alignment > Foresigh > Shielding  > Efficiency > Tactical > Effectiveness > Adaptability  > Execution

   * Kiyotaka Bet (Y1V11.5-Y2V2) (“Creative Interpretation”):

1-) STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS: Suzune's primary goal with this wager was not simply to gauge Kiyotaka's level or win a personal duel, but to force him to break the chains of "average student" that he had imposed upon himself, compelling him to either expose his talent or work openly for the benefit of the class.
   * Suzune knew Kiyotaka possessed immense potential. To prevent him from continuing to operate in the shadows, she provoked him so they could have a competition, a situation where a standard average wouldn't be enough to win.
   * If Suzune won: Kiyotaka would be contractually obligated to use all of his hidden abilities to advance to Class A, holding nothing back.
   * If Kiyotaka won: Suzune would join the student council, but she would have already achieved her goal of revealing her abilities.

2-) STRATEGY EFFICIENCY:
   * Unlike other class leaders who needed to spend millions of points or make protection concessions to move their pieces, Suzune did not spend a single private class point or incur debts with other courses.
   * Suzune used a simple series of subtle provocations and teasing to entice Kiyotaka into accepting the academic duel, saving herself the trouble of complex negotiations.
   * The time Suzune spent studying advanced mathematics was not a personal waste. Her score of 87 directly contributed to raising the class's overall average on the written exam.
   * By forcing Kiyotaka to earn 100 points, he was the one who had to bear the cost of exposure and the strain of solving university-level problems. Suzune managed to unleash the power of the class's "hidden weapon" in exchange for the sole personal condition of joining the Student Council, something that benefited the class by passively granting them more influence and private points each month.

3-) STRATEGY EXECUTION:
   1. Phase 1: Define the true problem and Bet
   1. At first glance, all the events that led to the academic challenge and bet to Kiyotaka in volume 11.5 seem like an arrogant attempt to test his strength, fueled by the fact that she usually got better grades. However, her true goal wasn't to discover if Kiyotaka was brilliant (something she already knew from vol 1), but to "break his chains" and force him to act in the best interest of the class.
   2. Phase 2: The Bet
   1. Suzune provokes Kiyotaka and designs a situation where, regardless of the outcome of the written exam, her class benefits:
   1. If Suzune won the bet: Kiyotaka would be contractually obligated to stop holding back and use all his hidden abilities to push the class toward Class A.
   2. If Kiyotaka won the bet: In order to defeat her, he would be forced to publicly reveal a fragment of his overwhelming potential.
   3. By pushing him to get a perfect score, Suzune managed to shatter the "average student" facade Kiyotaka used as a shield.
   4. The strategy's true success was confirmed after the exam; Kiyotaka's perfect score immediately aroused suspicion among his classmates.
   3. Phase 3: Intervention
   1. When the score is revealed, she intervenes with a brilliant explanation to the rest of the group, manipulating the narrative to portray Kiyotaka not as a suspicious threat, but as a "high-potential secret weapon" that the class had kept hidden.
   2. This move dispels the general mistrust and lays the perfect foundation for Kiyotaka to openly use his talents in any discipline in the future without raising destructive alarms. The restrictions that bound him are now removed.

4-) STRATEGY RESILIENCE: The usual resistance from an opponent in a bet is simply to win or refuse to participate. Suzune neutralized both resistances from the strategy's inception:
   * If Suzune won the bet: By contract, Kiyotaka would be obligated to openly use all his hidden abilities to lead the class to Class A.
   * If Kiyotaka won the bet: He would publicly shatter his facade as an average student in front of the entire school and Class 2-D.
Kiyotaka always tried to avoid attention and keep a low profile. The strength of Suzune's plan lay in directly challenging his pride and abilities (questioning his past achievements and implying that his victories were simply due to luck or Ryuuen's carelessness). By exploiting the exam's difficulty—a level where an A-rank student barely reached 80-84 points—Suzune eliminated the possibility of Kiyotaka "covering up" by scoring a modest 90. If he wanted to beat her, he had to put himself out there.
   * The strategy didn't depend on Suzune's numerical victory. It withstood Kiyotaka's crushing 100-point win without crumbling, because the final result was 100% accurate.

5-) STRATEGY SHIELDING:
   * A potential pitfall of challenging an academic powerhouse to a written exam is looking foolish or becoming demoralized. Suzune took extra precautions against this:
   * She studied until she achieved 87 points on a test with university-level material that she didn't even fully understand by the end. This ensured that, if she failed, no one could accuse her of incompetence; her score was elite level.
   * If she lost, the punishment was joining the Student Council. This, far from being a real disadvantage, was a politically advantageous move that strengthened her leadership position in the school and passively earned her points with her class. She turned the "penalty" into an institutional advantage.
   * The biggest strategic discrepancy that Kiyotaka's 100/100 revelation could cause was the distrust and chaos within Class 2-D; this is where Suzune shines at her best:
   * Immediately after the exam, Suzune took control of the public narrative in front of the class. She explained and framed Kiyotaka's grade in such a way that the class didn't see him as a liar or a threat, but rather as Class 2-D's high-potential "secret weapon."
   * By integrating the revelation within a controlled narrative, she gave Kiyotaka the freedom to use his abilities in the future without being persecuted or ostracized by his classmates. She neutralized any adverse reaction from the class before it could even begin.

6-) STRATEGY FORESIGHT:
   * The greatest feature of Foresight in this strategy lies in how Suzune anticipated the only two possible exam outcomes and structured the conditions so that both scenarios would fulfill their true objective: to break the "average student" chains that bound Kiyotaka and force him to contribute to the rise to Class A.
   * Scenario A (If Suzune wins): Kiyotaka is bound by a verbal contract to openly use all his abilities for the benefit of Class 2-D, holding nothing back.
   * Scenario B (If Kiyotaka wins): To defeat Suzune, who scored 87/100, Kiyotaka is forced to achieve a higher score. In doing so, he irreversibly destroys his facade as an average student in front of the entire school.
   * Suzune predicted that Kiyotaka wouldn't be able to win the bet by faking an average grade. By choosing a special exam with 10% content outside the high school curriculum, she forced Kiyotaka to choose between losing or revealing his true academic level.
   * Suzune proved to understand Kiyotaka's psychology better than he himself realized. She anticipated that if she directly asked him to help the class, he would refuse or make excuses, as he usually did.
   * Suzune used a facade of mild arrogance and mocking remarks to tug at Kiyotaka's pride and corner him into accepting the bet.
   * Kiyotaka tends to underestimate Suzune's maturity and acting ability. She anticipated that he would see the bet as a harmless challenge that he could control or use for his own purposes.
   * The real proof that Suzune planned this long-term comes immediately after the notes are published.
   * If an average student were to pull off a perfect 100 on an exam where even the top students didn't score above 90, the class would react with suspicion, alienation, or destructive envy.
   * As soon as the results are revealed, Suzune isn't paralyzed by her numerical defeat. She immediately takes control of the public narrative, addresses Class 2-D, and contextualizes Kiyotaka's score, presenting him not as a liar, but as a "high-potential secret weapon." In this way, she transforms Kiyotaka's victory into a political asset for the class and grants him the social freedom to use his talents publicly in future special exams without being ostracized.
   * Suzune did not design the bet in isolation. Her foresight was fueled by an analysis of the ANHS geopolitical map:
   * Suzune understood that the gap in points with the upper classes had narrowed and that the friendly alliance with Class B was no longer sustainable if they wanted to reach Class A. Having become potential enemies, Class 2-D needed to maximize its internal power to the limit.
   * She realized that relying solely on her own growth or that of Hirata and Sudō wouldn't be enough to defeat Sakayanagi and Ryūen. Activating Kiyotaka was a necessary condition for the group's survival and advancement.

7-) STRATEGY ADAPTABILITY:
   * In a conventional strategy, losing the bet would mean the collapse of the objective. However, Suzune left a "malleable space" in the terms of the agreement. If she won, she would force Kiyotaka to actively work for the class, giving it his all. If she lost (which was the realistic contingency scenario given her suspicions about his potential), the mere fact that Kiyotaka would have to get a perfect or outstanding grade would irreversibly shatter his "average student" facade.
   * When the unexpected result for the rest of the school occurred—a perfect 100 in Advanced Mathematics with university-level concepts like Lebesgue integrals and measure theory—Suzune didn't freeze or panic. She immediately adjusted her narrative for the class, framing Kiyotaka's grade as that of a "secret weapon" or a high-potential resource for Class 2-D. She turned a personal defeat into a unifying opportunity for the group.

8-) STRATEGIC ALIGNMENT:
   1. Class 2-D severed friendly relations with Class B as the point gap narrowed, making it essential to unleash their full potential.
   2. Kiyotaka was the class's strongest student, but he was bound by the constraints of his "average student" profile.
   1. Suzune observed Kiyotaka's inconsistencies throughout the year, his votes received from Class A, the protection point obtained, his confrontation with Manabu, and the previous defeat against Sakayanagi.
   1. Suzune understood that Kiyotaka wouldn't respond to polite requests. She adjusted her tone, using subtle provocation to entice him to accept the challenge. The psychological component aligned perfectly with the exam proposal.
   3. The wager forced the exam. To win the wager (or to avoid losing his status in the face of Acting Principal Tsukishiro's exam), Kiyotaka had to shine. The direct and inevitable consequence was the breaking of those constraints, allowing him to use his abilities more freely in the future for the benefit of the class.
   1. Suzune's strategy aligned so flawlessly with the bigger picture that it even fit with Kiyotaka's own secret needs (who needed to start showing his potential due to Tsukishiro's threats of expulsion). By giving him a formal scenario within the classroom to justify his perfect grade, Suzune served as the perfect bridge so that Kiyotaka's move wouldn't destroy the group's cohesion.

9-) TACTICAL COMPLEXITY:
   * Layer 1: The Alibi of Pride and Superficial Provocation
   * Suzune uses blatant provocation, subtly mocking Kiyotaka's lack of direct contributions to the class and questioning whether he's truly as capable as he claims or if his victory against Ryuuen was merely luck. She proposes a bet based on their written exam scores, making a "weak" threat to force him into an agreement.
   * This layer serves as a perfect decoy. Kiyotaka traditionally underestimates Suzune's perception of her pride. By projecting the image that she is acting out of arrogance and self-doubt, Suzune camouflages her true objective behind a behavioral bias that Kiyotaka anticipates.
   * Layer 2: The Straight Bet Condition
   * If Kiyotaka wins ==> Suzune joins the Student Council.
   * If Suzune wins ==> Kiyotaka must use his true abilities without holding back to lead the class to Class A.
   * It presents an illusion of choice and risk. On the surface, Suzune risks her personal freedom by making her entry into the Student Council conditional. This gives the wager a standard structure that Kiyotaka can process and accept. Of course, initially, Suzune had no idea what Kiyotaka was going to ask for; however, he couldn't ask for too much because he might lose Suzune's trust and, therefore, the wall that protected his normal life.
   * Layer 3: The Forced Disclosure Trap
   * Suzune intentionally chooses a special exam where the questions will be of an absurdly high level of difficulty.
   * She knows perfectly well that not even the best students (A-rank) will easily surpass the 80–90 point threshold. By forcing a math exam of maximum difficulty, Suzune eliminates the possibility of a tie and makes it impossible for Kiyotaka to win using an "average" score. If he wants to beat Suzune's potential 87 points, he is forced to achieve an outstanding or perfect score, thus revealing his true talent.
   * Layer 4: Media Manipulation and Breaking Chains
   * Following the publication of the results, Suzune uses this event to give an explanation that shapes the narrative in front of the rest of the class and the school.
   * Instead of letting Kiyotaka's note breed destructive suspicion or isolation, she steers the conversation to present him as the class's "high-potential secret weapon." In doing so, she dismantles the "Average Student" label that bound him and grants him social cover to act freely in the future.

Personal ranking for the best strategy categories in this feat:
Resilience > Efficiency >  Foresight > Shielding > Adaptability > Alignment > Execution > Tactical > Effectiveness

   * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4):
1-) STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS:
   1. The effectiveness of Suzune's strategy in the UIE is rated as extremely high, as she absolutely achieved all the strategic objectives set before setting foot on the island:
   1. Compliance with the Offensive Axis
   1. Suzune knew her class lacked a traditional offensive structure to compete against giants like Sakayanagi, Nagumo, or Ryūen.
   2. Seeing that Kiyotaka wasn't aiming for first place, she took advantage of Kōenji's suggestion and forced him to focus solely on first place, guaranteeing a real path to victory.
   3. Kōenji secured first place individually, injecting the required 300 class points and allowing Class 2-D to climb the overall standings.
   2. Compliance with the Defensive Axis
   1. Her primary goal wasn't to place in the individual top 10, but rather to prevent any group from Class 2-D from finishing at the bottom.
   2. She remained alone during the exam to move freely and rescue straggling classmates (like Miyake/Hasebe/Sakura's group) or manage crises (like Shinohara's).
   3. Class 2-D finished the exam without any expulsions or losing Class Points due to penalties.

2-) STRATEGY EFFICIENCY:
   * Instead of wearing out the whole class or wearing herself out trying to compete for physical/academic tasks against the whole school, she "outsourced" the offensive performance to Kōenji.
   * She demanded an absolute result (1st place) from Kōenji in exchange for his future freedom. If Kōenji failed, he was contractually obligated to actively participate in the next special exams. This "Win-Win" structure guaranteed performance without Suzune spending class resources to support him.
   * By choosing to be a free agent, Suzune did not consume expensive cards or form large inter-class alliances that required dividing the Class Points obtained.
   * Their support work was funded by obtaining food and supplies through individual tasks, redistributing these resources to vulnerable members of their class without relying on the class treasury.
   * While the other classes were expending massive amounts of energy, points, and interference fighting each other, Suzune kept Class 2-D operating outside the radar of mass conflict for most of the exam, only calling up the most capable for the Cross Year War

3-) STRATEGY EXECUTION:
   1. Phase 1: Analysis and control to save everyone
   1. Following Sae's explanation, Suzune determined that actively seeking top positions by competing in mixed groups with other classes was inefficient, as the Class Points would be divided, reducing the benefit. Therefore, she focused on a high-risk/high-reward strategy internally, as well as damage mitigation:
   1. The first thing she did was send a message to all the students in Class 2-D forbidding them from hastily forming groups before establishing basic rules and reaching a mutual agreement.
   2. Suzune decided not to form any initial groups and remain alone. Her strategy was to act as a reserve player, moving freely around the island, accumulating emergency points, gathering food and supplies, and joining any Class 2-D group if it was in imminent danger of finishing last.
   3. She intervened in internal conflicts to prevent emotional distractions from causing casualties or demotivating key members of the survival team.
   4. In the final stretch, she cooperated with Sakayanagi and Ryūen, forming a united second-year front to protect Kiyotaka from the schemes of first-year student Tsubaki, effectively coordinating the Class 2-D groups.
   2. Phase 2: Offensive
   1. Since Suzune lacked a reliable offensive method to score large amounts of points because her attention was divided on saving her classmates, she seized a crucial opportunity when Kouenji approached her to negotiate his "freedom." Suzune altered Kouenji's original terms to her advantage, creating a win-win situation for the class:
   1. She demanded that, to obtain his absolute freedom and unconditional protection in future exams (such as avoiding being scapegoated in a potential Class Poll), he had to strictly achieve first place overall in the solo exam.
   2. If Kouenji failed and did not achieve first place, he was contractually obligated to actively cooperate and deliver concrete results in all subsequent special exams.
   1. Suzune never promised to protect the Protection Point that Kouenji would earn for achieving first place; she only guaranteed that he would not be expelled, which meant that she could legally still use him as a sacrificial pawn if necessary.
   3. Phase 3: Results and Future
   1. At the end of the two-week exam, Suzune's strategy yielded a resounding result, propelling a massive leap for Class 2-D (which became Class 2-C upon the event's conclusion):
   1. No students from Class 2-D were placed in the bottom ten or expelled.
   2. Suzune managed to stay in the top 50% of the overall individual rankings with a total of 146 points, showing no signs of fatigue and surpassing her rival Ibuki.
   3. Kouenji did his part and secured first place overall. This granted the class 300 Class Points (since they weren't allied with any other classes), in addition to 1,000,000 Private Points and 1 Protection Point, monopolizing the benefits and preventing other classes from obtaining them.
   * Although on the surface it may appear to be an individual victory for Kouenji (who performed 90% of the physical and competitive effort), the strategic reality is that he operated as Suzune's offensive tool. She fortified the class's defense, eliminated the risk of demerit points, and manipulated Kouenji's brilliance to secure the top prize, enabling Class 2-D to accumulate the highest number of points on the exam and close the gap with the upper classes.

4-) STRATEGY RESILIENCE:
   * The biggest internal threat to Class 2-D was always Kōenji's apathy. A traditional plan that relied on his continued cooperation would have collapsed in the face of his refusal.
   * Suzune structured the bet with Kōenji so that even if he failed to achieve first place against colossal rivals like Nagumo, Kiriyama, or Sakayanagi, Kōenji would still be obligated to participate in the next special exam. This strategy absorbed the defeat without any net loss for the class.
   * While Classes 2-A (Sakayanagi), 2-B/2-C (Ryūen/Ichinose) and the 1st and 3rd year students (Nagumo, Hōsen, Tsubaki, etc.) engaged in massive alliances, sabotage, and direct blockades, Suzune kept Class 2-D off the main radar of hostilities.
   * Despite spending two weeks alone on the field, assisting her less capable teammates from group to group, Suzune showed no signs of fatigue and accumulated 146 points on her own, ensuring that her own presence withstood the wear and tear of the environment without incurring penalties.

5-) STRATEGY SHIELDING
   * Before the group stage began and the students acted out of emotion or desperation, Suzune sent out strict directives through Kushida, prohibiting anyone from forming final groups without her prior approval. This prevented weaker students from being trapped in disastrous combinations.
   * Upon being informed of the emotional interference between Ike, Shinohara, and Komiya, Suzune adjusted the group formations to ensure Ike remained focused on survival. This yielded minimal results.
   * Knowing that Class 2-D did not have the financial capital to pay the 6,000,000 Private Points fine in case of expulsion, Suzune dedicated her exam to winning Tasks that granted food and emergency points to redistribute to her faltering classmates.
   * When the first-year class launched the massive attack coordinated by Tsubaki to corner Kiyotaka, Suzune did not ignore the threat. She acted under Sakayanagi's scheme for the second-year forces and actively led the Class 2-D groups to intercept the enemy's movement and protect Kiyotaka.
   * Suzune clearly defined the terms of her contract with Koenji, the promise was to "grant him freedom" and "prevent his expulsion," but she never promised to protect his Protection Point. If Koenji were threatened later, he would be forced to spend his own Protection Point without Suzune breaking her word.
   * While other classes suffered attrition and losses, no group from Class 2-D fell into the bottom 10 risk spots, except for Sakura's, who didn't stay in that top spot for long, allowing Kōenji's 300-point victory to be a pure net gain that catapulted the class from 4th to 3rd place in the class war.

6-) STRATEGY FORESIGHT:
   * Before the exam began, Suzune foresaw that Class 2-D's impulsive nature would lead them to form hasty groups out of panic or emotional attachment, leaving them vulnerable to manipulation by other classes. She issued a direct order through Kushida forbidding the formation of groups without her explicit consent, centralizing decision-making.
   * Suzune acknowledged that Class 2-D had irreplaceable talent gaps. Facing inter-class alliances or the school's academic/physical elite in direct combat alone was tactical suicide.
   * Suzune previously stated that Kōenji felt cornered by the threat of examining direct elimination futures.
   * When Kōenji tried to negotiate a "lazy" effort in exchange for freedom, Suzune anticipated that giving him free rein without a strict metric would not contribute anything to the class.
   * She foresaw that openly proposing an anti-Class 2-A alliance would attract immediate and ruthless hostility from Sakayanagi. Therefore, she decided not to be the public face of any initial coalition, leaving the risk of conflict to other classes.

7-) STRATEGY ADAPTABILITY:
   * Suzune chose to remain without a group, acting instead as a roving rescue force. She traveled from area to area, gathering emergency supplies, provisions, and other necessities to distribute to the weakest or most struggling Class 2-D groups adapting to the island.
   * When Ike's emotional conflict affected internal cohesion and later Shinohara's group was crippled by the accident/attack on Komiya and Kinoshita, Suzune readjusted the structure and negotiated with Sakayanagi to prevent them from ending up in expulsions.
   * During the second week, when the first-year faction launched a coordinated, massive attack against Kiyotaka, Suzune adjusted her stance of passive neutrality. She agreed to act within Sakayanagi's command network, mobilizing the Class 2-D groups as a containment force to protect Kiyotaka without disrupting her class's overall strategy.
   * Suzune kept a constant eye on the leaderboard. She adapted her moves, ensuring she stayed in the top 50% (finishing with 146 points) so as not to be a burden, while keeping all the Class 2-D groups out of the bottom 10, neutralizing the risk of penalty or expulsion.

8-) STRATEGIC ALIGNMENT:
   * Suzune correctly identified a structural limitation of Class 2-D: trying to compete as a united bloc at the top of performance against Sakayanagi's Class 2-A or the third-year elite would dilute their few resources and leave weaker students vulnerable to expulsion.
   * She aligned the defensive stance of the rest of the class with Kōenji's individual ambition. By assuming the role of "floating support alone," she freed the rest of the class from the pressure of taking excessive risks, while redirecting the entire offensive weight of the class onto a single extraordinary vector: Kōenji.
   * Although initially indifferent to the emotional drama, he understood that if he didn't intervene by aligning the group pairs from the outset, the group's defensive performance would collapse. After the Shinohara-Komiya alliance was formed, he immediately reconfigured Ike's group with his close friends to bolster his motivation and prevent a breakdown on the island.
   * The strongest component of her alignment was the structure of the pact with Kōenji:
   * If Kōenji won 1st place: Class 2-D would receive 300 direct Class Points and 1 Protection Point. Suzune would keep her promise to grant him future freedom, but by withholding the Protection Point, she would guarantee the class a lifeline without committing to manually protecting him if he fell into danger in the future.
   * If Kōenji did not obtain 1st place: Kōenji was bound by a verbal contract to actively collaborate in the following special exams.
   * The strategy aligned the interests of a selfish character with the collective goal of Class 2-D, ensuring benefits regardless of the direct outcome of the exam.

9-) TACTICAL COMPLEXITY:
   1. Layer 1: Premature Class Control
   1. Before the exam began, Suzune used Kushida to send direct messages to students with outstanding abilities in Class 2-D, blocking any impulsive formation of inter-class groups without her supervision.
   1. This layer prevented the other classes (A, B, and C) from absorbing the key assets of Class 2-D at low prices, forcing the formation of groups to serve the collective interest of the class first.
   2. Layer 2: Active Safety Net and Anti-Ejection Shielding
   1. Suzune consciously chose not to form a three-person group, instead assuming a solitary role of "field support." She traveled the island assisting weaker groups of her class, accumulating supplies and reserve points for use in emergencies.
   1. By taking on the defensive role herself, she relieved the pressure on the rest of the class, ensuring that no group in Class 2-D fell into the bottom 5 expulsion positions or suffered Class Point deductions.
   3. Layer 3: Manipulation and Activation of Kōenji
   1. Lacking a stable offensive force to compete for first place against massive alliances or elites like Nagumo or Kiryūin, Suzune presented Koenji with an all-or-nothing deal:
   1. If he wanted absolute freedom and protection in future expulsion exams, simply "doing well" wasn't enough; he had to secure first place single-handedly.
   2. If Kōenji won, the class would receive 300 Class Points directly. If he lost, he would be contractually obligated to actively cooperate in future exams. In both scenarios, Class 2-D would win.
   3. If Koenji lost, he would have to collaborate with the class in the future, thus gaining a valuable asset for the future.
   4. All this without guaranteeing that he would save his protection point if the occasion required it, this left Suzune with an escape route to abandon him or force him to use his protection point if the opportunity arose.
   4. Layer 4: Defensive Coordination and Anti-Tsubaki Tactics
   1. During the critical phase of the exam, Suzune acted under the framework of defensive cooperation promoted by Sakayanagi, commanding the forces of Class 2-D to intercept the maneuvers of Class 1-C (Tsubaki) that sought the expulsion of Kiyotaka.

   * Handwriting Identification (Y2V4-Y2V8):

   * Fluency
   * speed (which generates microscopic tremors if slowed down)

   * She knows that stroke pressure (fude-atsu), fluidity, the spatial structure of Kanji, and the sacred order of strokes (hitsu-jun) are executed by muscle memory and will betray the experienced writer.

   * Suzune Cultural Festival Strategy (Y2V5-Y2V7):

   * Suzune approved a business model branched into 4 strategic offerings to diversify revenue while controlling costs:
   * The Maid Café: Strategically located on the first floor of the special building. The café focused its efficiency on selling beverages (coffee/tea) and selling photo rights (Cheki), a service that required minimal time per customer but generated high unit value and a huge profit margin.
   * Outdoor Stalls #1 and #2: Dedicated to food (takoyaki, okonomiyaki, pasta, and bread), these stalls operated independently and provided logistical support for the café.

This self-motivated the class through pride or greed, eliminating the friction of human management, preventing wasted time, and increasing the productivity of the entire class in one fell swoop.

He used the general rehearsal day to conduct tests, thoroughly document the failures, and deliver the corrections to Kiyotaka before the formal opening, ensuring that on the day of the festival everything would run smoothly without any unforeseen weather issues.

5-) STRATEGY SHIELDING;
   * Faced with the latent risk of other classes copying their ideas, Kiyotaka suggested layers of deception, which Suzune agreed to follow. When Ryuuen publicly "leaked" that Suzune's class would be opening a Maid Café and announced direct competition with a Kimono Café, the rest of the school and even Suzune's allies believed it was a devastating betrayal. In reality, Suzune used this as an early marketing ploy, deceiving her own allies in order to successfully deceive her enemies. This fabricated rivalry shielded the project by discouraging other classes from opening similar cafés, focusing customer attention on their conflict.
   * She perfectly identified the internal discord that Hasebe wanted to sabotage the class out of revenge. Instead of confronting her directly or isolating her (which would only reignite the conflict), Suzune tactically shielded her by giving her and Miyake general roles away from the Maid Café's operational center. By not assigning them key tasks, she cut off any avenue they had to carry out a decisive betrayal on the day of the festival.
   * To compensate for the significant weakness of having Kushida in a detached and unmotivated class, Suzune defended her continued presence. She allowed Kushida to secretly practice at night in Suzune's own room to hone her attention and accounting skills, thus protecting the execution front against miscalculations or customer neglect.
   * On the day of the festival, when Matsushita (a pillar of the project) fell seriously ill, the pre-existing organizational structure already contemplated the flexibility of shifts and hidden talents that Suzune recruited with the help of Kiyotaka, preventing the absence from causing a collapse in the service.

6-) STRATEGY FORESIGHT:
   * Possible internal sabotage.
   * Need for extreme secrecy.
   * Risk of copying by other classes.
   * Customer demographic profile.
   * Importance of cleanliness and visual appeal.
   * Possibility of leaks.
   * Probability that Hasebe would perform at the festival.
   * Preventive Mitigation of the Koenji Factor

7-) STRATEGY ADAPTABILITY:
   * On the day of the festival, Hasebe and Miyake's absence was confirmed, along with Matsushita's (one of the project's driving forces) serious illness. Suzune's strategy proved her adaptability, as the shift schedule was designed so that the absence of these three individuals wouldn't jeopardize the café's stability or customer service.
   * Suzune strategically opened the space for Kushida, Hasebe, and Miyake to reintegrate into the team at their own pace. Knowing Kushida was secretly practicing in her room, Suzune adapted on the fly, placing her on the front lines. Kushida not only optimized sales and eliminated accounting errors but also independently devised a plan to bring Hasebe and Miyake back mid-day. Suzune instantly adapted, welcoming Hasebe back and reassigning her as a maid to ease the workload.
   * Seeing that her own reserved personality didn't mesh well with direct customer service inside the café, Suzune adapted her role. She took on outdoor promotions, wearing a maid uniform, turning her natural seriousness into a unique draw for customers.

8-) STRATEGIC ALIGNMENT:
   * She made it clear that those who invested resources or offered winning ideas would receive a direct share of the 390,000 monthly private points derived from the 100 class points at stake.
   * By rejecting mediocre ideas, she aligned the class's pride and greed, forcing them to present plans with cost analyses, staffing levels, and estimates of real clients. This channeled the group's efforts directly toward operational excellence.
   * Although Matsushita, Satou, and Mii-chan's plan consumed almost half of the base budget, she determined that the high profit margin compensated for the initial risk.
   * She decided that the best defense against imitators was not changing the event, but execution. She aligned human resources by assigning Kiyotaka the task of recruiting the most attractive girls and controlling expenses, exploiting the class's individual abilities to surpass any rival. She assigned Hasebe and Akito Miyake general roles far removed from the center of operations under the pretext that they "didn't want to work as maids." By placing them in a peripheral but integrated position, she eliminated any possibility of betrayal and maintained complete control of the situation.
   * Suzune didn't structure the festival around a single, isolated booth, but rather created a business model with four interconnected, mutually supportive components.
   * The creation of the smokescreen focused the attention of the adults and faculty on the "cafe war," monopolizing the emotional interest of the guests. Furthermore, it completely discouraged third-year students from attempting to open similar events, as any latecomer would lack the operational depth and publicity impact of this fictional duel.

9-) TACTICAL COMPLEXITY:
   * The Layer of Fake Conflict and Rivalry Marketing
   * In coordination with Kiyotaka, Ryuuen, and Katsuragi, he designs a school-level staged event where a betrayal and open war are faked between his class's Maid Café and Ryuuen's Kimono Café.
   * The public announcement of a 5 million private points stake between the two classes is another tactical sub-layer designed to generate emotional tension in customers (adults), forcing them to spend more money by perceiving a duel where losing is not an option.
   * The Layer of Inner Deception
   * To ensure that the external deception of the rest of the school was undecipherable, Suzune adds a layer of highly sophisticated tactical complexity: deceiving her own peers and main organizers.
   * By making her own group genuinely believe that Ryuuen betrayed them by stealing their idea, Suzune leverages her class's organic reactions of shock and panic to flawlessly sell the deception to the rest of the school. She knows that if her allies learn the truth, the risk of an actual leak would ruin the strategy, so she accepts the tactical cost of leading her class under a facade of sunk cost stress.
   * The Network Revenue Model
   * At an operational level, Suzune's strategy is not based on a single front, but on a system of four intertwined tactics that generate overlapping mutual support and diversification:
   * The Maid Café acts as the central hub, maximizing time and profit by selling beverages (tea/coffee) and photo sessions (Cheki), which have a high unit value and are its primary source of income.
   * The outdoor light food stalls (Takoyaki/Okonomiyaki and Pasta/Bread) operate independently but also directly support the café. Students transport food orders from outside directly to the Maid Café tables, expanding the menu without overcrowding the physical space. Furthermore, the outdoor stalls give away half-price coupons for the Maid Café, encouraging customers to move toward the main attraction. Not to mention, the free light food stimulates customers' appetites.
   * A children's quiz competition, using the surplus budget, reinforces the strategy by expanding its demographic reach to younger visitors.
   * Preventive Management of Sabotage and Dissent
   * Suzune introduces layers of isolation and containment to neutralize internal threats that could destroy the strategy from within.
   * Knowing that Hasebe harbors a deep grudge and seeks revenge, Suzune neither confronts nor openly excludes her (which would only fan the flames). Instead, she assigns her and Miyake general roles away from the core operations under the logical excuse that they "don't fit the maid profile." This keeps them within the class's control radar, eliminating any tactical opportunity for betrayal or silent sabotage.
   * Rather than marginalizing Kushida because of past conflicts, Suzune defends her and allows her to rehearse intensely in her own room. This adds a layer of flawless execution to the public relations front, using Kushida's charming facade to break down the social barrier with adult male clients in a way that no other student could replicate.
   * El Filtro de Mérito y Activación de Capital Privado
   * The base layer of the strategy, implemented at the beginning of the planning period, consisted of an investment and reward system designed to break the class's apathy.
   * Faced with some students' lack of enthusiasm for funding the initial tests with their private points, Suzune establishes a rule of retribution:
   * Those who risk their resources and submit solid, well-researched proposals (costs, personnel, client projections) will receive a proportional share of the 390,000 monthly points derived from the 100 class points at stake. This fosters internal competition, raises the quality of submitted projects, and protects the class's official budget from unnecessary expenses.

   * Written Comprehensive Cooperative Exam (Y2V9):

1-) STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS:
   * The strategy was entirely effective, as the primary objective was to win the special exam against Class A and obtain the 50 class points. When the results were announced, Suzune's class was confirmed as the winner.
   * Suzune correctly identified that the exam penalized classes with high Academic OAA (Operational Achievement) scores and favored those with students earning D or E grades, since the latter contributed 4 or 5 points per correct answer instead of just 1 or 2. By empowering and guiding this group, she managed to surpass Class A in total points.
   * Despite winning the exam in cumulative score, her class's actual answer accuracy was 72%, falling short of the 86% accuracy achieved by Sakayanagi's class. This demonstrates that, while the mathematical effectiveness of the strategy worked thanks to the OAA point allocation system, in a purely knowledge-based competition under identical scoring conditions, the strategy would have failed.
   * By positioning Kouenji in the last turn of the sequence to solve only the final 2 remaining questions, he effectively mitigated the risk of him ruining the strategy structure, transforming an unpredictable factor into a controlled scenario without serious losses.

2-) STRATEGY EFFICIENCY:
   * Knowing that 10 minutes per student was insufficient to read all 100 questions, she had high-achieving students use their extra time to scan and locate easy problems.
   * To circumvent the ban on talking or leaving handwritten notes, she implemented the use of hand signals during the brief moments when students passed each other in the hallways. This prevented lower-achieving students from wasting valuable time searching for accessible problems within the classroom, allowing them to focus directly on solving the problems.
   * She demonstrated mental efficiency by ignoring the external sabotage tactics employed by Ryuuen and Ichinose, correctly concluding that wasting resources or time on external influences in a purely written exam was inefficient and counterproductive.
   * She organized study groups focused exclusively on students with low OAA scores, led by Hirata, Keisei, and Mii-chan. This maximized the return on the exam's most valuable "resources"—the D/E-rank students who multiplied the score—in just three weeks.
   * By letting Kouenji close the exam with only two optional questions, Suzune chose to sacrifice a potential four points rather than risk wasting time, incurring penalties for disobedience, or disrupting the order of her classmates.

3-) STRATEGY EXECUTION:
   * Phase 1: Focus and emotional stabilization of the group
   * Suzune reviews the exam rules and immediately understands that the key to victory lies not in directly competing intellectually against Class A, but in maximizing the value of the students with the lowest OAA (Academic Abilities D and E), since they contribute more points per correct answer. Noticing her classmates' anxiety about facing an undefeated opponent in purely written exams, she intervenes to calm the class, telling them to focus solely on themselves and not on what Sakayanagi or their surroundings are doing.
   * Phase 2: Deployment of intensive study groups
   * Along with Hirata, Keisei, and Mii-chan, she organizes and leads intensive study sessions specifically for students with the lowest scores on the OAA (such as Shinohara and Ike). To boost the group's morale and motivation, Suzune uses Sudou's remarkable academic progress and the pressure of the end of the term as emotional incentives, managing to get more than half the class to sacrifice their lunch breaks in the cafeteria to stay and study in the classroom.
   * Phase 3: Secure strategic cooperation of key players (Kiyotaka)
   * Suzune meets with Kiyotaka to ask him to actively participate in the special exam. Knowing he has a close relationship with Sakayanagi, she seeks his technical support in dealing with her. She manages to extract a commitment from Kiyotaka that, as her classmate, he will strictly obey any direct orders she gives him during the exam; if she orders him to answer high-risk or difficult questions correctly, he will do so with absolute precision, without making any deliberate mistakes.
   * Phase 4: Neutralization of the unpredictable factor (Kouenji)
   * Aware that Kouenji's unpredictable behavior could ruin the entire plan, Suzune speaks with him beforehand. Having no guarantee that he will take the exam seriously, she places him last in the turn sequence. Her tactic is to have the class complete 98 of the 100 available questions before his turn, leaving him with only 2 questions. This way, if Kouenji decides not to answer or leaves the question blank, the point loss (a mere 4) is minimal and doesn't violate any school rules, completely eliminating the risk of internal sabotage.
   * Phase 5: Sending the official order and preparing the stage
   * The night before the special exam, Suzune formally hands Chabashira-sensei the sheet detailing the exact order in which each of the 38 students in her class will enter the testing room. This action solidifies the logistical basis of her relay plan.
   * Phase 6: Implementation of the alternating system and sign language in the exam
   * On the day of the test, Suzune puts her strategy into action, alternating high-achieving students with lower-achieving ones. The tactic is executed as follows:
   * She opens the exam with Yukimura (Academic Ability), instructing him to quickly answer his two required questions and dedicate the remaining time exclusively to reading and analyzing the other 100 problems. Since talking, taking notes, or using cell phones are prohibited, Suzune takes advantage of the brief moment when the departing student passes the incoming student in the doorway. She devises a system of hand signals so that the advanced student can visually indicate to the lagging student which exact problem number to solve (for example, crossing their fingers twice to indicate question 55, or showing six and then nine fingers for 69).
   * This prevents students with lower OAA scores from wasting 10 minutes searching for accessible problems, reducing their anxiety and allowing them to focus solely on answering correctly.
   * Phase 7: Consolidation of the result
   * Thanks to this tactical deployment, Suzune's class compensates for its lower overall accuracy (72% vs. Class A's 86%) by fully exploiting the low OAA point multiplier system, managing to surpass Class A in total score and consolidating the final 50-point class victory.

4-) STRATEGY RESILIENCE:
   * Suzune understood that the exam rules disadvantaged classes with high academic OAA (like Class A), since students with lower grades (levels D and E) awarded more points per correct answer (4 and 5 points respectively). She designed her strategy to maximize scores by exploiting this mathematical asymmetry of the exam.
   * The strict 10-minute time limit per student created the risk that lower-achieving students would become overwhelmed searching for easy questions among the 100 available and make mistakes due to the pressure. By ordering the sequence by alternating high- and low-achieving students, she allowed the more capable to use their extra time identifying simple problems, relieving stress and wasted time for those who followed.
   * Instead of giving in to student fatigue from recently finishing final exams, Suzune channeled Sudou's progress and the psychological pressure following Sakura's departure to maintain high motivation. She led study groups focused exclusively on students with lower OAA scores, managing to get more than half the class to sacrifice their lunch breaks to study.
   * When Kiyotaka informed her about Ryuuen's sabotage of Ichinose's class, Suzune decided to ignore the external conflict and not intervene. She critically determined that trying to cover more than necessary on a purely written exam would be counterproductive, maintaining absolute focus on her own class's internal preparation.

5-) STRATEGY SHIELDING:
   * The biggest vulnerability in Suzune's strategy was Kouenji, who refused to confirm whether he would take the exam seriously. To prevent him from disrupting the order or making mistakes on a whim, she devised a solution: placing him last in the class sequence. By that time, the class would have already completed 98 of the 100 questions. If Kouenji decided to leave his two questions blank or not put in any effort, the point penalty would be negligible (4 points), and it would be recorded as "unanswered" rather than "failed," without violating any rules. Furthermore, she convinced him by appealing to his own financial interests (private points).
   * Since the rules strictly prohibited talking, using notes, memos, or cell phones to give hints, the strategy of alternating students would seem pointless. Suzune thoroughly analyzed the rules and confirmed beforehand that giving instructions on which problem to solve (without revealing the answer) did not constitute cheating. She devised a system of hand signals for the brief moment when students crossed paths entering and leaving the classroom (e.g., showing certain fingers to indicate the question number), compensating for the impossibility of verbal communication and guiding lagging students directly to the easier questions.
   * Recognizing that Class A had never lost a purely written exam and that Sakayanagi would have a plan, Suzune shielded her class's mindset by demanding they not worry about the opponent's strategies or about Sakayanagi, calming the group's anxiety.
   * Suzune neutralized the risk of highly difficult questions by engaging Kiyotaka beforehand. Although he refused to devise a strategy, she extracted a formal promise from him that he would cooperate on the exam by flawlessly solving any complex question, regardless of the subject, if she commanded him to do so.

6-) STRATEGY FORESIGHT:
   * She understood that the 10-minute limit per person would cause students with lower grades to feel pressured, trying to read and find easy problems among the 100 questions. Anticipating this, he devised a strategy where high-achieving students would solve the difficult questions first or filter the field.
   * She anticipated that the remaining time bonus was designed to favor the class with the highest OAA, so he strategically decided not to rely on it to secure victory.
   * She identified that the rules prohibited talking or giving hints about the answers, but did not prohibit indicating which question to answer. Noticing that students briefly crossed paths when entering and leaving the classroom, he devised a system of hand signals, anticipating that it was something the school would not punish.

This way, the class would have already completed 98 of the 100 questions. If he chose to leave the remaining two questions blank, the impact would be limited to just 4 points (since he was a B-rank student), and they would legally count as "unanswered" rather than "failed," completely eliminating the risk of him ruining the strategy with deliberate mistakes.

   * Suzune immediately identified that the reverse scoring system based on the OAA (where D and E ranks awarded 4 and 5 points per correct answer, respectively) naturally disadvantaged the high average academic level of Class A. Her entire strategy was aligned around maximizing the correct answers of her lowest-scoring students to surpass her rival's point limit.
   * She determined that seeking the remaining time bonus was an unnecessary and counterproductive risk, especially for slower reading students. She aligned the class's efforts to disregard the speed bonus and use the 10 minutes per person exclusively to ensure correct and calm answers.

All the group's motivation and psychological pressure (including the impact of Sakura's recent expulsion) was channeled directly into making these students take responsibility for their success.

All the group's motivation and psychological pressure (including the impact of Sakura's recent expulsion) was channeled directly into making these students take responsibility for their success.

9-) TACTICAL COMPLEXITY:
   * Exploitation of the Scoring System
   * Suzune identified that the exam awarded more points per correct answer to students with the worst scores on the OAA (a D or E level student would award 4 or 5 points, compared to the single point awarded by an A level student).
   * She understood that, because of this, Class A had a strict limit on the maximum points they could earn due to their high academic average.    * Since there was no guarantee that Kouenji would participate seriously, Suzune designed the sequence to deliberately place him last in the class order.
   * The tactic stipulated that the 37 previous students would each answer exactly 98 of the 100 available questions. By leaving Kouenji with only the last two questions (the minimum required by the regulations), Suzune completely eliminated the risk of him answering capriciously, spoiling key problems, or making costly mistakes in the bulk of the exam. If he chose not to answer, the maximum loss would be only 4 points, which wouldn't critically affect the result.
   * Alternating Dynamics of Order and Visual Signal System
   * The exam gave a limit of 10 minutes per student, insufficient time to read and understand the 100 questions in search of the simplest problems, which would cause panic and mistakes in the lagging students.
   * Suzune opened the exam with Keisei Yukimura (OAA Level A). Her instruction wasn't to answer as many questions as possible, but rather to quickly solve his two required problems and then use the remaining time to scan and locate the easier problems in the rest of the test for the lower-achieving student following behind. The overall plan was to alternate high-achieving students with low-achieving students.
   * Although the rules strictly prohibited talking, leaving notes, or using phones, Suzune found a loophole in the brief moment when a student leaving the classroom crossed paths in the hallway with the student waiting to enter. She devised a code of hand signals (for example, showing two open palms to indicate question 55, or combinations of fingers for question 69).
   * With this tactical overlap, the advanced student indicated to the low-performing student exactly which question number to go to, saving them reading time and allowing them to immediately concentrate on solving calmly.
