   * Kiryuuin Trial (Y2V9):

1-) STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS:
   * Identification and exposure of the true culprit (100% accuracy); Using the clues provided by Kiyotaka and her own logic regarding the inconsistencies in the case, she accurately deduced that Kiriyama was the mastermind behind the attempt to frame Kiryuuin.
   * Neutralizing Kiriyama through his own confession: Knowing he lacked irrefutable physical evidence (following his philosophy of not acting without absolute certainty), he orchestrated a scenario of psychological pressure within the Council itself. He used the threat of summoning the pawns (Yamanaka and Tachibana) as bait, forcing Kiriyama to break down and confess publicly before everyone present.
   * Drastic limitation of Nagumo's power: By ruling that from that moment on, the private points collected by the third-year students should be used exclusively for their own benefit, Suzune took away Nagumo's immense capital for manipulating exams and buying favors.
________________
   * Consolidation of her authority as President: She successfully resolved her first official case on her first day in office, earning the immediate respect of the first-year council members, Kiryuuin, Kiriyama, and Nagumo himself.

2-) STRATEGY EFFICIENCY: Suzune manages to solve the case using extremely limited resources. She employs:
      * Reporting the incident officially to school authorities would have triggered lengthy investigations, strained the third year, and resulted in severe punishments that would have damaged the Student Council's reputation.
      * By resolving the matter internally and imposing a voluntary one-week suspension on Kiriyama, they contained the scandal without losing control of the situation.
      * Suzune didn't waste time researching on her own from scratch. She took the data collected by Kiyotaka and Asahina and immediately connected it with her own prior knowledge of Nagumo and Kiryuuin's habits to structure her entire mental map.
      * Instead of confronting Kiriyama alone in private, she held the trial in a room full of brilliant minds. By skillfully guiding the conversation and getting everyone to use their intellect to corner Kiriyama as a group, she multiplied the pressure on him without having to exhaust herself in a one-on-one debate.
      * The threat to summon the key third-year witnesses was extremely effective. It avoided the tedious bureaucratic process of locating them and bringing them to testify. He used that simple threat as the final trigger for Kiriyama to surrender on the spot.
      * Furthermore, she avoids actually summoning Yamanaka and Tachibana. The threat of summoning them is merely a psychological tactic. This means that she:
      * solves the case in a single meeting
      * without bureaucratic procedures
      * without a serious external investigation.

3-) STRATEGY EXECUTION: Suzune's execution is extremely structured. Her tactical process follows clear phases:
      * Phase 1: Information gathering and initial deductive analysis.
      * Before the session begins, Suzune analyzes the logical inconsistencies in the case, such as Nagumo's lack of motive for attacking Kiryuuin and Kiriyama's unusual scheduling error. Upon receiving Kiyotaka's report on the third-year contracts and Tachibana's intervention, Suzune deduces that Kiriyama is the mastermind behind the operation. True to her philosophy of not acting without absolute certainty, she devises a tactical plan to force him to confess within the council chamber.
      * Phase 2: Setting the stage under a false ignorance.
      * Upon entering the student council room, Suzune formally assumes the chair and begins the session by acting as if she knows nothing about the background of the case). This action is a deliberate tactic to ensure that everyone present shares the same information base and to avoid revealing her strategy to Kiriyama from the outset.
      * Phase 3: Exposure and establishment of the central contradiction.
      * Suzune formally questions Kiryuuin and Nagumo, requiring them to present their statements in front of the council witnesses. By having Kiryuuin support the accusation against Nagumo, and Nagumo defend himself by demonstrating that he had no personal gain from incriminating her, Suzune publicly validates the existence of an insurmountable contradiction, establishing the grounds for the conclusion that a third party manipulated the situation.
      * Phase 4: Forced involvement of the main suspect.
      * Suzune goes directly to Kiriyama to ask for his opinion as vice president and Kiryuuin's classmate. Kiriyama falls into the trap by suggesting that they avoid further investigation and close the matter with a simple apology and compensation from Yamanaka. This proposal, aimed at preventing the school from finding out and declaring Nagumo innocent, allows Suzune to assess how the suspect is trying to deflect attention and close the case quickly.
      * Phase 5: Deployment of confidential information.
      * Suzune introduces into the debate the key details provided by Kiyotaka, including the third-year student's subservient network and Tachibana's role as an intermediary for Nagumo's alleged orders. With these elements in hand, she logically argues that Yamanaka and Tachibana aren't lying, but were instead deceived by someone powerful and trusted enough to act as Nagumo's spokesperson, thus narrowing the accusations directly to Vice President Kiriyama.
      * Phase 6: The final bluff to break the culprit's resistance.

This psychological pressure causes visible panic in Kiriyama, leading Nagumo to intervene and allow the witnesses to enter, destroying Kiriyama's defense and forcing him to openly confess his entire sabotage plan.
      * Phase 7: Sentence issuance and consolidation of political power.
      * Following Kiriyama's confession, Suzune assumes her role as judge, imposing a mandatory apology and a voluntary one-week suspension in his dormitory. She indirectly holds Nagumo responsible for the contract management and forbids her from using third-year private points for personal whims in the future, neutralizing her largest financial resource and ensuring absolute control of the campus on her first day as president.

8-) STRATEGIC ALIGNMENT: In this case, Suzune's objective from the moment she receives the information from Kiyotaka is clear: to consolidate her authority as the new president, resolve the Kiryuuin conflict without involving the school, and neutralize Nagumo's absolute financial control. Suzune aligns her strategies as follows:
      1. Before entering the trial, Suzune connects the key pieces provided by Kiyotaka: the third-year submission contracts, the emotions of dissatisfaction with the grade, and the distorted flow of orders (Nagumo → Tachibana → Yamanaka). She identifies the linear contradiction (Nagumo denies the orders, but his pawns are convinced they come from him) and deduces the only intermediary with enough power and trust to act as a spokesperson: Vice President Kiriyama.
      2. She doesn't act impulsively based solely on her deduction. Instead, she aligns the order of the testimonies in ascending order: she begins with the complainant (Kiryuuin), moves on to the main defendant (Nagumo), questions the suspected witness (Kiriyama), and supports her logic with the opinions of Kushida and Nanase so that the entire courtroom moves in the same direction.
      3. By irrefutably exposing Kiriyama, she manages to perfectly align the results:
      1. She brings justice and closure to Kiryuuin.
      2. She prevents the school authorities from intervening (which would have had serious and uncontrollable consequences).
      3. She leverages Nagumo's "management responsibility" to impose an unprecedented restriction:
      1. Prohibiting the arbitrary use of third-year students' private points, thus openly dismantling the former president's greatest power.

9-) TACTICAL COMPLEXITY:
      * Layer 1: Information Management and Neutralization of the Ayanokōji Factor
      * Upon entering the room and seeing Kiyotaka, Suzune immediately realizes that Nagumo has drawn him into a dispute. When Nagumo reveals the anonymous voting competition for the presidency, Suzune doesn't focus on the surface (the election itself), but rather on the hidden threads: she deduces that there's a private points bet involved and that Nagumo is seeking a "spectacle" for mere meritocratic entertainment.
      * Although she initially planned to cede the position to Ichinose (knowing that a popularity campaign would be ineffective), Suzune agreed to formally compete because she understood the "message between the lines": Nagumo held absolute power at that time, and direct opposition was futile. This allowed her to gain access to the presidential platform and legitimacy for what would follow.
      * Layer 2: Simulation of Ignorance
      * As the formal session begins, Suzune sits in the presidential chair and acts as if she knows absolutely nothing about Kiyotaka's prior investigation or the details of the theft. This charade forces Kiryuuin and Kiriyama to present the official narrative from the outset, compelling everyone present (including the first-year students) to synchronize their information on the same page.
      * Suzune decides to accept two mutually exclusive premises as "true":
      * Nagumo categorically states that he did not give the order.
      * The pawns (Yamanaka and Tachibana) firmly assert that they were obeying Nagumo's orders.
      * Instead of dismissing one version as a lie, Suzune introduces variable "X":
      * someone with sufficient authority and the trust of the third-year students to falsely present themselves as Nagumo's direct emissary.
      * Layer 3: Collective Manipulation and Concurrence of Geniuses
      * Suzune doesn't interrogate in a linear fashion. She uses the minds of those present as analytical tools. She asks questions directed at Kiriyama (to gauge his level of cynicism and his attempts to close the case quickly with compensation) and at Kushida (who logically deduces that Yamanaka is protecting the real culprit despite the fear Nagumo inspires in him).
      * By having highly intelligent figures analyze the case in real time, the logical net closes exclusively around Kiriyama without Suzune having to directly accuse him from the start.
      * Layer 5: The Witnesses' "Bluff"
      * True to her historical philosophy of not acting without absolute certainty, Suzune needs the culprit to betray himself.
      * Suzune formally announces that, using her powers as Student Council President, she will summon Yamanaka and Tachibana to the hall immediately. This triggers Kiriyama's immediate panic, a micro-reaction detected by the elite students in the room. Seeing Nagumo support Suzune's motion to bring the witnesses, Kiriyama realizes his web of lies is about to physically collapse and is forced to confess.

Personal ranking for the best strategy categories in this feat:
Effectiveness > Shielding >  Tactical > Foresight > Alignment > Adaptability > Execution  > Efficiency  > Resilience

      * Koenji Neutralization Strategy (Y2V10):

1-) STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS:
      * Suzune took a calculated risk by leaving Koenji unprotected in round 4 (when he already had two mistakes and was on the verge of elimination). Her intuition proved correct: Koenji wouldn't put his fate in the hands of others and would respond correctly to save himself, giving her points in the process
      * She tests Koenji's Protection Point as a resource to be used, indirectly warning him that if he doesn't defend himself, he could get hurt, and getting him to stop depending on her (as in Y2V5) by removing the pressure of his promise
      * To demonstrate that Koenji would play seriously if cornered, Suzune attempted to set a trap for Class A. If Sakayanagi continued to nominate him without Suzune using a protection, Koenji would succeed and grant automatic defense points to his class. However, this specific trap proved ineffective against Sakayanagi. She immediately deciphered Suzune's strategy and stopped nominating Koenji in round 5 to avoid "giving" him points. Although Sakayanagi read Suzune's intentions, the objective of neutralizing the attacks on Kouenji was fully achieved. From turn 5 onward, Kouenji's name disappeared from Class A's target list. This benefited Suzune, who could then use her five protections on those who needed them.

2-) STRATEGY EFFICIENCY:
      * Absolute preservation of protective slots (Resources)
      * In a 20-turn exam where the class has only 5 protection slots per round to defend 37 students, permanently allocating a slot to Kouenji would have reduced their actual defensive capacity to only 4 slots.
      * By not protecting him in turns 1, 2, 4, and 5, Suzune saved vital defensive resources, allowing her to use those free slots to protect other vulnerable students or those who needed to keep their motivation high (such as Keisei Yukimura or Ken Sudou).
      * Utilizing the Kouenji Protection Point as an alternative resource
      * Instead of depleting the class's collective resources by protecting him, Suzune structured the strategy so that the cost of any failure would fall on Kouenji's own individual resource (his Protection Point). This minimized the potential damage to the rest of the group at zero cost to the class.
      * Management of the psychological factor and reaction time
      * Although Suzune's lack of immediate explanation created tension and suspicious glances in her own class, this opacity was necessary for reasons of efficiency. Had she openly explained the plan to her classmates, Kouenji would have discovered her damage control strategy. The silence allowed him to execute the move quickly and in real time without interference.

3-) STRATEGY EXECUTION:
      * Phase 1: Setting the Scene and Narrative Induction
      * At the start of the exam, Suzune determines that a student is only eliminated after accumulating three incorrect answers. Based on this, she calculates that Kōenji is not in immediate danger of expulsion until he makes at least two mistakes in a row.
      * In order not to waste her protection slots (limited to five per turn) on a high-achieving student from the very first round, Suzune consciously decides not to protect Kōenji in Turn 1 and distributes the protections among colleagues with a higher risk of failure.
      * Phase 2: Tolerance of Kōenji's disinterest and group containment
      * During Turn 2, Kōenji is nominated by Class A. True to his apathetic attitude, he hands in his answer sheet blank, making his first mistake.
      * Faced with Kōenji's inaction and her classmates' discontent, Suzune maintains a firm and composed demeanor. Showing no signs of weakness or agitation, she prevents panic or disunity from spreading within the group, stabilizing the class's atmosphere while actively gathering information in her notebook and on her phone.
      * Phase 3: The assumption of extreme risk and the execution of the "pressure trap"
      * In the fourth defensive round, Sakayanagi raises the difficulty of the "Gourmet" category to level 2, seeking to corner Kōenji (who has already accumulated two failures and is on the verge of elimination).
      * Despite common logic dictating that she should protect Kōenji to keep her promise not to let him be expelled, Suzune decides not to include him on the list of protected students. With this, she executes a plan with a double purpose:
      * Knowing that Kōenji is extremely intelligent and will not want to ruin his own destiny or lose his personal "protection point" obtained on the deserted island, Suzune corners him to force him to answer seriously.
      * He intends to tempt Sakayanagi to continue attacking Kōenji, thinking he is unprotected, with the expectation that Kōenji will respond correctly and thus give his class easy defense points.
      * Phase 4: Success in activating Kōenji and the dilemma of the protection point
      * The pressure pays off. Backed into a corner and without Suzune's shield, Kōenji is forced to write for the first time, correctly solving the Gourmet Level 2 problem and saving himself from elimination.
      * Behind her apparent "betrayal" of her promise to protect Kōenji, Suzune had a trick up her sleeve that she didn't reveal to the class to prevent Kōenji from finding out. Kōenji possessed an individual "Protection Point." In the event that her class came in last place and Kōenji was eliminated, Suzune planned to force him to use up this point to avoid expulsion. Since she had never promised to protect Kōenji's "point" (only his continued enrollment at the school), the strategy guaranteed absolute safety for the rest of the class without technically breaking her promise.
      * Phase 5: Psychological deterrence and exam stabilization
      * Although Sakayanagi manages to decipher Suzune's intentions after Turn 4 and decides not to nominate Kōenji again in Turn 5 so as not to give Suzune any points, the main objective is successfully achieved.
      * By demonstrating that Kōenji will respond seriously when under real threat of elimination, Class A identifies him as a useless and high-risk target for wasting attacks. Suzune manages to definitively neutralize nominations against Kōenji for the remainder of the round, winning the psychological bet and ensuring her class's stability without expending valuable protection resources on him.

4-) STRATEGY RESILIENCE: Suzune has a contract that obliges her to protect Koenji, which means she doesn't have one of the 5 slots when fighting against other classes.
      * Suzune knew that Kouenji possessed great academic ability and keen intuition, but also a free spirit that would prevent him from willingly putting in the effort. She endured him handing in blank exams in the early rounds (accumulating two mistakes and nearly being eliminated) without panicking or giving in to the pressure to protect him. On turn 4, when the difficulty of the "Gourmet" genre reached level 2 and Kouenji was just one mistake away from being eliminated, Suzune firmly maintained her decision not to protect him. This resistance forced Kouenji to take the pen and answer correctly himself to avoid the risk, proving the soundness of her prediction.
      * In the worst-case scenario (if Kouenji had failed and been eliminated), the strategy would still be immune to the exam's expulsion rule. Suzune planned to force Kouenji to consume the "Protection Point" he had earned during the deserted island exam. This way, even if the class came in last place and Kouenji was the candidate, expulsion would be avoided without harming the other students.

5-) STRATEGY SHIELDING:
      * The biggest weakness of having to look after Kouenji was that it reduced her protection slots, leaving her with only 4 slots to protect the other 37 students in the class. Suzune mitigated this weakness by interpreting her promise literally: she promised him "freedom" and "not being expelled," but never promised to safeguard his personal Protection Point. By not protecting him, she freed up the 5 slots for vulnerable students.
      * Sakayanagi knew of Suzune's promise to Kouenji thanks to leaks from Hashimoto, and planned to use the "Gourmet" genre to force Suzune to spend turns protecting him. Suzune actively countered this by not protecting him, turning Kouenji into a "trap." If Sakayanagi attacked him, Kouenji would be forced to respond well to save himself, automatically granting Suzune's class defense points.
      * Upon realizing this protection, Sakayanagi understood that continuing to attack Kouenji would only benefit Suzune. Therefore, on turn 5, Sakayanagi completely avoided nominating Kouenji. Suzune managed to stop the opponent from attacking her most troublesome piece without having spent a single protection resource on it.
      * Suzune didn't explain her tactic to her classmates despite their distrustful glances and the internal tension. She knew that if she revealed her plan to force Kouenji to use his Protection Point, he would have realized the trick long before and avoided falling into the trap.

6-) STRATEGY FORESIGHT:
      * Before the critical rounds begin, Suzune carefully analyzes the terms of the protection and freedom pact she has with Kouenji. Her foresight allows her to decipher that, although she is obligated to prevent his expulsion from school and allow him to act as he pleases, the exam rules offer her a loophole: Kouenji possesses a personal "Protection Point" obtained during the deserted island exam. Suzune anticipates that, should her class place last, she can legally use Kouenji's Protection Point to nullify any expulsion, formally keeping her word without needing to expend her limited exam resources.
      * Suzune anticipates Kouenji's psychology and egocentrism. She knows that if she grants him unconditional protection from the first turn, he will continually submit blank sheets and contribute nothing. Therefore, she foresees that the only way to force him to participate seriously is to leave him unprotected just when he is on the verge of elimination (with two accumulated failures). She accurately calculates that Kouenji will consider losing his personal Protection Point "too troublesome" and will be compelled to answer correctly out of pure self-interest.
      * Suzune anticipates that Class A will try to exploit Kouenji's perceived weakness. Her long-term plan involves deliberately leaving Kouenji exposed with an invisible "shoot me" sign to lure Class A into attacking him on his own turf (the Gourmet genre), transforming her class's apparent vulnerability into a source of free defense points thanks to the correct answers Kouenji would be forced to give.

7-) STRATEGY ADAPTABILITY:
      * At the start of the exam, the ideal plan dictated that Suzune should permanently spend one of her five protection slots on Kouenji, limiting her defense to only four teammates per turn. Demonstrating a high degree of adaptability to the rules, Suzune decides not to protect him in the first round, assessing that with zero mistakes, Kouenji is not in immediate danger of elimination. This allows her to dynamically free up her five slots to protect students who are genuinely struggling with the genre.

8-) STRATEGIC ALIGNMENT: In this exam, Suzune faced a serious initial dilemma: a prior agreement with Kouenji in which she promised to grant him freedom and prevent his expulsion from school. This threatened to permanently waste one of her five protection slots on him during the 20 turns, leaving her with only four for the other 37 students.
      1. Suzune figures out that the only way someone can be expelled is if the class comes in last place and the leader has to nominate them from among the eliminated students. She also remembers that Kouenji possesses a Protection Point earned during the deserted island exam.
      2. Her promise stipulated that she wouldn't expel him and would let him act freely. During the exam, Suzune didn't impose any restrictions on him (thus upholding his freedom). Regarding expulsion, Suzune reasons that she never promised to protect his Protection Point. If Kouenji were to be eliminated and the class came in last place, she could nominate him for expulsion, and Kouenji would be forced to use his Protection Point to save himself. In this way, no one in the class would be expelled, and the promise would remain intact.
      3. Suzune knows that Kouenji will maintain a "1% uncertainty" and won't blindly leave his fate in anyone's hands when he's on the verge of elimination (2 failures).

9-) TACTICAL COMPLEXITY:
      * Layer 1: Surface Appearance Layer
      * Outwardly and in front of her own teammates, Suzune appeared to break her promise to protect Kouenji by leaving him unprotected in the crucial round of the Gourmet genre (Difficulty 2), when he had already accumulated two failures for handing in blank sheets.
      * Suzune deliberately chose not to explain her true intentions to her classmates to prevent Kouenji from discovering her plan. She accepted the temporary cost of her classmates' distrustful glances and the apparent loss of credibility to keep the tactical trap intact.
      * Layer 2: Everything else
      * Sub-Layer 2.1: Direct Psychological Layer
      * Suzune knew that even if someone is guaranteed 99% safety, people always retain 1% doubt.
      * By refusing to waste a protection space as if he were "a baby", Suzune forced Kouenji to take the pen to avoid being eliminated.
      * Technically, Suzune kept her promise to "let him act freely" (by not imposing any restrictions on him) and avoided spending a permanent protection slot, freeing up that slot to defend other vulnerable students in the class.
      * Sub-Layer 2.2: Financial Shield Layer/Expulsion
      * Suzune's promise was to "prevent Kouenji from being expelled," not to "protect his Protection Point."
      * If her class finished in last place and Kouenji was the only one eliminated, Suzune planned to force him to spend the Protection Point he earned on the deserted island exam. This way, the class wouldn't be expelled if they lost the exam.
      * It made the wound of a hypothetical defeat much less deep, removing Kouenji's personal protection before he could cause further harm to the group through his lack of cooperation.
      * Sub-Layer 2.3: Quick Trap Layer to the Enemy Class
      * By leaving Kouenji on the verge of elimination without protection, Suzune symbolically set a target for Kouenji that said "shoot me".
      * Suzune anticipated that Class A would spend their points to aggressively attack an unprotected Kouenji in Gourmet Level 2. Since she knew that Kouenji would respond seriously in order not to be eliminated, Kouenji's correct answers would have granted Suzune's class free defense points at the expense of Sakayanagi's resources.
      * Sub-Layer 2.4: Information and Counter-information Layer
      * Class A believed they had the advantage because they knew (through Hashimoto's information gathering) about the prior agreement between Suzune and Kouenji.
      * Class A attacked assuming Suzune would be forced to protect Kouenji. By breaking this pattern and not protecting him, Suzune completely dismantled the tactical scheme Hashimoto had tried to set up, throwing the attackers into confusion.

      * Five Reachers strategy (Y2V10):

1-) STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS: Implicit objective:
      * The strategy failed to achieve its primary objective. Ryuuen rejected Suzune's bait. Instead of devouring Ichinose, Ryuuen secretly made a pact with her in the second half, gifting her points to propel her to second place without any eliminations.
      * Suzune secured third place with 59 points (surpassing Sakayanagi's Class A, which finished fourth with 53 points). By leaving Class A in last place, Suzune minimized the damage to her own class and forced a loss for their direct rival. However, she failed to achieve her goal of reaching first place (which Ryuuen took with 69 points) or second place (Ichinose with 62 points), so Suzune herself considered the final result a frustrating defeat.

2-) STRATEGY EFFICIENCY:
      * By deciphering the hidden intentions behind Ichinose's attacks in the final phase, Suzune was able to optimize the use of her five protection slots per turn. Knowing in advance who would be nominated thanks to information shared by Ichinose, she successfully protected several of her teammates without wasting protections on guessing. This prevented mass eliminations and blocked the rival classes' point gains.
      * The exam required coordinating and processing data from over 80-100 students in real time via mobile phones. Suzune demonstrated high operational efficiency by quickly noticing anomalies in Ichinose's behavior, establishing direct communication before it was too late, and adjusting her class's defensive posture to ensure they didn't fall to last place.
      * By finishing in 3rd place, Suzune's Class B suffered a -50 class point penalty. However, it wasn't the worst-case scenario (4th place deducted 100 points and carried the risk of expulsion).
      * During the first half of the exam, Suzune lost three students (Sotomura, Ijuuin, and Hondou) due to three incorrect answers. However, by avoiding last place at the end of the exam, she completely mitigated the maximum risk: no students from her class were expelled.

3-) STRATEGY EXECUTION:
      * Phase 1: Setting the Scene and Narrative Induction
      * Immediately after Chabashira explains the rules of the exam (attack/defense turns, nominations, points, and penalties for expulsion), Suzune takes the floor to ask key questions about the leader's role.
      * Confirm that the leader is exempt from being nominated for tasks (not at risk of direct elimination) and that eliminated students can remain in the classroom actively participating in discussions and analysis.
      * Phase 2: Tactical Manipulation of Class C
      * During the first half of the exam (turns 1 to 10), Suzune executes a systematic plan focused on Honami Ichinose's protective psychology.
      * When attacking Class C, Suzune deliberately limits the number of students with two failing grades (Reachers) to a maximum of five. Instead of creating a sixth Reacher, she diversifies her attacks to include students without failing grades.
      * Knowing that Ichinose will desperately protect those five Reachers to prevent anyone from being expelled, Suzune weakens the rest of the class by forcing them to accumulate a strike. This leaves Class C in a position of massive vulnerability, handed to Ryuuen (Class D), who desperately needs points in the second half, to "devour" them by attacking the unprotected students.
      * Phase 3: Maintaining margin and neutralizing losses in the first half
      * Suzune absorbs the elimination of three of her students (Sotomura, Ijuuin, and Hondou) during turns 8 and 10, but manages to place her class in 2nd place with 28 points (only 1 point behind Sakayanagi's Class A).
      * At the end of shift 10, he assesses the state of the exam. Seeing no signs of prior alliances between other classes (which would have required coordination from the beginning to mitigate the exam's randomness), he assumes that the individual class dynamics will continue.
      * Phase 4: Adapting to Ryuuen's rejection and reading signals
      * Suzune quickly deciphers that Ichinose's repeated attacks are not aggressive, but an encrypted message to provide Class B with defense points.
      * She demonstrates adaptability and initiative by being the one who takes the initiative to contact Ichinose directly to coordinate a common front.
      * Phase 5: Consolidation of the anti-Class A emergency alliance
      * Ichinose reveals to Suzune in advance who she will nominate for her attack turns.
      * With this inside information, Suzune uses her five protection slots with 100% effectiveness, securing vital defense points. The objective of the execution becomes mitigating damage: blocking Class C's attacks, preventing Class A from scoring points, and securing at least third place to avoid the risk of expulsion from her classroom, pushing Class A to last place.

4-) STRATEGY RESILIENCE:
      * Suzune had assumed that an effective inter-class alliance could only form from the start of the first half due to the exam's randomness. However, the presence of a traitor in Sakayanagi's Class A allowed Ryuuen to eliminate randomness, filter attack targets, and form a late alliance with Ichinose in the second half. This completely shattered Suzune's anticipated scenario.
      * Although their macro-strategy for first place was dismantled, the core structure of their strategy didn't crumble to the point of destruction. It withstood the onslaught long enough to prevent their class from falling to last place or suffering catastrophic expulsions, safely securing third place thanks to all the points earned in the first part of the test.

5-) STRATEGY SHIELDING
      * Noticing that Karuizawa was being nominated repeatedly by Ichinose in an unusual way during the second half, Suzune didn't dismiss it as a mere coincidence. She realized that Ichinose was sending a direct message to give them points.
      * Suzune took the initiative to contact Ichinose to coordinate defenses in real time. By receiving the Class C nominees list in advance, Suzune secured crucial defense points for her class. This acted as a buffer, preventing Sakayanagi's Class A from pulling ahead and protecting Class B from elimination.

6-) STRATEGY FORESIGHT:
      * Suzune devised an offensive play where she intentionally limited the number of students on the verge of elimination to exactly five. She anticipated that this would force Ichinose to consistently use all five of his defense slots on those same five students each turn, preventing him from protecting other members or changing tactics.
      * Suzune predicted that the margin of victory would be between 50 and 55 points, and that if Ryuuen aggressively attacked Ichinose, Class B would maintain its one-point advantage and secure the victory or direct passage to the top without direct attrition against Class A.
      * Suzune assumed that an alliance between two rival classes (like Ryuuen and Ichinose) was only viable if it was forged during the first half of the exam due to the randomness of the questions. She failed to foresee that Ryuuen would find a traitor within Class A (Hashimoto) to leak Sakayanagi's objectives. This leak eliminated the element of randomness and allowed Ryuuen and Ichinose to form a late and unexpected alliance in the second half of the exam.

7-) STRATEGY ADAPTABILITY:
      * In the second half of the exam, when Ryuuen refused and began to give points to Ichinose to elevate her to second place, Suzune quickly identified that there was a secret pact between the two.
      * Seeing that her original plan to be first had collapsed due to the Class A data leak, Suzune immediately pivoted her victory strategy to one of damage mitigation:
      1. She noticed that Ichinose was systematically nominating Karuizawa as a veiled message/lifeline.
      2. She proactively contacted Ichinose to form a tactical counter-alliance in the final stretch.
      3. Upon receiving the advance list of nominees from Ichinose, Suzune used her slots to successfully protect her students, slow Sakayanagi's advance, and secure third place.
      4. This emergency adaptation prevented her class from falling to last place and ensured that Sakayanagi's Class A finished at the bottom of the leaderboard, minimizing class point losses as much as possible.

8-) STRATEGIC ALIGNMENT:
      1. Suzune perfectly aligned the well-known personalities and philosophies of the other leaders:
      1. Suzune realized that Honami Ichinose would never allow a classmate to be expelled or eliminated.
      2. Suzune knew that Ryuuen's class, currently in last place after the first half of the exam, would desperately need a massive influx of points in the second half to survive and have a chance at first place.
      1. Suzune aligned both trends to create a strategic line where, by wearing down Ichinose's Class (leaving them vulnerable), she "served Ichinose up on a silver platter" for Ryuuen to devour her in the second half, wearing each other down and opening the direct path for Suzune to fight Sakayanagi's Class A.
      2. When the dynamics of the exam changed in the second half due to the pact between Ryuuen and Ichinose, Suzune instantly aligned her actions with Ichinose's hidden intentions:
      1. Noticing that Ichinose was repeatedly nominating certain students (like Karuizawa) to send an implicit message and give her points, Suzune contacted her.
      1. They aligned their defenses to coordinate who would be nominated, ensuring that the classes ranked second and below would consistently accumulate defensive points, shielding themselves from expulsion and effectively dragging Sakayanagi's Class A down to last place.

9-) TACTICAL COMPLEXITY:
      * Layer 1: The Trap of the "5 Reachers" and the Armantization of Ichinose
      * Suzune exploited Honami Ichinose's altruistic and unwavering philosophy to neutralize her class without needing a brute-force confrontation:
      1. By targeting Class C, Suzune intentionally kept the number of students about to be eliminated at exactly five.
      2. Knowing that Ichinose would never allow a classmate to be eliminated for fear of expulsion, Suzune forced her to spend her five protection slots exclusively on those same five "Reachers" each turn.
      3. By concentrating Ichinose's defense on those five "Reachers," the rest of Class C was left completely unprotected against one-fail questions. This made Class C the classroom with the highest number of students with one accumulated fail in the entire exam, even without having eliminated anyone yet.
      * Layer 2: "boiling Ichinose at Ryuuen's Table
      * Suzune intertwined Layer 1 with a geopolitical maneuver designed to direct Ryuuen's attacks without having to negotiate directly with him:
      1. Reaching the halfway point of the exam (Turn 10), Ryuuen was in last place with only 19 points and needed to score massively (at least 30 points in the second half) if she wanted to survive or win.
      2. Having left Class C full of students with one missed shot and blocked defenses, Suzune left Ichinose's class wide open for Ryuuen to devour in the second half and quickly recover points.
      3. This would keep Ryuuen and Ichinose occupied and pitted against each other, allowing Suzune to concentrate all her second-half attacking resources exclusively against Sakayanagi's Class A to fight for first place.
      * Layer 3: The Anti-Class A Alliance
      * When the board turned upside down due to unpredictable factors, Suzune demonstrated reactive tactical complexity:
      1. She noticed that Ichinose was systematically nominating specific individuals from his class (like Karuizawa) as a "lifeline" or implicit message.
      2. Suzune took the initiative to contact Ichinose by phone to coordinate defenses in real time.
      3. She transformed the communication into a pragmatic alliance to pass information about the nominees in advance, ensuring that both Class B and Class C would protect each other and guarantee that Sakayanagi's Class A would fall to last place.

      * Kanzaki Control (Y2V12):

        1-) STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS:
      * Suzune achieved a crushing 7-0 victory. She completely depleted Kanzaki's life bar while keeping her own 7 lives intact.

2-) STRATEGY EFFICIENCY:
      * She sacrificed 0 lives out of his 7 available. In terms of the game's direct "lives”, the efficiency was at its maximum.
      * She resolved the battle in just a few rounds, taking advantage of Kanzaki's quick surrender after the initial four-life hit. This spared her a prolonged time drain or uncertain rounds before her duel with Ichinose.

3-) STRATEGY EXECUTION:
      * Phase 1: Preparing the Ground and Assembling the Elite Group
      * Suzune pre-plans the lineup of participants for the fight against Class D by selecting calm, bold students with a good ability to maintain a "poker face" and an intuition for reading the room (such as Ken Sudō, Akito Miyake, Chiaki Matsushita, and Kayano Onodera). The tactical objective of deploying this high-level group from the very first discussion is to take control of the flow of the debate, force Kanzaki to doubt himself, and compel him to adopt a purely defensive stance from the outset.
      * Phase 2: Dismantling
      * During the first discussion, Akito Miyake falsely claims to be the Graduate and accuses Norihito of being an Honor Student. Suzune deduces that Miyake is actually an Honor Student seeking personal gain. While Kanzaki falls for the deception and mistakenly nominates Norihito, Suzune directly nominates Miyake as an Honor Student. This action instantly costs Kanzaki four lives. Taking advantage of the commotion caused by Miyake's departure, Suzune notices that Yui Ninomiya is visibly shaken. Recognizing this subtle sign as confirmation that Ninomiya is the second Honor Student, Suzune promptly nominates her before Kanzaki can react, winning the first argument decisively and leaving Kanzaki with only 3 lives remaining while keeping her own 7 intact.
      * Phase 3: Exploiting the Opponent's Mental Collapse
      * During the break between discussions, Kanzaki approaches Suzune, swallowing his pride and bowing deeply, pleading with her to concede the victory to prevent the gap with Class A from becoming insurmountable. Suzune mounts an unyielding mental block: she judges Kanzaki's attitude to be disrespectful to Ichinose and the exam. Maintaining a cold and inflexible demeanor, she flatly rejects the plea, denying him any possibility of negotiation or diplomatic compromise.
      * In the following rounds, Suzune observes that Kanzaki completely loses focus, becomes apathetic, and begins to skip his turns consecutively without making any nominations. Seeing that her original plan (to keep him on the defensive) is no longer necessary because her opponent has abandoned the match, Suzune stands up in the middle of the argument to confront him, suppressing any hint of personal sympathy so as not to let her guard down.
      * Suzune concludes the execution of her strategy by eliminating the co-owner of the Class D Ichinose without receiving a single point of damage, closing the duel with a flawless score of 7-0 in her favor.

4-) STRATEGY RESILIENCE:
      * She withstood Kanzaki's psychological pressure by firmly rejecting his request to concede victory.
      * She endured the extreme mental fatigue caused by the isolation and structural disadvantages of the exam, actively pursuing a tie until the very last moment.

5-) STRATEGY SHIELDING
      * She neutralized the psychological pressure and desperate plea of ​​the rival strategist to secure his class advantage.

7-) STRATEGY ADAPTABILITY:
      * She adjusted his game strategy upon noticing Kanzaki's apathy and repeated passes, abandoning the initial defensive stance to execute a direct offensive and quickly end the confrontation.

8-) STRATEGIC ALIGNMENT:
      1. She achieved a perfect 5-0 victory against Hamaguchi without taking any damage, employing a strategic approach that completely neutralized one of the top students in the opposing class.
      2. She secured a resounding 7-0 victory against Kanzaki by anticipating his defensive play, rejecting his psychological appeals, and effectively exploiting his misjudgments.
      3. She successfully structured a balanced first discussion group composed of calm, expressionless students, immediately taking four lives from Kanzaki by correctly discerning her own partner's role.

      * D Class Upgrade (Y1V1-Now):

1-) STRATEGY EFFECTIVENESS:
      * Suzune's central goal has always been to reach Class A. To achieve this, she divided her goal into four pillars:
      * Avoid losing class points due to misconduct or exams.
      * Raise the academic, physical, and mental level of the class.
      * Build a cohesive unit.
      * Neutralize internal/external threats and minimize expulsions.
      * Concrete Achievements and Results:
      * They started Year 1 in the first month with 0 class points (compared to Class A's 940). By the end of Year 2, after the final exam, the class had achieved official first place (Class 2-A) with 1,233 points.
      * In the initial exams, the lowest scores put several students at risk of expulsion. By Year 2, the minimum passing grade for the class was raised to 53 points, eliminating the risk of failing due to simple academic deficiency. The most outstanding case is Sudou, who went from being a problem student with elementary school-level skills to being in the Top 10 academically in his generation.
      * She managed to transform the "worst group in ANHS history" (divided, apathetic, and individualistic) into a disciplined force capable of executing complex plans.
      * She became the Student Council President, which guaranteed a passive flow of class points, access to privileged information, and institutional authority to protect her class.
      * She managed betrayals and interference adaptively by exposing and eliminating Yamauchi, neutralizing Kushida's sabotage until she usefully reintegrated her, and handling the traitor Maezono when she threatened internal cohesion, even without knowing of her existence.
Despite initial setbacks in Year 1 (such as falling into Ryuuen's traps at the Sports Festival or the VIP leak on the cruise), Suzune's effectiveness lay in her adaptability. She adjusted her strategy to stop fighting alone, acknowledged her intellectual limitations against natural geniuses, and transformed each defeat into a learning experience to avoid repeating mistakes.

2-) STRATEGY EFFICIENCY:
      * She understood that the key to leadership wasn't doing everything alone, but rather bringing the class together. She leveraged Sudou's athletic prowess, Yukimura's analytical skills, Hirata and Karuizawa's social leadership, and Kushida's extensive network of contacts.
      * During UIE, she made a strategic bet with Kouenji. She demanded first place in exchange for her future freedom in subsequent exams. This ensured the class's victory and a Protection Point without having to invest class resources to support him.
      * She knew when to rely on Kiyotaka and when to push him through bets (like the partner exam) to force him to reveal some of his potential without disrupting his peaceful life. She used him as the class's most valuable asset to secure crucial victories without exhausting other members.
      * Instead of spending private points or relying on external help, she organized internal tutoring systems before each major exam. This prevented expulsions and raised the overall average with minimal resource costs.
      * To preserve Kushida for her long-term value (social and academic skills), he decided to sacrifice Airi Sakura, the lowest-performing asset in the OAA.
      * She temporarily broke the class's trust, causing absenteeism, resentment, and doubts about her leadership. It took months of effort, public apologies, and the support of Kiyotaka, Hirata, and Sudou to heal the rift. Nevertheless, from a purely utilitarian point of view, she preserved a high-value resource (Kushida) in exchange for a dispensable one (Sakura).
      * To secure Kiyotaka's participation as a General and win the exam against Ichinose's class, Suzune accepted unequal conditions and signed a clause stipulating that Kiyotaka would withdraw his support from the class until graduation. She achieved the desired outcome (reaching Class A), but at the cost of permanently losing her most powerful strategic asset.

3-) STRATEGY EXECUTION:
      * Phase 1: Operational Diagnosis and Damage Minimization Tactics
      * After the initial shock of discovering the true workings of the S-System on May 1st and seeing Class D reduced to 0 points, Suzune implements her first direct tactics. Lacking social authority or financial resources (private points) to execute complex maneuvers, she focuses on passively reducing penalties through three basic rules:
      * eliminate tardiness
      * cut off classroom disruptions
      * ensure no one fails the midterms.
To this end, she conducts a test study group with the "trio of idiots." When the group fails tactically due to the students' apathy, Suzune coldly calculates that expelling the worst performers is the most direct way to "plug the point leak." However, after interacting with Kiyotaka, she readjusts her operational approach because she understands that each student is a potential asset and that getting rid of them would create an irreversible deficit. She redesigns the tactic to provide them with personalized instruction on the essential content to save them from failing.
      * Phase 2: Legal Resolution, Crisis Containment and Initial Foreign Alliances
      * Faced with Sudō's burgeoning conflict with Class C, the strategy shifts to a procedural approach to avoid suspension and the resulting massive loss of class points. Suzune mounts the defense at the hearing, dismantling the opposing testimonies. Simultaneously, she uses the opportunity to forge a formal non-aggression pact with Ichinose, successfully shifting Class B's strategic focus to Class A and reducing their direct conflicts to only Ryūen's Class C.
      * Phase 3: Adaptive Containment and the Search for the Internal "X" Factor
      * During the first high-stakes special exams, Suzune's strategic execution stumbles due to her own limitations in vision and internal control. On the island, she attempts to execute a defensive plan of point conservation and concealment of the leader while suffering from a 38°C fever, a collapse ultimately resolved by Kiyotaka. On the Cruiser, although she manages to neutralize the collusion between Classes B and C thanks to their prior alliance, an internal leak prevents her from gaining points. The breaking point occurs at the Sports Festival, where she falls directly into the trap orchestrated by Kushida and Ryūen. The lesson of this phase reshapes her tactical execution as she realizes that her selfish desire to catch up with her brother blinded her and that she lacks the absolute individual talent to win alone. From this turning point onward, Suzune accepts that she must build a genuine support structure within the class.
      * Phase 4: Development of Collective Assets
      * Suzune radically transforms her strategy, shifting from a defensive approach to a personnel restructuring. She initiates Sudō's comprehensive training through continuous private tutoring, transforming the class's biggest academic burden into an academically competent and loyal athlete. For the Paper Shuffle exam, she employs one of her most important tactics by establishing standardized study groups that raise the overall average, relying on the academics of Class B to compensate for her weaknesses. She also contractually binds Kushida through a gambling game to limit her potential for sabotage. This continues her attempt to integrate Kushida into the class as an asset, later at the Mixed Training Camp (Y1V8), where she strategically assigns the less communicative students (like Mii-chan) to groups with Class B, while using Kushida's "good girl" facade as a spokesperson to strengthen cohesion and prevent demerit.
      * Phase 5: Assuming the Role of Moral Executor and Consolidating Leadership
      * With the Class Survey exam, the class structure collapses into paranoia and intrigue fueled by Sakayanagi and Yamauchi to expel Kiyotaka. Suzune executes a politically high-risk intervention: she voluntarily assumes the role of "moral enforcer." She publicly exposes Yamauchi's betrayal, dismantles Kushida's manipulation network, rescues Kiyotaka from expulsion, and redirects the group's hatred toward a justified exit, earning the class's respect and undisputed leadership after Hirata's emotional breakdown. With her leadership consolidated, she faces Class A in the Event Selection Exam, where she designs the event selection to maximize competitive potential, neutralize systemic disadvantages, and protect the class by either nominating herself or managing the Commander's risk. Although they fall due to external interference (Tsukishiro) and Kōenji's apathy, Suzune has created a class that stops its self-destruction and shows unprecedented competitive strength.
      * Phase 6: Breaking Up Alliances and Restructuring
      * At the end of the first year, during spring break, Suzune analyzes the numbers and unilaterally ends her alliance with Ichinose. With 275 points accumulated and a strengthened class, Ichinose's Class B goes from being a useful partner to a direct obstacle to reaching Class A. In the Partner Exam, she guides the class without suffering any casualties or capital losses, neutralizes the Hōsen threat thanks to Kiyotaka, and executes the academic wager maneuver against Kiyotaka. This tactic serves a dual purpose: to force him to reveal some of his true abilities in a controlled environment and to condition his integration into the class. In the Deserted Island Exam, Suzune adopts a purely defensive tactical stance to avoid last place, but she makes a key deal with Kōenji: she demands absolute first place in exchange for her future freedom in subsequent exams. This move allows her to win the test, securing her promotion to Class 2-C without expending class resources to support her.
      * Phase 7: Political-Institutional Conquest and the Dilemma
      * The most critical moment occurs during the Unanimous Voting Exam when Kushida sabotages the unanimity by seeking someone's expulsion by a mere 100 points and revealing everyone's secrets. Suzune makes the coldest decision of her career. She refuses to expel Kushida, valuing her skills and network of contacts for the upperclassman war, and redirects the expulsion to Sakura. Although Kiyotaka accepts the verbal execution, branding Sakura as the lowest-ranking in the OAA, Suzune bears the political and moral cost of the internal rift. During summer vacation, Suzune expands her strategy to the institutional sphere. She proposes operational improvements to Nagumo for the treasure hunt on the cruise ship, gaining prestige with the school administration. Following Ichinose's resignation from the Student Council, Suzune strategically recruits Kushida (to force her reinstatement and leverage her social network) and first-year Nanase, assuming the Student Council Presidency. This gives the class a passive flow of points, access to information, and political power over the school.
      * Phase 8: Repairing the Social Fabric and Maximizing Academic Achievement
      * Suzune spends the following months healing the severe crisis of confidence caused by Sakura's expulsion. At the Cultural Festival, she definitively recruits Kushida by offering her a truly useful role, manages the class positions, and implements a temporary alliance with Ryūen to win first place. Academically, she institutionalizes intensive mandatory tutoring weeks before each exam. The tactical impact is massive: the class's academic floor rises dramatically, leaving the lowest grade at 53 points (Hondō) compared to the historic 14 at the start of Sudou, further fueled by the latent fear of being the next "OAA sacrifice." In the cooperative written exam, Suzune independently devises a cross-answer strategy where higher-scoring students transfer points to lower-scoring ones, defeating Sakayanagi's Class A without Kiyotaka's strategic intervention by gaining class points.
      * Phase 9: Intelligence Management, Coping with the Final Exam, and Transition to Full Independence
      * During the Survival and Elimination Exam, Suzune formally assumed the role of leader of the second-year Class B after logically dismantling the objections of the traitor Maezono and establishing strict anti-leak protocols. She forced Kōenji to compete seriously, using his Protection Point as a safety net for the group.

Furthermore, she creates a strategy in which everyone can contribute ideas. This strategy operates on multiple levels—both individual and collective. Instead of imposing a plan and merely issuing orders, she fosters an environment where everyone can contribute, learn, and improve under her guidance. This reduces reliance on just one or a few exceptionally talented students. Every student has the opportunity to offer ideas from their own unique perspective. Even initially flawed ideas may contain useful elements that, once refined, can be transformed into viable solutions. Moreover, Suzune identifies errors, inconsistencies, and weaknesses within the proposals; by refining the students' ideas, she enhances the quality of the overall plan. Additionally, by integrating diverse perspectives, they are able to construct more comprehensive strategies. This not only supports the approach taken for the cultural festival and the "Survival Dropout Exam," but also teaches the students to consider a range of possibilities and solutions. The variety of proposals provides alternatives for unforeseen situations, ensuring the class is not solely dependent on a rigid, singular strategy. Because the students have participated in the development of the plan, they better understand the reasoning behind the decisions; this allows them to execute the plan with greater insight and coordination, preventing a scenario where they might otherwise follow instructions blindly without grasping their purpose—ultimately making them more effective. Furthermore, by taking part in the planning process, the students feel a sense of shared responsibility for the outcome. This boosts their willingness to collaborate, carry out the strategies, and strive for victory—serving as a powerful motivator.

Finally, in the End-of-Year Exam, she faced an extremely unfavorable scenario and the prior leak of Kiyotaka's position to Ichinose's class. Suzune accepted Kiyotaka's demand that she withdraw all future cooperation if she had to intervene, took the lead in the standings, suffering devastating attrition against Ichinose, allowing Kiyotaka to secure a 10-0 victory. With the subsequent expulsion of the traitor Maezono and the class's promotion to Class 2-A, Suzune organized an integration meeting to solidify the group.

8-) STRATEGIC ALIGNMENT:
      1. Starting with 0 class points, Suzune understood that the direct path to Class A was not through great strokes of luck, but through building a path of sustained and cumulative growth.

Summary

Summary        1
Planning Categories        2
● Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6):        2
● Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10):        7
● Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12):        12
Summary
________________
Planning Categories
      * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6):

Planning Formulation -

Step 1: Analyze the Special Exam System

Suzune began by carefully evaluating the rules of the sports festival. She identified the critical elements: the mandatory minimum of five participations, the maximum of ten events per student, the penalty for absence, and the difference in scoring between individual and team competitions. She understood that the system rewarded not only athletic ability but also the effective management of human resources. This structural analysis formed the basis of her entire plan.

She realized that her class started with a numerical disadvantage compared to Ichinose's or Ryuuen class, which meant an automatic gap in points simply for participating. Instead of ignoring this difference, she incorporated it into her projections. She understood that they would need to compensate with multiple strategically distributed first-place finishes.

She also determined that Sakayanagi's class represented the greatest strategic risk, not only because of their academic and organizational capabilities but also because of their ability to devise counter-strategies. Before thinking about maximizing points, she decided that she had to reduce the operational capacity of the most dangerous rival.

She not only read the exam questions themselves, but also the complications within her class. Several students were absent, and her own decisions had fractured the class. Suzune had to focus on winning the sports festival while her entire class was in disarray.

Step 2: Rebuilding the Class

Before developing any strategy, she decided to address the internal rifts directly. She rebuilt group cohesion using Hirata to manage tensions, and successfully reintegrated Kushida as an active resource instead of keeping her as a destabilizing element. This decision strengthened the internal structure and recovered human capital that would be crucial for group competitions and strategic maneuvers.

Step 3: Forming a Coalition with Ryuuen

Accepting the reputational risk and the implicit mistrust, Suzune opted for a formal alliance with Ryuuen's class. This decision was not emotional but structural: joining forces increased the likelihood of neutralizing Sakayanagi and preventing internal competition that would fragment points. The formal contract eliminated the risk of spontaneous betrayal and solidified cooperation for the next exams.

Step 4: Neutralize Sakayanagi

Suzune accepted a controlled cost: Kiyotaka's absence and the loss of 10 points, in exchange for guaranteeing Sakayanagi's non-participation. She immediately recalculated the projections under this new scenario. This was a preemptive move: reducing the rival's command capacity before the event even began.

Step 5: Design the Individual Competitions Distribution

To avoid wasting points competing against allies and Ryuuen class, she organized with Katsuraagi the entries so that her class and Ryuuen's class would minimize direct confrontations in individual events. This way, they maximized their chances of securing top spots without canceling each other out.

She established clear limits: each student could only participate in a maximum of three members of the class in the group events. This prevented the over-saturation of top athletes like Sudou. Furthermore, she balanced the number of participants between the two allied classes and agreed on a point distribution to maintain mutual benefits.

Suzune understood that relying solely on physical talent was a fragile strategy. By imposing participation restrictions, she ensured sustainability throughout the day. Her planning aimed not only to win events but also to maintain consistent performance until the end.

Although a primary alliance existed, she did not prohibit situational cooperation with other classes if circumstances demanded it. This prevented strategic rigidity and allowed her to maintain flexibility in the face of unexpected developments.

Step 6: Execute with Early Impact

Suzune decided to personally compete in key events at the start of the festival. By winning the 100 meters and the hurdles, she generated an early advantage and psychological momentum. This early leadership boosted class morale and solidified the coherence of the designed plan.

Short Term Planning - The sports festival plan is, structurally, a short-term plan. The event lasts only one day, and the preparation phase covers a brief period before the exam (one week). Suzune demonstrates high competence in short-term planning because she:
      * Quickly interprets the rules of the system.
      * Reorganizes her class within a limited timeframe.
      * Formalizes an alliance before the event.
      * Adjusts projections following Sakayanagi's absence.
      * Designs the exact distribution of participation.
It's not just about reacting, but about structuring a complete plan within a compressed timeframe. Early execution (victories in the first events) confirms that the plan was ready before the exam began. In this short period, these are the strategies and tactical actions that Suzune specifically outlined in the days/weeks leading up to the exam to ensure her class's immediate victory in the event:
      * Creation of the "Elite Group" for Kushida's Reintegration and Internal Stabilization:
      * In the days leading up to the festival, Suzune meticulously planned Kushida's reintegration. She identified that she needed an unpredictable, external third party to break through Kikyou's defenses, deducing that Ibuki was the perfect fit. Anticipating Ibuki's refusal, she prepared a direct incentive (offering to negotiate pre-registrations for the festival). She manipulated the conversation so that Ibuki herself would propose the three personal duels, giving Suzune the leverage she needed to bring Kushida back in time.
      * Alignment Strategy with Kouenji:
      * A few days before the event, she approached Kouenji to confirm his financial motivation. Knowing that he would only be competing individually to obtain private points and a Class Transfer Ticket, Suzune planned the class structure, anticipating that he would secure between 10 and 55 guaranteed individual points.
      * Class Cooperation Pact:
      * Knowing that her class was at a 20-point disadvantage due to having only 38 students compared to Ichinose's 40, she planned a private meeting at a karaoke bar with Ryuuen and Katsuragi. She brought Kiyotaka along as a deterrent and to exert psychological pressure. She formulated a joint plan to dominate the team events, ensuring that 1st and 2nd place in the festival would be reserved for Class B (Suzune) and Class C (Ryuuen), thus breaking Sakayanagi's (Class A) monopoly.
      * Event Participation Management:
      * She designed a participation schedule in advance to prevent the burnout of key athletes (like Sudou) by imposing a contractual limit of a maximum of 3 team events per person. Furthermore, she strategically distributed her teammates across core disciplines to accumulate the minimum 5 participation points per student (ensuring a base score of 380 points) and maximize first-place finishes in individual and team events.

Long Term Planning - These are the far-reaching visions that Suzune compiled and whose preparation at the Sports Festival serves as a basis for the class's future towards Class A:
      * Justification and Transformation of Kushida into a "Weapon" for Class A:
      * The plan to save Kushida in the Unanimous Voting Exam wasn't a momentary impulse, but a long-term vision spanning the three years of high school. Suzune planned to retain Kushida's academic and athletic abilities to compensate for Sakura's absence. During the Y2V6 negotiations, she demonstrated to Kushida how she could use her mask and outstanding performance to rise to Class A and clear her name before graduation.
      * Managing Political Positioning and Class B Value:
      * For the past few months, Suzune's class had been viewed as a "flawed class" with no negotiating power. Suzune's long-term plan was to elevate her class's reputation and image so that rivals like Ryuuen and Katsuragi would be forced to treat them as equals at the negotiating table, allowing her to forge future strategic alliances without sacrificing any personal points.
      * Risk Management of the Class Transfer Ticket (Kouenji):
      * She foresaw the long-term risk that Kouenji or other students might accumulate enough resources to drop out of the class using the class transfer ticket offered to the first-place winner of the festival. She kept her protection contract with Kouenji active to discourage him from leaving for rival classes before graduation.
      * Building Autonomy Led Without Dependence on Ayanokouji:
      * As a long-term growth goal, Suzune designed her plan for the Sports Festival, assuming full responsibility as the leader. She used Kiyotaka only as an objective evaluator or a deterrent presence in negotiations with Ryuuen, independently handling internal conflict resolution and formulating the operational plan. She sought to avoid dependence on Kiyotaka.
      * Accepting the Emotional and Political Cost:
      * Aware that Sakura's expulsion would leave lasting scars for months (such as Hasebe's resentment and threats of revenge), Suzune incorporated the impact of the emotional toll into her long-term planning. She resolved to endure the cold stares and hostility of her classmates without wavering, focusing on delivering concrete results to logically validate her leadership in the eyes of the entire class over time.

Planning Intricacies - Suzune's plan for the Year 2 Sports Festival is a multi-level strategy comprised of 4 interconnected strategic layers.
      1. Layer 1: Internal Class Control
      1. Before performing any numerical calculations, Suzune understood that a fragmented class would ruin team competitions. She couldn't devise an external plan if her base had collapsed after the Unanimous Voting Exam.
      1. Suzune began the meeting by publicly apologizing, standing with her head bowed, taking full responsibility for breaking her promise regarding the expulsion. By absorbing the hostility of Shinohara and the class without flinching, she dampened the discontent and stabilized the minimum discipline required to move forward.
      2. Tactically supported by Hirata's previous intervention and Matsushita's recommendation to keep secrets quiet, she contained gossip towards rival classes.
      3. Suzune approached Kouenji directly to confirm his motivation. Deciphering that he would only compete in individual events to earn money, she calculated that Kouenji would contribute between 10 and 55 points on his own without needing to be integrated into the fragile team dynamic.
      2. Layer 2: Psychological Manipulation and External Infiltration
      1. Kushida wouldn't open the door to anyone in her class, and their absences would cost her vital points. Unable to use her own resentful classmates, she devised an indirect strategy using an external element:
      1. She looked for someone outside the class who could break down Kushida's defenses, who had no social connections to filter Kushida's true personality, and who was completely unflappable. Ibuki fit the bill perfectly.
      2. To convince Ibuki, Suzune used the Sports Festival prerogative (up to 5 events) as an incentive.
      1. Suzune wanted to guarantee exactly three direct matches against Ibuki to avoid draws (which would happen with two or four matches).
      2. Knowing that if she initially proposed three, Ibuki would demand five, Suzune feigned reluctance when offering the opportunity to fight, letting Ibuki negotiate and impose the number of three duels.
      3. Ibuki kicked Kushida out. Once inside, Suzune applied a strategy of persuasion in stages:
      1. she disarmed Kushida's victimhood
      2. cornered her by appealing to her childish pride
      3. finally logically and strategically reassembled her, showing her that returning as an "honor student" who accepts her faults was her only form of functional survival and revenge.
      3. Layer 3: Mathematical Micro-Optimization of Regulations and Risk Matrix
      1. Suzune broke down President Nagumo's festival regulations to identify structural flaws in the system and use them to her advantage:
      1. Class B had 38 students, starting with an automatic 20-point deficit compared to Ichinose's class (40 students). Suzune calculated exactly how many first-place wins in basic skills (at least 4 extra) they needed to neutralize the disadvantage.
      2. Suzune analyzed the registration time windows. For example, registering in the first group of the 100 meters (10:15 a.m.) allowed her to compete and then be immediately free to accumulate consecutive competitions, optimizing fatigue and time performance.
      3. She prohibited hyper-competitive groups from saturating individual events. She avoided relying solely on athletes like Sudou to prevent burnout (limit of 10 events) and distributed members according to their abilities and club restrictions.
      4. Layer 4: Building the Alliance
      1. To break the hegemony of Class A, Suzune planned a strategic bilateral coalition.
      1. She dismissed Class A as the rival to beat and rejected Ichinose due to his lack of physical strength. She chose Ryuuen despite the risk of betrayal, relying on Katsuragi as the rational anchor.
      2. She brought Kiyotaka to the karaoke meeting not to lead, but as a deterrent. His mere presence projected an invisible threat onto Ryuuen, enhancing Suzune's perceived value and negotiating authority.
      3. When Ryuuen tried to blackmail her by demanding compensation in Private Points or bringing up past grievances, Suzune remained unfazed. She showed a genuine willingness to walk away from the table and break the deal, forcing Ryuuen to give in and accept a 50/50 agreement based purely on collective ability.
      2. Contractual conditions of the Alliance:
      1. Equalizing participants in team events to distribute points equitably between both classes.
      2. Establishing a limit of a maximum of 3 team competitions per student to prevent "stacking" or overloading of star athletes.
      3. Adjusting schedules to prevent allies from eliminating each other in individual competitions.
      4. Approving flexible alliances with other classes at appropriate times.
      3. The final link in the planning architecture required neutralizing the most dangerous strategist on the exam.
      1. Suzune realized that Sakayanagi, unable to compete physically due to her condition, would likely retreat to the dormitories to avoid damaging her pride by marching with a cane. This meant that Class A would not only lose their leader on the field but also suffer an automatic loss of 10 participation points.
      2. Suzune accepted the deal with Kiyotaka where he would guarantee 100% Sakayanagi's absence in exchange for removing him from the festival planning.
      3. With Sakayanagi out and Class A reduced to 37 effective students, the numerical disadvantage shifted to Sakayanagi, opening the perfect door for the crushing final result.

Planning Aim -
      * Suzune's primary goal at the festival was to achieve 1st place overall for her class (which granted +150 Class Points). Despite starting with structural disadvantages and severe internal crises, the goal was achieved exactly 100%, resulting in the final standings: 1st Suzune Class (+150), 2nd Ryuuen Class (+50), 3rd Ichinose Class (0), 4th Sakayanagi Class (-150).
      * With only 38 students, her class started with a 20-base-point disadvantage compared to classes with 40 students. To solve this problem, Suzune pre-arranged the participation of key athletes to ensure individual first-place victories that would precisely close that gap.
      * She understood precisely that she couldn't organize the teams or move forward with the festival strategy if the class remained divided after the Unanimous Vote Exam. She strategically resolved the problems of Kushida and Hasebe's absences and contained Shinohara's resentment to stabilize the class at the minimum level necessary to compete collectively.
      * She identified the worst-case scenario as Class A (Sakayanagi) winning the festival. She managed to isolate Sakayanagi by agreeing to a strategic trade with Kiyotaka, eliminating her most dangerous tactical rival and taking 10 points away from Class A.

      * After confirming that Kouenji would actively compete for the monetary prize, he structured the strategy knowing that he would win individual events autonomously, integrating that free variable within his overall point total.

This allowed them to reserve victories in high-scoring team sports, splitting the points equally and negating the initial numerical disadvantage.

Planning Invincibility -
         * The deal with Ibuki:
