Planning Accuracy -
         1. Alignment and Connection of Internal and External Components
         1. Compensation for the initial numerical handicap:
         1. Suzune's class had only 38 students compared to Ichinose's or Ryuuen’s class of 40, which meant starting the festival with an automatic disadvantage of 20 points (since the mere minimum participation of 5 events awarded 10 points per person).
         1. To bridge this gap without overexploiting her athletes in individual events, Suzune designed an alliance architecture based on the rules of group/special competitions.
         2. Synergy of the Alliance with Ryuuen's Class:
         1. She identified Class A (Sakayanagi) as the common enemy to neutralize in order to prevent them from widening the point gap. Suzune combined the physical strength of her class (Sudou, Kouenji, Onodera, herself, etc) with the athletic arsenal of Ryuuen's class (Albert, Ishizaki, etc.).
         2. They designed the team competitions by dividing the slots exactly 50/50 between both classes. The festival rules stipulated that all members of a winning team would receive the same number of points, allowing both classes to accumulate points simultaneously and on a large scale without penalizing each other.
         2. Rigor in the Details and Clauses of the Alliance Contract
         1. Avoid Direct Collisions:
         1. She coordinated the distribution of individual pre-registrations to prevent the aces of her class from competing against the aces of Ryuuen in the same race blocks, maximizing the chances of both classes getting first and second places in different individual events.
         2. Participation Limit per Person:
         1. She included an explicit clause in the contract limiting each student to a maximum of three group/team events, regardless of their physical ability. This prevented the burnout of key athletes like Sudou or Albert and ensured a more even distribution of effort across the class.
         3. Protection against Sabotage:
         1. She was informed that during the festival she could not use mobile phones for real-time monitoring. Therefore, she formalized the agreement through official student council documentation, eliminating any possibility of direct betrayal or arbitrary withdrawal on Ryuuen's part.
         3. Integration of Disruptive Parts
         1. Monetization of Kouenji:
         1. Suzune analyzed the festival rules (prizes of up to 2,000,000 Private Points for the individual ranking). Knowing that Kouenji was motivated by winning money in previous exams, she accurately predicted that he would compete exclusively in individual events and aim for first place in each one.
         1. She integrated it into her mental spreadsheet as an autonomous and unconditional source of up to 50 to 55 points for the class, without needing to spend resources managing it or forcing it to do teamwork.
         2. Tactical Incentive of Ibuki
         1. To break Kushida's absence, Suzune offered Ibuki the pre-registration for events as a reward.
         1. Suzune wanted three direct confrontations with Ibuki to ensure a definitive outcome. Instead of proposing three matches, Suzune initially acted reluctantly to guide them to negotiate the exact number of three events, thus ensuring that Ibuki carried out the break-in at Kushida's room precisely on her terms.
         3. Kushida's Operational Reintegration:
         1. Suzune didn't plan the festival based on a false sense of emotional "harmony," but rather on a pragmatic survival strategy. She redefined Kushida's usefulness as a high-achieving "Honor Student" who would score key points in individual events and collaborate in recruiting external allies purely out of self-interest in reaching Class A.
         4. Control of Uncontrollable Variables and Rational Exchange:
         1. Sakayanagi Preventive Neutralization:
         1. Suzune calculated that Sakayanagi would not actively participate due to her physical condition (estimating a 60% probability of absence) and that Class A would suffer from a lack of leadership on the field.
         2. When Kiyotaka offered to raise that probability to 100% certainty in exchange for being released from participating in the festival plans, Suzune instantly recalculated the point matrix. She agreed to forgo Kiyotaka's potential athletic contribution in exchange for ensuring the complete invalidation of the Class A strategic core, guaranteeing that the original plan would not encounter unforeseen interference.

Personal ranking for Suzune Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6) categories:
Short Term Planning > Planning Aim > Planning Preparation Process > Planning Coverage >  Planning Accuracy > Planning Process > Planning Intricacies > Planning Versatility > Planning Risk Calculation > Planning Contingency >  Long Term Planning > Planning Invincibility

         * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10):
Planning Formulation -

1. Step 1: Rapid consolidation of leadership and design of the initial operating framework

As soon as Chabashira announced the rules of the Special Survival and Elimination Exam, Suzune demonstrated an immediate ability to structure a plan of action for her class.

In just ten minutes after the start of lunch break, she managed to gather 37 of her classmates in the classroom and devised a process for eating and debating simultaneously, optimizing the limited time available.

Within this tight timeframe, she formulated a strategy to assume the leadership position. Instead of aggressively putting herself forward, which would have generated friction or suspicion after the events of the unanimous exam, she deliberately opened the opportunity to the class. She predicted that no one would want to assume the immense responsibility of potentially being forced to expel a classmate, thus ensuring that the class organically elected her.

When Maezono questioned her suitability, Suzune reacted in a matter of seconds, formulating a logical response, cornering the objection, and immediately solidifying the group's unity. In that same quick session, she formulated essential information security guidelines, prohibiting the use of recordable digital media to discuss strategies in hallways or common areas.
Now in control, she created a system that allows everyone to state their weaknesses in private, in order to prepare for the exam.

2. Step 2: Lightning analysis

By taking control of the class, Suzune developed a strategy in which class members were required to contribute ideas; this not only fostered their independence and helped them unify as a group, but also allowed them to explore their own concepts and broaden their scope of thinking under Suzune’s guidance.

For example, when Ike proposed securing 68 points by simulating illness in 32 students, Horikita didn't dismiss the idea lightly. Instead, she immediately conducted an analytical assessment to evaluate its feasibility.

In a short time, she dissected the plan's mathematics (5 x 20 = 100 - 32 = 68 points) and instantly identified its structural flaws. She formulated a theoretical counter-strategy based on the reaction of rival classes and demonstrated to her group that by establishing a fixed maximum score of 66 to 68 points, the three opposing classes (especially leaders like Ichinose or Sakayanagi) would immediately notice the anomaly and collaborate with each other.

By achieving only a 34% success rate on average-difficulty tasks against a small, unresponsive class, the rivals would easily surpass the 67-point mark. Horikita transformed a tempting proposal into a demonstrable risk model, concluding that it was a losing plan 9 out of 10 times and redirecting the group's behavior toward legitimate academic preparation.

She did the same with the subsequent proposals her classmates brought her, drawing inspiration from each one for her own plan.

3. Step 3: Formulation of the opening movement and data measurement probe

Starting the first half of the exam with the advantage of attacking first and having only 3 minutes to communicate his decision to teacher Sakagami, Horikita used her pre-planned opening strategy. Lacking prior information on the actual difficulty of the school's questions, he devised a low-risk, high-return strategy.

First, she selected the "English" subject at Difficulty Level 1 and nominated five students from Class C with below-average academic performance (Kobashi Yume, Watanabe Norihito, Amikura Mako, Ninomiya Yui, and Shibata Sou). This lightning-fast plan aimed to test Class C's success rate, assess how aggressively Ichinose used her protection slots in traditional academic subjects, and gauge the exam's evaluation standard without spending accumulated points. In the end, she succeeded in getting Class C to use protection slots and obtained a clear starting point for adjusting the level of her future attacks.

4. Step 4: Formulating the tactical trap and managing risk with Kouenji

Due to her arrangement, Suzune was obligated to protect Kouenji, forcing her to face the exam with only 4 out of 5 available slots, which put her at a significant disadvantage against her rivals. Furthermore, with information leaked by a triador, there was a constant threat that rival classes would bombard Kouenji to force him into making mistakes.

During Sakayanagi's attack in Turn 1 in the "Calculus" category and later in "Gastronomy," Suzune intentionally decided not to grant Kouenji a protection slot, even though he was accumulating errors. Her strategic approach was based on a psychological assessment of Kouenji, he would never leave his fate at the mercy of another person's decision if he was just one mistake away from elimination. Therefore, the lack of protection would force him to take his answers seriously and succeed on his own merit, which is exactly what happened. Simultaneously, she devised this move as a trap for Sakayanagi, seeking to draw Class A's attacks toward Kouenji in order to gift defense points to Class B. Furthermore, within her plan, Suzune considered that if Kouenji were eliminated early, it would force him to spend his personal Protection Point, instantly eliminating any possibility of any Class B student being expelled at the end of the exam in the worst-case scenario.

5. Step 5: Formulation of the defensive block to Class C

During the first half of the exam, Horikita identified Ichinose's pattern of protecting her classmates at all costs to avoid expulsions. Based on this observation, she quickly formulated a containment plan. When attacking Class C, she intentionally distributed the targets to keep the number of "Reachers" (students with two failures) at exactly five, without creating a sixth. By deliberately restricting the pressure to this group of five, she forced Ichinose to fix and waste her five protection slots exclusively on those same students turn after turn. This design allowed her to neutralize Class C's tactical flexibility, freeze their chances of a comeback, and serve Class C for Ryuuen to devour them in the second half of the exam, ensuring that Class B remained at the top of the leaderboard.

6. Step 6: Integration of Kushida intelligence network

In the second half of the exam (Turn 13), when the general information about Class A's academic abilities had run out and the nomination period was winding down, Horikita switched to a plan B. Suzune had previously established a collaborative protocol with Kushida for times of uncertainty. Leveraging Kushida's social memory and deep knowledge of students' specific weaknesses throughout the school year, Horikita accepted the recommendation to nominate Satonaka in the "Kanji" category. The immediate implementation of this nomination allowed Class B to exploit a blind spot that Sakayanagi hadn't covered. The same pattern held true for the following nominations.

7. Step 7: Anti-Class A Defensive Alliance

Towards the end of the exam, Suzune noticed that Ryuuen's Class D and Ichinose's Class C were registering streaks of hits and perfect defenses that couldn't be attributed to mere chance, jeopardizing Class B's position.

Noticing that Ichinose was relentlessly and obsessively nominating Karuizawa for 10 consecutive turns in the second half, Suzune immediately formulated a contingency plan. She made direct contact with Ichinose to forge an emergency alliance aimed at sinking Sakayanagi's Class A.

Knowing in advance the list of chosen ones revealed by Ichinose, Horikita surgically allocated her protection slots to Karuizawa and other designated targets, accumulating consecutive defense points and guaranteeing 3rd place for Class B. This prevented a last-minute collapse, ensuring that Class A finished in 4th place with a loss of -100 class points, minimizing the damage to his own group.

Short Term Planning -
         * She designed an efficient process to gather the class immediately after announcing the exam. She planned to eat and discuss strategy simultaneously to make the most of the break.
         * Before running, she strategically manipulated the discussion by opening the floor to the class. She anticipated that no one would voluntarily assume such a high responsibility, consolidating her leadership through the class's unanimous decision and ensuring her legitimacy after a previous loss of confidence.
         * In response to Maezono's objection regarding her leadership, Suzune used straightforward logic, suggesting unworkable alternatives (such as appointing someone who hadn't applied or Kouenji), neutralizing the opposition in one fell swoop and keeping the class focused.
         * She prohibited discussing tactics in hallways or crowded places, and banned the use of digital media to communicate strategies within the classroom in order to prevent leaks.
         * She listened to and quickly evaluated proposals from her classmates, instantly identifying their structural flaws in the face of the rules and the counterattack from the other classes.
         * There is also the initial tactical strategy
         * In the first half, she executed a plan to measure the school's difficulty standard using Level 1 "English" questions against Class C.
         * She actively supported her predictions using Kushida's information network on the academic and personal weaknesses of the other classes.
         * Upon noticing Ichinose's repeated pattern of attacks on Karuizawa in the second half, she read Honami's implicit message, made direct contact with her to form an immediate strategic alliance, and coordinated her class's protections to secure 3rd place and avoid last place.

Long Term Planning - It's not a rigid long-term plan. It's an adaptable framework for multiple shifts.

Planning Intricacies -

         1. Layer 1: Internal Organizational Management and Social Control of the Group
         1. She organizes a class meeting during lunch just 10 minutes after it has started, forcing students to eat and debate simultaneously so as not to waste vital preparation time.
         2. Suzune anticipates that no one wants to take on the immense responsibility of the leadership position. To avoid embarrassing herself by not appointing herself or eroding her position after the previous events (UVE), she publicly addresses the situation and openly asks if anyone wants the position. When no one steps forward out of fear, she manages to get the class to elect her through necessity and indirect consensus.
         3. She formally prohibits discussing strategies in hallways, crowded classrooms, or using recordable digital media (such as text messages) to prevent information leaks to other classes.
         2. Layer 2: Tactical Exploitation of the Rules and Psychological Trapping
         1. Suzune uses her commitment to protect Kōenji to set a double trap. By intentionally refusing to use protection slots on him during the first few turns (despite him accumulating two consecutive mistakes), she forces Kōenji to respond seriously to avoid elimination on his own.
         2. By leaving Kōenji exposed as an easy target, she was trying to get Sakayanagi to nominate him, believing he would make a mistake, in order to secure a free defense point when Kōenji answered correctly.
         3. In the scenario where Kōenji had been eliminated for not being protected, Suzune planned to force him to spend his own personal Protection Point. This would uphold her promise "not to let him be expelled" without using up class resources and absorbing the expulsion penalty for the entire class.
         3. Layer 3: Macro-Strategic Reading and Manipulation of Rival Classes
         1. Design the first move of the exam by attacking Ichinose's Class in the "English" category (Difficulty 1) of students of low-medium academic level, not only to secure points, but to measure the general difficulty standard that the school will use.
         2. She intentionally attacks by scattering the targets to keep only 5 students on the verge of elimination (the Reachers). This forces Ichinose to continually expend her 5 protections on them, blocking her class. With this, Suzune intended to "serve Ichinose up on a silver platter" so that Ryūen could devour her in the second half and drag her down.
         3. It integrates Kushida as its active intelligence network during advanced rounds to identify specific personal weaknesses of rivals that are not solely dependent on their public academic performance.
         4. Layer 4: Contingent Adaptation
         1. Upon realizing in the second half that Ryūen had defied her prediction and unexpectedly allied herself with Ichinose using leaked information from Class A, Suzune quickly deciphers the pattern of indirect messages Ichinose was sending her, making contact with her.

Seeing her plan to win first place ruined, she immediately pivots to a defensive objective by allying with Ichinose through pre-arranged protections to secure third place, avoid expulsion from her own class, and push Sakayanagi's Class A into last place.

Planning Aim -
         * Suzune managed to keep her class in 2nd place with 28 points (just 1 point behind Sakayanagi's Class A), ensuring a solid base of defensive and offensive points.
         * She managed to place Class B in 3rd place with 59 points, minimizing the loss of class points (-50) and ensuring that Class A suffered the greatest impact (-100 points and the obligation to expel a student from their side).

         * After Chabashira explained the rules and Hirata reassured her, Suzune immediately devised a plan to make the most of the lunch break, gathering 37 of her classmates in the classroom to eat and discuss exam tactics simultaneously.

         * She appreciated the idea for enriching her own perspective and reassured Ike so as not to undermine his morale.
         * Suzune calculated that if two rival classes wanted to ally themselves to share victory, they would have to do so from the first half of the exam to accumulate enough points within the randomness of the test.

She used this move not only to score points but also as a data-gathering tool to gauge question trends and establish difficulty standards for the rest of her plan.

She established contact with Ichinose to coordinate the reported protections in the second half (Turns 15-20), successfully blocking Class A and securing 3rd place to avoid falling to the bottom of the standings.

         1. The way she led the way in having one less slot.
         1. Early identification of risks in the exam structure

         1. She devised a plan to prevent any member of the class from being expelled if they finished last.
         2. Her plan involved letting Koenji be eliminated if necessary, knowing he possessed a personal protection point.

         1. When Ike presented her with a plan to secure 68 points by having 32 students simulate illness, Suzune thoroughly analyzed all the scenarios, variables, and potential hazards:
         1. She identified that the school would likely detect the feigned illness 99% of the time, even though the rules imposed no restrictions on it.
         2. She assessed that by eliminating 32/34 students from the start, the class fixed its maximum score at 66-68 points with no possibility of increasing it, leaving them at the mercy of their opponents' successes.
         3. She predicted that, since it was a very visible strategy from turn 1, the other three classes would collaborate, taking advantage of the low difficulty of their attacks to easily score more than 67 points, making it a losing plan 9 out of 10 times.
         3. Tactical Trap Design Against the Ichinose Class
         1. To maximize her success in the overall standings, Suzune covered the variable of Ichinose's protective behavior.
         2. She devised a dispersed attack tactic: she intentionally kept the number of "Reachers" (students one mistake away from elimination) in Class C at exactly five.
         3. Knowing that Ichinose would obsessively protect those five students to avoid any expulsions, Suzune attacked the other students without creating a sixth Reacher. This caused Ichinose to waste his five protection slots on the same students while the others accumulated mistakes, leaving Class C "on a silver platter" to be worn down.
         4. Information security variable management:
         1. She anticipated the danger of data leaks or breaches in the class strategy. She strictly prohibited discussing tactical plans in common areas (hallways or busy classrooms) and banned the use of any digital device that left a record or could be easily recorded (such as mobile phones).
         5. Decision support system:
         1. To ensure optimal coverage in the selection of offensive targets when basic academic data was exhausted, she planned to use Kushida as an "information bank." She anticipated the need to draw on Kushida's knowledge of rival students' personal tastes, habits, and weaknesses to undermine their defenses in non-academic areas.
         6. Adapting to emergencies and last-minute alliances
         1. Suzune initially estimated that an alliance between rival classes was only viable if it was forged during the first half of the exam. She overlooked the extreme possibility of a traitor within Class A passing information to Ryuuen.
         2. Upon noticing Ryuuen's perfect blocks and the signs in Ichinose's nominations during the second half, Suzune quickly recalculated her plan. She contacted Ichinose directly to coordinate defensive protections and seal an implicit pact to sink Sakayanagi's Class A to 4th place, guaranteeing that her own class would finish in 3rd place with minimal damage (-50 points) and no risk of expulsion.

Planning Risk Calculation -
         * When Ike proposed a plan where 32 students would fake illness from the first shift to secure 68 points, Suzune quickly assessed the risks and limitations of the rule:
         * She identified that, while it provided a safe minimum score, it limited the group's maximum potential score and left them completely vulnerable if the other three classes achieved a success rate above 34% (67+ points).
         * She calculated how the strategy would lose effectiveness in classes with larger numbers of students (using Ryūen's class with 40 students as an example, which would reduce the maximum score to 66 points).
         * She anticipated that such a conspicuous strategy would provoke immediate cooperation from rival classes to surpass them, concluding that it was a losing plan 9 out of 10 times for a group with the academic strength of Class B.
         * Suzune accurately calculated the risk to her reputation and the group's trust following the events of the unanimous vote exam (Sakura's expulsion). Instead of directly running for leader—which would have provoked rejection or accusations of seeking immunity from expulsion—she manipulated the flow of the conversation by opening the candidacy to the class. When Maezono questioned her leadership, suggesting they look for a better candidate like Kiyotaka or Kōenji, Suzune demonstrated impeccable judgment:

         * Knowing beforehand that Kiyotaka would never put himself forward, she cornered and neutralized Maezono's position in one fell swoop, without creating any rifts in the group's morale.
         * Suzune calculated a double-edged maneuver, leaving Kōenji unprotected after making mistakes:
         * She accepted the risk of losing the class's trust by breaking her "promise" at first sight.
         * She knew that Kōenji, seeing himself in real danger (2 mistakes), would answer seriously to save himself, acting as bait for Sakayanagi to attack a target who would give correct answers and award points to Class B.
         * If Kōenji were eliminated, the plan to take his personal Protection Point to use in the exam would be fulfilled. This would eliminate the possibility of any other classmate being expelled if the class came in last place.
         * During the first half, Suzune calculated Ichinose's defensive strategy (who tries to protect all their teammates at the cost of their own points). Suzune deliberately spread her attacks to keep exactly five of Ichinose's students in "Reacher" status without creating a sixth. With this, she "cooked" Class C, leaving its members exhausted so that Ryūen could devour them in the second half and they could serve as a buffer for Class B.
         * Suzune theoretically evaluated inter-class alliances, concluding that for an alliance to function under the rules of this exam, it had to be formed in the first half to accumulate enough points and counteract randomness. Although her theoretical premise was logical, she admitted her miscalculation in not considering an extreme external variable: the presence of a traitor in Class A who leaked Sakayanagi's objectives to Ryūen, making late alliances in the second half viable.

         3. She plans the initial class meeting during lunch, specifying an efficient format (eating and discussing simultaneously) to optimize the scarce 10 minutes of break and not waste preparation time.
         2. Logical and Precise Dismantling of Defective Proposals
         1. Ike proposed artificially sickening 32 students to guarantee an automatic score of 68 points. Suzune breaks down the math behind the plan and immediately identifies its structural flaws.
         2. She observes that it's a plan with a "rigid ceiling" of points. If applied to a class with more students (like Ryuuen's with 40 students), the ceiling drops to 66 points, eliminating any possibility of increasing the score through attacks.
         3. She assesses that if one class withdraws at the beginning, the other three classes will immediately cooperate to surpass it, since they would only need 34% correct answers on the basic difficulty (level 1) to exceed that mark.
         4. She rates the plan as a losing strategy "9 out of 10 times" due to its inability to adapt to changing circumstances in the second half of the exam.
         3. Kouenji's Combined Strategy for Internal Control
         1. Suzune had a prior agreement with Kouenji not to expel him and to guarantee his freedom. At the same time, she only had 5 protection slots, and defending him relentlessly would have wasted defensive resources.
         2. In the early rounds, Suzune decides not to use her protection on Kouenji, even though he left his answers blank.
         3. He wouldn't leave his fate in Suzune's hands if he was on the verge of elimination. Upon reaching two consecutive incorrect answers (the danger zone), Kouenji is forced to answer and solve the problem correctly on his own to avoid elimination.
         4. If Kouenji gave in and was eliminated early, he would spend his own personal Protection Point. This fulfilled the promise not to expel him, removed his individual insurance, and freed the class from the threat of another student being expelled if they finished in last place.

         1. Select the genre "English" at difficulty level 1 against Ichinose's Class C. Justify the choice by arguing that it is a standard academic subject with the most clear metrics for measuring the actual level of difficulty and exam trends before taking risks with irregular categories.
         2. She recognizes that by the middle of the exam, the academic data for Class A has run out. She activates Kushida as a living information network to name precise targets based on the personal weaknesses that Kushida actively gathers.
         5. Isolation of Ichinose
         1. Suzune notices that Ichinose prioritizes absolute protection for her teammates to prevent anyone from suffering three misses and being eliminated.
         2. In the first half, Suzune intentionally distributes her attacks to keep only five students in Class C with two misses.
         3. By not generating a sixth "Reacher," she forces Ichinose to use her five protection slots repeatedly and inefficiently on the same students. This hinders Class C's point growth and sets them up to be devoured by Ryuuen in the second half of the exam.
         6. Alliance Calculation and Anomaly Discovery
         1. Suzune deduces that an alliance between enemy classes is only mathematically viable if it's formed at the start of the first half, due to the exam's unpredictability and the need to accumulate points symmetrically.
         2. Seeing no signs of cooperation in the first 10 turns, she logically and accurately rules out the possibility of a late alliance.
         3. Her analysis was flawless under normal circumstances. However, the presence of a traitor within Class A, leaking Sakayanagi's objectives to Ryuuen, eliminated the element of randomness. This allowed Ryuuen and Ichinose to execute a perfect alliance in the second half.
         4. Detecting Ichinose's encrypted communication (the consecutive nominations for Karuizawa), Suzune reacts quickly, deciphers the message, and joins the pact to divert attacks toward Class A, securing 3rd place and relegating Sakayanagi's class to last place.

         * Special end-of-year exam (Y2V12):

Planning Formulation -

         1. Exam profiling

After receiving the school's vague initial explanation and noticing that the exam would be held in the Special Building in a one-on-one format with no purely academic or athletic basis, Suzune quickly deduced that the exam would resemble a "battle through conversation." Instead of seeking out specialists, she formulated that the optimal strategy was to pre-select students with the fewest possible weaknesses and high versatility to ensure solid results under any unforeseen circumstances.

         2. General's Role

Suzune had planned to be the general herself, but upon receiving Kiyotaka's unexpected offer to participate, she demonstrated quick tactical thinking by prioritizing the collective victory over her own pride as a leader. She decided to relinquish the general position to Kiyotaka (assuming the role of Middle Guard herself), thus ensuring Kiyotaka would be an absolute safety net for the exam.

         3. Information leak mitigation

To prevent Kiyotaka's harsh conditions from affecting class morale or being discovered by other classrooms, Suzune took multiple immediate precautions to keep the terms a complete secret. She planned the final lineup to maximize Kiyotaka's status as a surprise factor against rival classes. To the point that even when a triador revealed the secret, rival classes were still unsure whether it was true or not.

         4. Strategic segmentation of the class

To prevent Kiyotaka's harsh conditions from affecting class morale or being discovered by other classrooms, Suzune took multiple immediate precautions to keep the terms a complete secret. She planned the final lineup to maximize Kiyotaka's status as a surprise factor against rival classes. To the point that even when a triador revealed the secret, rival classes were still unsure whether it was true or not.

         5. Configuración del "Grupo Élite" de discusión

The rules for the special exam were revealed. When it came time to assign her classmates to the five discussion groups required by the rules, Suzune coordinated with Hirata to design an initial "elite group." She meticulously selected students who stood out for being calm, bold, quick-thinking, able to read the atmosphere, and able to maintain a poker face (such as Sudou, Miyake, and Matsushita). The planned objective of this group was to take complete control of the flow of the conversation.

         6. Win or clear Kiyotaka's path

Suzume had no advantage in this exam, and with only 7 lives, she had to destroy 22. She had to strive for victory, and, in the worst-case scenario, clear the way for Kiyotaka.She defeated Hamaguchi using only her skill. Against Kanzaki, she used her elite group in the first discussion to control the pace of the debate. Thanks to this, she wasn't fooled by the participants' superficial statements about their roles, correctly identified Miyake as an honor student, and dismantled Kanzaki's defense by immediately taking away 4 lives. Afterward, she maintained strict emotional discipline by rejecting Kanzaki's pleas and capitalized on his apathy to finish the game cleanly with a perfect 7-0.

Facing Ichinose in a completely unfavorable scenario and noticing that her opponent possessed a near-perfect intuitive perception of the participants, Suzune formulated a distraction tactic on the fly to even the odds. This psychological maneuver slowed Ichinose's progress and gave Suzune the necessary time to correctly guess Hatsutori's executive role and regain a life.

When she was overtaken in the final stretch by Ichinose's insurmountable social knowledge base of her classmates, Suzune tried to wear down her opponent's life counter as much as possible and gather all available visual information to facilitate Kiyotaka's clean entry, completing the exam transition to the General in a controlled manner.

Short Term Planning -
         * Initial strategy in the face of a lack of information
         * Lacking details about the actual content of the exam and faced with thousands of possibilities, she decided that trying to guess the test was a waste of resources. She formulated a strategy of choosing three representatives with the fewest possible weaknesses, capable of adapting to any situation.
         * At the same time, she considered the alternative tactic of secretly selecting students with extreme strengths and weaknesses to seek a surprise advantage.
         * To ensure the class's victory, she accepted Kiyotaka's demand to assume the role of General under severe conditions.
         * This forced her to adjust her tactical combat plan; now she would assume the role of Middle Guard and would have to fight aggressively to eliminate as many lives as possible from the rival without giving Kiyotaka a turn, thus preventing him from activating the condition of loss of cooperation.
         * Furthermore, she took numerous defensive and secrecy measures to prevent other classes from discovering that Kiyotaka would be participating as a General. Note: This strategy was thwarted before the exam due to a leak by a traitor (Maezono) within her own class.
         * Strategy for selecting and forming participant groups and the clashes of the Special Exam:
         * In collaboration with Hirata, she organized her 35 fellow participants into an initial "elite group" composed of calm students who could think quickly, maintain a poker face, and read the atmosphere (Sudou, Miyake, Matsushita, etc.) to control the flow of the first discussion.
         * Against Hamaguchi (Vanguard):
         * Used pure skill (7-0).
         * Against Kanzaki (Middle Guard):
         * She devised a tactic to induce him to remain on the defensive without risking an attack.
         * During the nomination phase, she correctly deduced the Honor Students' intentions and identities, reducing Kanzaki's life total by four in a single move.
         * She maintained tactical discipline, rejecting Kanzaki's pleas to concede victory and responding with a direct confrontation, ultimately defeating him 7-0.
         * Contra Ichinose (General):
         * When cornered, she used a deceptive tactic, throwing out a verbal "bait" by claiming that executive roles were distributed equally between both classes (1:1), forcing Ichinose to divide her attention and buying time to secure a nomination in Hatsutori.
         * She attempted to undermine Ichinose's confidence by bringing up Kanzaki's surrender and later Kiyotaka's.
         * Realizing that her defeat was almost inevitable due to the inherent disadvantages, she restructured her objective in real time to "reduce Ichinose's lives as much as possible" to clear the way for Kiyotaka.

Long Term Planning -
         * The Strategy for Promotion to Class A:
         * All of Suzune's planning and risk-taking are part of her master plan to take her class from Class D to Class A before graduation. This long-term plan justifies sacrificing immediate advantages or enduring high levels of stress to secure the 250 class points needed for the end-of-year exam.
         * Seeing this as one of the most important steps to transform the "class of defects" into a united and stable group, capable of defeating any threat.
         * Management of the Deal and Contract with Kiyotaka:
         * Suzune used Kiyotaka's presence as a long-term "safety net" that allowed her to take strategic risks and learn in the process.
         * When she realized that Kiyotaka demanded exclusive use of the "Traitor" resource (his only key strategic weapon in the exam), Suzune renegotiated the proposed condition, extending Kiyotaka's demand for help from six months to an obligation to collaborate with the class until graduation if she lost and he won.

Planning Intricacies -
         1. Layer 1: Structural Planning and Role Selection
         1. Given the impossibility of predicting the specific content of the exam (which the school did not reveal beforehand), Suzune deduced that creating specific strategies would be a waste of resources. Her selection plan was structured around choosing students with the fewest possible weaknesses and a versatile profile (capable of performing in dialogue or any condition not strictly academic or physical), selecting Hirata and Kushida as ideal candidates due to their adaptability.
         2. This led her to accept Kiyotaka's condition for assuming the position of General.
         3. After hesitating between Kushida and Hirata as representatives, she decided to keep Kikyo Kushida in the group of participants. Not knowing what roles the participants would require in the discussions, she kept Kushida as a versatile cover on the front lines. These two, in turn, forced her to restructure the class formation to maximize their chances of victory.
         1. Vanguard: Yōsuke Hirata.
         2. Mid Guard: Suzune Horikita.
         3. General: Kiyotaka Ayanokōji.
         2. Layer 2: Layers of Security and Information Management
         1. Suzune designed Kiyotaka's appointment as General to serve as a surprise element against rival classes. She took numerous precautions and information containment measures to prevent leaks and keep secret that Kiyotaka wouldn't be acting as an ordinary participant. Ultimately, this plan was undermined by an internal traitor, Maezono, who leaked the position to Hashimoto's class, who then leaked it to Ichinose's class.
         2. She kept the terms of Kiyotaka's agreement (that he would withdraw his cooperation from the class until graduation if she participated) strictly secret. The purpose of this secrecy was to prevent other classes from exploiting or using this emotional/strategic vulnerability against her.
         3. Layer 3: Tactical Distribution of Participants
         1. Suzune strategically planned the distribution of her classmates. She intentionally grouped together the students with the best quick thinking, awareness of their surroundings, communication skills, and poker faces.
         1. She designed the Elite Group's deployment specifically for the first phase of their matches. The goal of this distribution was to control the flow of the initial debate, force the opponent onto the defensive, and secure clean victories from the start without suffering any life losses.
         4. Layer 4: Deception Tactics and Maneuvers during Execution
         1. As a Mid Guard with 7 lives against the opponent's profile, her initial plan was to force the opponent into doubt and passivity, keeping him on the defensive without risking hasty attacks that would reduce her own lives before reaching the General.
         2. When she faced Honami and began losing lives rapidly, Suzune improvised a strategic layer based on the psychology of the exam.
         1. She exploited the principle of "school justice" (ANHS) to sow doubt in Ichinose, asserting that executive roles should be distributed equally (1:1) between both classes.
         2. She used this tactic to force Ichinose to shift her analytical focus to her own classmates instead of Suzune's. Her aim was to narrow the rival's field of vision and buy time to regroup.
         3. She attempted to use the information gleaned from Kanzaki's emotional breakdown in the previous fight to psychologically destabilize Ichinose, looking for loopholes to generate stalemate discussions and restart the debate groups.
         5. Layer 5: Extreme Contingency Plan
         1. When forced to cede the key resource of the exam (the "Traitor's" right) to Kiyotaka, Suzune restructured the clauses of their agreement: she modified the condition demanded by him so that, in case of failure, her commitment to help would last until graduation instead of just six months.
         2. Recognizing the insurmountable disadvantages of the system and the rules, Suzune's final plan mutated into a containment strategy: if she couldn't win the fight, her ultimate goal became to wear down as many of Ichinose's lives as possible to clear the way for Kiyotaka, thus securing the final 250 class points for the class.
Planning Aim -
         * Achieved a perfect 5-0 victory against Hamaguchi.
         * Achieved a perfect 7-0 victory against Kanzaki.
         * Exhausted and consumed the resources of Ichinose's class to the maximum.
         * Ensured that Kiyotaka took the final turn in an advantageous position to win the exam.
         * Finally achieved exam victory for his group, allowing the class to advance to Class A with a total of 1233 points after the test ended.

Planning Process -
         1. Resolution and Adaptation in the Face of Insufficient Information
         1. The school didn't reveal the test details even to the teachers before exam day. Given the impossibility of guessing the content amidst a myriad of variables, Suzune concluded that the best strategy was to select representatives with the fewest general weaknesses to minimize risks regardless of the format.
         2. When choosing the main representatives, she opted for Hirata instead of Kushida as the vanguard/defense, strategically deciding to keep Kushida within the group of "participants." This ensured she had a pillar of high adaptability and communication skills at the debate table.
         2. Acceptance and Adjustment for Kiyotaka's Claim
         1. When Kiyotaka demanded the position of General and exclusive use of the "Traitor" right, Suzune lost her primary strategic tool. Instead of letting the planning collapse, Suzune accepted the condition to prioritize the class's victory and reorganized her own role, moving to the Middle Guard.
         2. Aware that placing Kiyotaka as General would raise doubts about her leadership and lead to leaks, Suzune devised multiple security measures to keep the lineup a secret from the other classes.
         3. Realizing that Kiyotaka was taking away her best weapon to win independently, Suzune changed the terms: she demanded that, in case he intervened and won under her conditions, he would work for the class not just for six months, but until graduation.
         3. Tactical Organization of Participant Groups
         1. Since she couldn't use the same group of participants in consecutive rounds, Suzune had to strategically structure the lineups of seven students. For her first decisive match, she assembled an "elite group" of students with composure, reading ability, and quick thinking to control the defensive flow.
         4. Tactics During Encounters.
         1. In the battle against Kanzaki, Suzune correctly deduced Miyake's maneuver as an honor student and the reaction of the other participants, managing to identify the exact roles without making mistakes and taking away 4 lives from Kanzaki right away.
         2. Completely outmatched by Ichinose's perceptive abilities and rapidly losing lives, Suzune attempted a real-time distraction: she sowed doubt in Ichinose by claiming that the school distributed executive roles symmetrically (1:1) between both classes. This deception aimed to force Ichinose to focus on her own classmates to buy time, allowing Suzune to pinpoint Hatsutori's location and temporarily regain a life.

Planning Preparation Process -
         * Faced with an exam with unfamiliar rules, she deduced the need to select versatile representatives with as few weaknesses as possible, rather than academic or athletic specialists.
         * She decided to leave Kushida in the group of ordinary participants to ensure cover and adaptability on that front, resolving the final lineup with Hirata as the vanguard, herself as the middle guard, and Kiyotaka as the general.
         * When Kiyotaka requested exclusive use of the "Traitor" right (her only strategic weapon), Suzune modified the terms of the agreement on the fly to force him to cooperate with the class until graduation instead of just six months, prioritizing collective victory over his own role as general.
         * She used the school's principle of fairness to deceive Ichinose, sowing doubt that executive roles were divided 1:1, which diverted her opponent's attention to her own classmates and gave her the necessary time to successfully nominate Hatsutori.

Planning Coverage -
         * Suzune correctly identified that, since the specific content of the exam wasn't revealed until the day itself, trying to guess among thousands of possibilities was a waste of resources. Her initial planning strategy consisted of assembling a versatile lineup by choosing students with as few weaknesses as possible instead of specialists.
         * It considered the gender balance required by the rules and evaluated the best options for the Vanguard, Middle Guard, and General roles:
         * She evaluated and discarded high-risk/high-reward profiles like Kōenji or Kiyotaka under normal circumstances (due to lack of cooperation or risk of defeat due to apathy).
         * She opted for versatility and communication skills, initially selecting Hirata and Kushida.
         * Upon modifying the strategy to incorporate Kiyotaka as a General, she replaced Kushida with Hirata as Vanguard. She strategically kept Kushida among the "participants" to ensure coverage on all fronts, without knowing what role the non-representatives would play.
         * Risk coverage evaluating the 4 possibilities derived from the agreement with Kiyotaka:
         * Forced defeat of Kiyotaka: You would gain his full support for 6 months.
         * Suzune defeats the rival General from the Middle Guard: The cooperative relationship remains unchanged.
         * Suzune loses but Kiyotaka wins: They gain the 250 class points, but lose Kiyotaka's future cooperation.
         * Both lose: They maintain Kiyotaka's cooperation for 6 months.
         * She kept Kiyotaka's condition for participation a strict secret, anticipating that other classes might use that information against her. Despite her efforts and containment measures, this secret was compromised by the presence of a traitor within the class.
         * Noticing that Kiyotaka was demanding exclusive use of the "Traitor" right, Suzune readjusted the condition: instead of 6 months of support if they lost, she extended Kiyotaka's support requirement until graduation.
         * By structuring the 35 participants into groups of 7, he foresaw the need to send a group with speed reading ability, self-control, and social intelligence to ensure the first victory against Kanzaki.
         * She considered the rule restriction that prevented using the same group in consecutive rounds, planning to rotate her classmates despite the collective skill gap.
         * Suzune's ultimate cover for this exam lay in positioning Kiyotaka as the last line of defense (General). Although her personal goal was to clear the exam in the Middle Guard without him having to intervene, her planning ensured that, should she fail her individual reading, the class's overall victory and the 250 points would be virtually guaranteed through him.

Planning Versatility -
         * Initially, Suzune considered forming a balanced team based on communication and adaptability (Hirata and Kushida). However, realizing there would be no clear information about the participants' tasks, she strategically readjusted her team. She decided to keep Kushida among the participants to ensure dominance and control during the group discussion phase, while selecting Hirata as the Vanguard. This was reinforced by a specialized discussion group of calm-minded students to manage the initial discussion and secure the game against a participating opponent.
         * She employed multiple containment measures to keep Kiyotaka's position as General a secret and use him as a surprise factor against the other classes. Although the information was later leaked by an internal traitor, Maezono, her cover-up was so thorough that it could barely be considered rumors.

Planning Risk Calculation -
         * Consciously accepting the enormous risk of handing over the role of General to Kiyotaka and assuming the position of Middle Guard, drastically reducing his own margin of error (7 lives against the rival's 22) and assuming the pressure of having to win aggressively in order to have a safety net that ensures his victory.
         * Assess Kanzaki's desperate situation and maintain strict emotional discipline by firmly rejecting his plea to concede victory, calculating that compromising would weaken his class's ranking and show disrespect to the competition.
         * Strategically decide to sacrifice their own survival on the board and wear down Ichinose as much as possible, assuming their own defeat beforehand as the most mathematically viable way to ensure the class's final victory through Kiyotaka.
         * Analyze the internal behavior of the participants and choose not to follow the current of the superficial debate.

Planning Contingency -
         * She structured an initial lineup to mitigate the risk of a lack of information, positioning Hirata as the vanguard to gauge the dynamics of the exam and reserving herself as the middle guard.
         * She strategically kept Kushida among the regular participants to ensure versatile and highly adaptable support to cover unforeseen events in the discussions.
         * As a last resort, she formulated a damage control strategy aimed at wearing down Ichinose's lives as much as possible, facilitating a later, risk-free victory for Kiyotaka in the position of general.
         * She deliberately used Kiyotaka's presence as an absolute safety net in her plan, allowing her to assume middle guard and take aggressive risks under the certainty that the class would not lose overall points.

Planning Invincibility -
         * She designed an "elite group" composed of calm, poker-faced students so she could control the flow of the conversation over which she had no influence.
         * She immediately modified the contract clauses with Kiyotaka after giving him the "Traitor" right, safeguarding the class's future by extending their obligation to cooperate 100% until graduation in case of defeat.

Planning Accuracy -
         * She correctly identified her opponent's logical and straightforward selections in advance (Ichinose as a general, Kanzaki as a middle guard, and Hamaguchi, Hatsukawa, or Niiura as vanguards).
         * She aligned with Kiyotaka's analysis, unanimously determining that Hirata and Kushida were the strongest standard choices in their class due to their versatility and lack of obvious weaknesses.
         * She kept Kiyotaka's condition of future non-cooperation strictly secret from the entire class and took multiple preventative measures to avoid information leaks to rival classrooms.
         * She precisely executed the separation of key roles by deliberately keeping Kushida outside the representatives and among the participants to ensure operational coverage on all fronts of the exam in the absence of detailed information.
         * She calculatedly modified the contract clauses with Kiyotaka during negotiations, extending his obligation to cooperate with the class from six months until graduation as a direct counterweight before relinquishing exclusive use of the "Traitor" right.
         * She planned a preemptive psychological counterattack by forming an initial group with the calmest, boldest, and most composed students in his class to put Kanzaki on the defensive and control the flow of the discussion.
         * She devised a strategy of diversion and verbal deception during the confrontation with Ichinose to force her to focus her attention on her own classmates and buy time to attack.

Personal ranking for Suzune (Y2V12) categories:
Planning Coverage >= Planning Intricacies > Planning Aim > Planning Versatility >= Planning Process = Long Term Planning >= Planning Preparation Process > Planning Risk Calculation  > Planning Accuracy > Planning Contingency > Planning Invincibility >= Short Term Planning

Linear Thinking -
         * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1):
Suzune always begins with a clear diagnosis:
         * She recognizes her class's lower structural position.
         * She assesses real risks (expulsion, sabotage, attrition).
         * She identifies the limitations of the exam system.
She prioritizes goals:
         * Avoid expulsions
         * Maintain internal stability
         * Minimize point losses
         * Prepare the ground for the future
Suzune doesn't take isolated actions; her strategy progresses through defined stages: Initial observation → Limited negotiation → Evaluation of external responses → Tactical adjustments → Consolidation of the result
She prefers to wait and miss immediate opportunities rather than disrupt the strategy's progression.
         * She doesn't rush critical phases.
         * She doesn't skip processes.
         * She doesn't improvise unnecessarily.
Suzune executes exactly what she plans, without visible contradictions:
         * There are no decisions that break the strategic line.
         * There are no redundant moves.
         * There are no actions that compromise future phases.

         * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4):
Design, maintain, and execute a step-by-step strategy over two full weeks, without losing coherence despite external pressure, unbalanced talent, emotional conflicts, and changing rules. From the outset, Suzune defines one non-negotiable priority: Survive the exam without any eliminations.
         * Expulsions ⇒ irreversible loss
         * Avoiding expulsions ⇒ class stability
         * Stability ⇒ future possibility of advancement
Then Suzune divides the exam into logical phases:
         * Before the exam
         * Stop impulsive group formation
         * Centralize decisions (pre-arranged messages)
         * Evaluate the class's actual economic capital
         * Identify emotional risks (Ike, Shinohara -  examples)
         * During the exam
         * Act as mobile support
         * Distribute resources where there is greater risk
         * Avoid unnecessary point fights
         * Stay out of the "eye of the storm"
         * After the exam
         * Monitor mergers of low-ranking groups
         * Prevent sudden changes in the bottom 10
         * Ensure a position ≥ top 50%
Instead of improvising or changing the plan upon seeing exceptional talent, she integrates them as modules, not as replacements for the system. Suzune delegated offensive tactics without altering her plan:
         * Kiyotaka → indirect pressure / anomaly control
         * Kouenji → pure offensive by contract
         * She → structural stability in all groups
UIE is full of dilemmas:
         * Cooperate vs. compete
         * Class vs. year
         * Gain points vs. avoid expulsion
Suzune doesn't solve everything, but she does maintain consistency:
         * If something threatens survival → it's prioritized
         * If something only improves points but adds risk → it's discarded
Final result:
         * No students from Class 2-D expelled
         * Class out of the bottom
         * Kouenji fulfills his bet
         * The class structure is strengthened for future exams

         * UVE (Y2V5):
Suzune's strongest demonstration of Linear Thinking occurs when she structures the expulsion process during the unanimous voting exam, particularly in the decision to preserve Kushida and expel Sakura Airi based on the OAA.
         * Step 1 — Identifying the Absolute Constraint
         * The exam requires at least one expulsion to avoid failure.
         * She doesn't debate this condition. She takes it as an axiom. Here she establishes the first structural line:
         * 0 expulsions = failure → Not an option.
         * Step 2 — Defining Dual Objectives
         * Save Kushida (strategic asset).
         * Meet the exam requirement.
         * She recognizes the apparent contradiction and doesn't avoid it by integrating it into the logical system.
         * Step 3 — Eliminating Critical Variables
         * She automatically excludes:
         * Strategic leaders (herself).
         * Emotional regulators of the masses (Hirata, Karuizawa).
         * Individuals with high structural impact.
         * Here, the approach is based on functional criteria, not on personal feelings.
         * Step 4 — Elimination of the “future potential” factor
         * Suzune dismisses the speculative argument:
         * “It could improve in the future.”
         * This is key. Eliminating hypothetical projections stabilizes the equation.
         * It eliminates uncertainty → Reduces the problem to measurable data.
         * Step 5 — Selection of the assessment framework
         * She introduces the OAA as a neutral standard.
         * This is the purest example of linear thinking:
         * No emotions.
         * No relationships.
         * No subjectivity.
         * Only institutional quantification.
         * Define the criterion → Apply the criterion → Accept the result.
         * Step 6 — Final comparison
         * Sakura Airi and Ike = lowest OAA.
         * Ike = viable alternative, but with greater social utility.
         * Logical conclusion:
         * Sakura is the least costly variable for the class structure.

         * Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6):
The logical structure she uses to bring Kushida back and functionally reintegrate her into the class during the Sports Festival. Before the Sports Festival:
         * The class is internally fractured.
         * Kushida is emotionally detached.
         * There is a risk of passive sabotage.
         * The class is understaffed (38 students).
         * Each participant is mathematically critical.
         * Internal stability directly impacts competitive performance.
Suzune doesn't confront with reproach. She constructs a closed logical line:
         * Step 1 — Current State: Factual premise. Not emotional.
         * If you don't actively participate, the class loses points.
         * Step 2 — Direct Consequence: Immediate cause-and-effect relationship.
         * If the class loses points, we fall further from Class A.
         * Step 3 — Extended Consequence: Introduces individual interest.
         * If we fall further from Class A, your position within the school loses value.
         * Step 4 — Time Horizon: Change of time frame → introduces future projection.
         * School is a limited stage. After graduation, no one will remember your time here.
         * Step 5 — Identity Reframing: Transform the conflict into a strategic investment.
         * You can use this opportunity to strengthen your position and then start fresh outside of here.
         * Step 6 — Logical Closure: An inevitable conclusion based on accepted premises.
         * The rational option is not to isolate yourself, but to capitalize on the system before leaving it.

         * Written Comprehensive Cooperative Exam (Y2V9):
Suzune's strategy follows a highly structured, systematic process.
         * Step 1 — Exam System Analysis
         * Suzune first identifies the fundamental parameters of the system:
         * 100 total questions.
         * Maximum of 5 questions per student.
         * Time limit: 10 minutes.
         * Scoring based on OAA, not difficulty.
         * This allows her to deduce the exam's central mechanism:
         * Students with lower academic ability award more points for correct answers.
This discovery becomes the foundation of the entire strategy.
         * Step 2 — Identifying the Main Problem
         * Suzune recognizes that the biggest obstacle is not answering questions, but rather:
         * Finding easy questions within 100 problems
         * Doing so in less than 10 minutes
         * Especially for students with lower ability.
         * Therefore, she formulates the problem as:
         * "How can we reduce the time spent searching for easy questions?"
         * Step 3 — Functional Segmentation of the Class
         * Suzune divides the class into strategic roles:
         * High-achieving students
         * analyze the exam
         * identify easy questions
         * Low-achieving students
         * answer the assigned questions
         * maximize the system's score
This division creates a functional chain of tasks.
         * Step 4 — Designing the Execution Order
         * Suzune then organizes the sequence of participants:
         * High-achieving student -> Low-achieving student -> High-achieving student -> Low-achieving student
         * This produces a constant flow of information. Linear process:
         * Analyze questions -> Identify easy questions -> Transmit information -> Answer questions -> Earn points
Each step depends on the previous one.
         * Step 5 — Implementing the Communication Mechanism
         * Suzune identifies a gap in the exam rules. Students:
         * cannot speak
         * cannot leave written clues
         * But they can briefly see each other when passing by the door.
         * Based on this, she designs the system of hand signals.
         * Example:
         * Q55 → two open hands twice
         * Q69 → six fingers + nine fingers
This system is a direct solution to the problem identified in step 2.
         * Step 6 — Risk Management
         * Suzune adds a final stabilization step:
         * the Kouenji case. Problem:
         * unpredictable
         * could sabotage the strategy
         * Linear solution:
         * place it last
         * leave only 2 questions remaining
         * This minimizes the impact of its behavior.

         * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10):
Complete refutation of Ike's "sick" strategy + multi-class outcome modeling. Suzune:
         * Takes a proposal with a fixed outcome (68 points)
         * Identifies its structural limit (unchangeable maximum cap)
         * Introduces external variables (reaction of 3 other classes)
Builds a conditional model:
         * If other classes cooperate → they exceed 66+
         * If they optimize scoring → they only need 17 additional points
         * Quantifies implicit probabilities (≈34% minimum success rate)
         * Concludes: strategy loses 9/10 scenarios
Follows a strict logical chain:
         * Input (rules)
         * Fixed output (68 pts)
         * External interaction
         * Rival optimization
         * Overall outcome
There are no intuitive leaps → it is sequential, deterministic, and mathematical, integrating the basic exam rules + system limits

Personal ranking for the best Linear Thinking feats:
Written Comprehensive Cooperative Exam (Y2V9) > UVE (Y2V5) > Sports Festival Planning (Y2V6) > Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10) > Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4) > Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1)

Non Linear Thinking -
         * Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1):
Suzune evades and passes Nanase's test, rejecting an immediate gain to avoid altering the future structure of the board. Suzune:
         * Evaluates a proposal that does not directly benefit her class
         * Connects external factors:
         * Class 1-D internal politics
         * Risk of civil war
         * Future influence of Ichinose

         * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4): To detect a real, direct threat by connecting economics, psychology, social behavior, and timing into a single operational intuition. Not because I have solid proof, but because the shape of the dataset indicates real danger. Suzune receives elements that don't fit together linearly:
         * An anonymous note with jumbled words “Noon / K.A. / Expelled / I2”
         * The waste of paper and pen
         * Final exam (no one wastes points)
         * Kiyotaka being targeted for a reward
         * Amasawa's anomalous behavior
         * Disproportionate hostility to “a joke”
Instead of looking for a closed logical chain, she layers things up:
         * Economic cost ⇒ serious intention
         * Temporal moment (last day) ⇒ urgency
         * Specific name (K.A.) ⇒ concrete objective
         * Amasawa's active interference ⇒ real event in progress
         * Excessive emotional reaction ⇒ something she can't afford to reveal
Suzune connects areas that don't normally mix:
         * Exam economics
         * Behavioral psychology
         * Hierarchy of threats
         * Social metabehavior (who blocks whom and why)
         * Strategic risk vs. personal gain
Suzune She decides to:
         * Leave her designated zone
         * Risk her score
         * Ignore the logic of “maximizing benefits for herself”
         * Investigate the perpetrator of the entire event.

         * UVE (Y2V5):
The simultaneous decision to save Kushida and redesign the class's political architecture through an alternative expulsion. In most scenarios, the mental framework would be:
         * Kushida betrays → expel her.
         * Divided class → Use Kushida as a scapegoat to save them.
         * Risk of failure → minimize losses.
Suzune breaks this predictable flow. Instead of accepting the dichotomy:
         * “Saving Kushida” vs. “class stability,”
she connects three seemingly incompatible variables:
         * Kushida's dangerousness.
         * The mandatory requirement of expulsion.
         * The need for strong leadership for the future that unites the class.
And she formulates an unexpected synthesis:
         * Saving the most disruptive element can become a strategic investment if the alternative sacrifice redefines the internal hierarchy.
Connection between seemingly independent variables:
         * Variable A — Kushida = Internal risk.
         * Traditionally a toxic asset.
         * Variable B — OAA = Objective Metric.
         * A cold, institutional tool.
         * Variable C — Suzune's Authority.
         * At that still fragile moment.
The nonlinear occurs when Suzune transforms:
         * A moral problem (betrayal).
         * Into a structural problem (balance of power).
         * Using an administrative tool (OAA).
To consolidate future leadership, she doesn't follow a direct path. She creates an intersection between domains:
         * Psychology → Politics → Institutional Metric → Power Dynamics.
Breakdown of the expected pattern. The class expected:
         * Emotional justice.
         * Immediate punishment.
         * A simple moral resolution.
Suzune introduces a different logic: “The class doesn't need emotional justice; it needs to know the extent of its naiveté.”
This conceptual leap isn't progressive, it's transversal. Now, talk about integration of contradictions. Nonlinear thinking also involves tolerating paradoxes:
         * Saving the one who did the most harm.
         * Sacrificing the one who posed the least threat.
         * Increasing cohesion through a divisive decision.
That doesn't follow a straight line. There's a difference from her previous linear thinking. In linear analysis, Suzune:
         * Defines criteria.
         * Applies the OAA.
         * Reaches an inevitable conclusion.
But before that, the real nonlinear leap occurs:
         * The very idea that Kushida can be saved while still fulfilling the exam requirement.

         * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10):
Creation of the “Reacher Trap” against Ichinose + indirect manipulation of enemy behavior. Suzune detects a key psychological trait:
         * Ichinose = extreme aversion to eliminations
         * Connects non-linear elements:
         * Strike system (2 mistakes = risk)
         * Limited protection slots (5)
         * Attack distribution
Designs an emergent structure:
         * Maintain exactly 5 “Reachers” (2 strikes)
         * Force constant protection on them
         * Prevent the creation of a 6th → system control
Result:
         * 0 eliminations (apparent advantage)
But:
         * Massive accumulation of “1 mistake”
         * Class becomes hypersensitive to future collapse
         * Becomes an ideal target for Ryuuen
It doesn't follow a classic step-by-step process because it connects:
         * Enemy leader psychology
         * System mechanics
         * Temporal dynamics (early → late game payoff)
         * Generates a delayed effect (delayed collapse trap)

Personal ranking for the best Non Linear Thinking feats:
UVE (Y2V5) > Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4) > Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10) >  Partner Exam Strategy (Y2V1)

Suzune doesn't interpret the OAA merely as:
         * Scores
         * Ranks
         * Immediate measures
But as:
         * A pressure mechanism
         * A behavioral control tool
         * A system that alters collective motivation
She understands that with Class D there is a distinction between real leadership and formal leadership. Suzune understands that:
         * Having authority ≠ wielding influence
         * A leader can exist without a title
         * Legitimacy arises from social recognition, not from the assigned role
This implies reasoning about:
         * Symbolic power
         * Collective trust
         * Structural stability
During inter-class negotiations, Suzune:
         * Doesn't only evaluate explicit benefits
Analyzes:
         * Future debt
         * Strategic dependence
         * Risk of betrayal

         * Suzune UIE (Y2V2/Y2V3/Y2V4):
The conceptual redefinition of her role, of Kiyotaka, and of the exam's "value," transcends points, zones, or visible results. In UIE, most students think in concrete terms:
         * points
         * rankings
         * zones
         * resources
immediate survival. Suzune abstracts the exam as:
         * a pressure system designed to reveal human relationships, dependencies, and limitations.
Therefore, her goal is not: "to earn a lot of points." But rather: "to avoid expulsion, protect the class structure, and keep key members operational." Suzune stops seeing Kiyotaka as:
         * a friend
         * a close ally
         * a strong individual
         * and begins to see him as a structural node.
She thinks: "His existence keeps the class stable and is important to me." That is conceptual Think. That is why she decides to:
         * risk points
         * leave a zone
         * even forgo the exam
         * because Kiyotaka's abstract value > the numerical value of UIE.

         * UVE (Y2V5):
The reconceptualization of “a student’s value” as a structural function, not a moral or emotional attribute. During motion 5, the apparent conflict is concrete:
         * Who should be expelled?
         * Who deserves to stay?
         * Who caused the most harm?
Most people think in tangible terms:
         * Popularity.
         * Behavior.
         * Likeability.
         * Immediate performance.
Suzune abandons this approach. Instead of evaluating people as concrete individuals, she transforms them into functional variables within the class war for Class A. That is abstract thinking. Suzune stops thinking:
         * “Kushida is a threat who could betray us in the future.”
And starts thinking:
         * “Kushida is a valuable resource with a unique talent that will be key in the class war.”
Similarly, Sakura ceases to be:
         * “A shy and kind classmate.”
To become:
         * “The variable with the smallest measurable structural contribution.”
Abstract thinking requires:
         * Decoupling emotion from analysis.
Separate individual ethics from systemic efficiency. Operate at the level of principles, not particular cases. Suzune doesn't evaluate:
         * "Is it fair?"
         * "Is it cruel?"
She evaluates:
         * "What maximizes the class's probability of survival?"
The OAA is not just a meritocratic metric. For Suzune, it becomes:
         * A symbolic model of quantified academic-social value.
She understands that:
         * It's not perfect.
         * It doesn't capture humanity.
It doesn't completely predict the future. But she uses it as an abstract representation of objective contribution to guide her in identifying the worst student. This implies understanding the difference between:
         * Reality.
         * And the model that represents reality.
Suzune operates on concepts such as:
         * Power structure.
         * Collective stability.
         * Strategic function.
         * Human cost-benefit.
         * Social capital.
None of these have a physical form; they are theoretical constructs, and yet she manipulates them mentally as if they were concrete pieces. This moment is not simply about choosing. It consists of simultaneously understanding:
         * The exam as a Darwinian selection mechanism.
         * The classroom as an organic system.
         * Leadership as an abstract principle of cohesion.
         * Sacrifice as a strategic investment.
It is not reacting to events, but rather operating on ideas that lead to logical thought:
         * Axiom → criterion → conclusion.
Which leads to abstract thought:
         * Redefining what “value” is.
         * Redefining what “saving” means.
         * Redefining what “expulsion” represents.

         * Suzune Cultural Festival Strategy (Y2V5-Y2V7):
Understanding public betrayal within a structural marketing system through staged rivalry. She understands that:

This isn't logistics. It's an understanding of how collective attention works. It transforms:
         * School event → into competitive narrative.
         * Visible rivalry generates:
         * Taking sides.
         * Emotional identification.
         * Increased spending out of pride or support.
         * Concentration of attention toward the poles of conflict.
She understands that the public doesn't act solely on product. They act on:
         * Story.
         * Intensity.
         * Drama.
         * Stake.
Suzune understands that:
         * If 12 classes compete on equal footing, → the market fragments.
         * If 2 classes dominate the narrative, → the market concentrates.
Ryuuen's 5 million point bet isn't just a bet. but rather a symbol that generates in the public:
         * Emotional gravity.
         * Urgency.
         * Perceived magnitude.
         * Suzune accepts:
         * Deceiving her own class.
         * Simulating betrayal.
         * Controlling leaks.
         * Maintaining layers of truth and lies.
By allowing the “betrayal” to become public before the event:
         * They capture the narrative first.
         * They force third parties to react.
         * They discourage late entry.
         * They create anticipation.

         * Written Comprehensive Cooperative Exam (Y2V9):
The first level of abstract thinking emerges when Suzune reinterprets the exam. At first glance, the exam appears to be:
         * an academic competition.
But Suzune understands that it is actually:
         * A system that focuses not on answering questions, but on maximizing point production.
This involves abstracting the exam as:
         * Scoring system + Ability distribution + Time constraints
Due to the exam rules, Suzune has to transform each student into a strategic variable. Conceptual example:
         * Academic A = low score per answer
         * Academic D/E = high score per answer
This means that the least capable students become the most valuable assets. Suzune also abstracts the main rules that constrain the group during the exam. The actual system is:
         * students enter
         * students exit
         * they cannot speak.
But she conceptualizes the exam as an information flow. Abstract model:
         * Observer -> Information -> Transmission -> Resolution
Hand signals function as a data channel within the system. This transforms the exam into a potential distributed communication system that can be used to assign abstract roles:
         * Information Explorers
         * Scoring Executors
         * Risk Stabilizer (Kouenji)
This reflects abstract thinking applied to the strategic organization of groups.

         * Survival and Elimination Special Exam (Y2V10):
Suzune understands that, although the system appears competitive, there is a theoretical scenario where all four classes could tie by cooperating, and she deduces why the rules prevent this. This isn't a superficial reading of the rules. It's system abstraction combined with game theory:
         * She detects equilibrium conditions.
         * She identifies constraints designed to disrupt that equilibrium.
         * She's operating on the system's metastructure, not on direct actions.
Suzune predicts that:
         * Lower classes (Ryūen, Ichinose) have a structural incentive to form an alliance.
         * The feasibility depends on the timing (beginning vs. second half).
She doesn't observe actions, but rather models future incentives, integrating:
         * Rules
         * Scoring
         * Competitive psychology
Generating an abstract model of collective decision-making under uncertainty. Continuing with the exam, she observes and develops a probabilistic theorization of its design. She deduces that:
         * Level 1 ≈ ~50% success rate
         * Level 2 ≈ ~20%
         * Level 3 ≈ ~10%
No complete explicit data → infers a system pattern, constructs an implicit difficulty vs. performance function. This is statistical abstract thinking. For the exam, she creates a conceptual category:
         * “Reachers” = students with 2 errors
         * Manipulates the system so that Ichinose is forced to protect them
Why it's abstract:
         * It doesn't exist in the rules → it's an abstraction created by Suzune
         * Turns individual states into strategic variables
         * Cognitively redefines the playing field
